## Implementation plan
# Plan: build out the remaining interactive models

Branch: `feat/model-buildout` (off `origin/main` @ 58fc6ad). Date: 2026-10-01.

## Request

Founder, verbatim: "use the style, rules and methods for what weve devolped so far and
build all the models in depth for the topics and subjects that are still left unbuilt".

## Where things stand

- 8 spec types are built: `function-grapher` (2D plot, flagged `built:false`, no tests, not
  reachable from any page) and 7 physics models: `projectile`, `wave-oscillator`,
  `circuit-diagram`, `free-body-diagram`, `circular-motion`, `orbit-sim`, `ray-diagram`.
  Of those 7, `orbit-sim`, `ray-diagram` and the circuit solver have tests; `projectile`,
  `wave-oscillator`, `free-body-diagram` and `circular-motion` have none.
- 7 spec types in `src/data/curriculum/specTypes.ts` are planned but unbuilt: `surface-3d`,
  `vector-field`, `unit-circle`, `linear-transform`, `molecule-3d`, `reaction-energy`,
  `step-walkthrough`.
- Curriculum: 734 concepts in 23 courses, 230 of them marked high 3D-fit. Mapped to a
  model: 4 (all physics). Math 438, chemistry 76, biology 71, computing 54 have zero.
- The mapping has already drifted: `raw/physics.json` sets `specType` on 1 concept while the
  generated `physics.json` carries 4, so re-running the curriculum build today would drop 3.
  The build script is run by hand and is not in `package.json`.
- The browse spine is the topics page (`#/topics`). It reads the concept list from the
  Supabase `concepts` table and shows "Try →" on a chip when the row has `spec_type`. That
  column is set on 1 row. Only the founder can re-seed it.
- The Try page (`#/try`) has no text input. The spec endpoint (`api/spec.ts`) has one
  caller, behind a build flag that is off by default. The classifier is not on any path a
  student can reach today.
- `docs/DESIGN.md` (approved): AI emits a validated JSON spec, a deterministic renderer
  draws it, a deterministic numeric check is the binding correctness gate, unknown input
  gets a named fallback and never a guessed visual. It names "~12 spec types" as the
  near-term build target and says the project is pre-product with no demand evidence.

## Goal

Every course that contains a concept a model can faithfully show has in-depth interactive
models for those concepts, each built to the same bar as the existing physics models, and
every such concept is reachable from the topics page and walkable in unit order.

One gate and one report:

- **Gate** (the curriculum build, `bun run gen:curriculum`, fails otherwise): every course
  has at least one mapped concept or an entry in an explicit exclusion list. Wave 0 seeds
  that list with one "pending Wave N" entry per course not yet covered (table below); each
  wave deletes the entries it satisfies; after Wave 6 the list may contain only permanent
  exclusions, each with a reason. The gate also fails on an entry naming a course that
  does not exist and, in check mode, on an entry for a course that is already mapped (in
  a model branch that is a warning, because the branch does not edit the shared list).
  In check mode it also fails when every model planned for Wave N is built and a
  "pending Wave N" entry is still there, so a pending entry cannot outlive its wave.
- **Report** (printed by the build, never a gate): per-course coverage, and every high
  3D-fit concept that is still unmapped.

Expected coverage after all waves is roughly 250 to 300 of 734 concepts: the families
named in the catalog below. The rest are symbolic-manipulation, proof and vocabulary
concepts no model shows faithfully. The number is a report, not a target to hit, so that
nothing pushes toward mapping a concept a model does not really show.

Courses and the wave that first gives each one a mapped concept:

| Wave | Courses first covered |
|---|---|
| already mapped | AP Physics 1, AP Physics 2, High school physics, Physics library |
| 0 | Algebra 1 |
| 1 | Algebra 2, Precalculus, Trigonometry, Linear algebra, Multivariable calculus, AP Chemistry, High school chemistry, High school biology, Algorithms |
| 2 | Organic chemistry |
| 3 | AP Biology |
| 4 | AP Calculus AB, AP Calculus BC, Differential equations, Geometry |
| 6 | AP Statistics, AP Computer Science Principles |
| never | Computer programming (permanent exclusion) |

## The recipe (definition of done for one model)

The recipe the 7 physics models established, written down. A model is done when it has:

1. **Spec**: a TypeScript interface and named presets (at least a default). A spec is a flat
   record of numbers, strings, enums, arrays of those, and arrays of flat records one level
   deep (a list of charges, data points or graph edges), so a JSON schema can be generated
   from it later without hand-authoring. Every preset carries a one-sentence "try this"
   prompt in the spec's `notes` field, in the form "Change X and watch Y: you should see
   Z". Every preset is either the model's default or mapped to at least one concept, so no
   preset is reachable only by typing a URL.
2. **Deterministic core**: the closed-form (or otherwise deterministic) maths in one place,
   used by BOTH the validation gate and the renderer, so what is drawn is what was checked.
   `core.derive(spec)` returns every number the stage draws or a readout shows; the
   widget draws those and does not recompute them. `core.ranges` gives each numeric
   control's range. A comparison of a numeric result against a symbolic one is a test on
   fixed test functions; at run time `validate` checks ranges and finiteness only.
   `validate(spec)` refuses non-physical or out-of-range input with a plain reason and
   the name of the field that caused it, which is how the reason lands beside the right
   control.
   Controls clamp to the valid range, so a student cannot reach a refused state by
   dragging. A boundary that means something (total internal reflection, a triangle with
   no solution, a singular matrix) is drawn and labelled, not refused. Only nonsense is
   refused, and the reason appears next to the control that caused it.
3. **Equation set** where the catalog's "Eq" column says yes: LaTeX/KaTeX display, residual
   form, solve-for-any-variable through `useEquationPanel`, canonical-SI units with the
   unit dropdown from `units.ts`. LaTeX strings are constants in code and are never built
   from anything a student typed.
4. **Word-problem parser** where the catalog's "WP" column says yes: a regex parser that
   fills the knowns and selects the asked-for unknown, offline. Where a model has a parser
   but no equation set, the parser only fills the widget's inputs. A parse has three
   outcomes, each with its own line under the input: filled ("Set v = 12 m/s, angle =
   30 degrees"), partial ("Set v = 12 m/s. Couldn't find the angle."), and not understood
   ("Couldn't find the numbers", with an example to try).
5. **Widget** on one of three stages, all in the same palette:
   - `3D`: `Scene3D` in the clean-3D look (matte pearl/chrome, ONE glass-accent focal
     object, azure / warm-azure vectors from `PALETTE`), with a 2D SVG fallback from the
     same maths when WebGL is missing. `frameloop="demand"` unless animating. No `Label3D`
     inside a demand-frameloop scene; labels there are HTML laid over the canvas or a
     legend beside it (the existing `ps__legend` pattern).
   - `plot`: the shared `PlotStage` (axes, curves, probe point, shaded regions) for
     graph-shaped models.
   - `board`: the shared `BoardStage` for discrete models, in one of two layouts named in
     `meta.ts`: `canvas` (geometric figures) or `table` (grids and step lists).
   A model whose lesson is two linked pictures (a cart and its graphs, a gas and its PV
   diagram) declares a second, `aux` stage in `meta.ts`; `ModelFrame` places it, the model
   does not. Every stage has an overlay slot for free SVG, so a model can draw what a kit
   lacks without editing the kit.
   Every stage sits in the shared `ModelFrame` and takes its controls from the `controls`
   kit (below); a model's own CSS styles only the inside of its stage and uses only the
   existing colour and font variables, never literal colours. Under reduced motion, time
   becomes a manual scrub and nothing autoplays. Nothing jitters on hover. Every control
   is reachable by keyboard, including anything dragged on the stage (the shared
   `DragHandle` gives arrow-key nudging, a mirrored number field and a 44px touch target). Controls are at least 44px tall at 375px. Readouts
   sit in a polite live region, and the stage carries a one-sentence text description of
   its current state.
6. **One primary interaction per model.** Variations are presets of that interaction. A
   second, different interaction is a second model.
7. **Registration**: the model's folder holds the four required files (`meta.ts`,
   `core.ts`, `presets.ts`, `Widget.tsx`), each with the default export the contract in
   `src/engine/models/types.ts` names; the registry is generated from the folder listing.
   Concepts are mapped in the model's own `concepts.ts`. The folder starts from
   `bun run new:model`, never from a blank file.
8. **Tests**: a standalone `bun` assertion script in the model's folder (same style as
   `src/engine/physicsModels.test.ts`, no node or bun APIs, so the app typecheck stays
   clean), written with the shared `check` and `near` helpers so a failure prints the
   expected and the actual value, covering the closed forms against hand-computed values, the gate's refusals, the
   equation set's consistency with the core, the parser, and that every preset validates.
   The test runner picks it up by filename. The registry's range-sampling test (below)
   covers the corners of every control without the builder writing it.
9. **Verification**: `bun run build`, `bun run lint`, `bun run sweep <type>` (below) at
   375px and 1280px with and without WebGL, and a hands-on browser pass of the new model:
   controls drive the picture, readouts match a hand calculation, and the concept a link
   names is visible when the model opens. A second agent that did not build the model
   repeats the hands-on pass before the model is merged, following `docs/MODEL-REVIEW.md`
   and returning its pass/fail report (on a pass the second agent, not the builder,
   removes the model's `stub` mark), and checks the model against the design checklist: one glass object, palette and kit controls only, labels legible,
   nothing clipped at 375px, keyboard reaches everything, reduced motion still teaches.
   Screenshots of each new model (375px and 1280px, 3D and fallback), saved by the sweep,
   go to the founder with the PR.
10. **Reference data**: any table of facts the maths cannot derive (bond angles, standard
    potentials, the codon table, torsional barriers, organelle sets, pathway and mechanism
    steps) lives in the model's `data.ts`, with the source cited beside each table as an
    address that can be opened. The
    second agent checks each table against its cited source. Where sources disagree (ATP
    yields, for one), the model shows the value with the convention it follows.

## Wave 0: foundation and browse

Today a new model edits six shared files (`spec.ts`, `validate.ts`, `equations.ts`,
`wordProblems.ts`, `VisualizePanel.tsx`, `specTypes.ts`), `VisualizePanel` imports every
widget eagerly, and the `test` script is a hand-written `&&` chain. That does not survive
going from 8 models to 73. Wave 0 fixes the shape once, so a new model touches only its
own folder, and makes the topics page a working course browser.

### Wave 0 in four PRs

Wave 0 moves and reformats code that has no tests today, so it lands as four stacked PRs,
each one version, each through `/review` then `/ship`:

| PR | Version | What it carries | Behaviour change |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0a | 0.3.4.0 | the test runner and `testkit.ts`; CI; one lockfile and a pinned Bun; tests that pin today's behaviour of the five untested models (closed forms, and each default preset's readout values as `fmt` formats them, with no rendering) written against the current code; the topics column fix already on this branch; the spec endpoint switched off (below) | none, except the endpoint |
| 0b | 0.3.5.0 | the model folders, the contract, the generator and Vite plugin, the registry, the catalog and the single mapping source, `validateMappings` and the gate, `planned.ts`; `safeExpression`; the ESLint model rules, `check-models` and `check:branch`; `projectile` and `function-grapher` moved into their folders; the `function-grapher` Algebra 1 presets and mappings, as data | none: the Try and topics pages look and behave as before |
| 0c | 0.3.6.0 | `ModelFrame` and its slots; the `controls` kit, `PlotStage`, `Transport`, `DragHandle`, `ExpressionInput`, the quantity reader and the KaTeX component; the palette additions; the single WebGL check; `fmt`; in-place navigation with a route error boundary; deep links; the topics page rebuilt on the catalog; `projectile` and `function-grapher` converted to the frame's contract; the six legacy widgets wrapped in the slots | the new frame and browser; first real mappings |
| 0d | 0.3.7.0 | usage logging and its SQL; the sweep; the browser specs, including the walk through the Algebra 1 mappings and the rendered readout text; the scaffold with its plot and 3D templates; the worktree setup; the three docs | logging |

The two reference models, PR by PR:

| PR | `projectile` | `function-grapher` |
|---|---|---|
| 0b | its maths, equations, parser and widget move into its folder; the widget still holds its own state; nothing a student sees changes | its drawing code moves into its folder; its expression check is rebuilt on `safeExpression`; the Algebra 1 presets and mappings are added as data |
| 0c | converted to the frame-owned spec and the slots | its drawing code becomes `PlotStage`, which it then uses; converted to the frame-owned spec and the slots; `built: true` |
| 0d | covered by the sweep and the browser specs | the browser walk through its Algebra 1 concepts |

The 0a tests keep the same assertions through 0b; only their import paths and file
locations change. The one deliberate difference in 0b is `function-grapher`'s expression
check: every pinned preset must still validate and give the same values, and the PR lists
any expression the old check accepted that the new one refuses. The `fmt` change in 0c
updates the pinned readout strings in the same PR, so the diff shows every readout that
moved.

The estimates are replaced with actuals after 0b, and again after Wave 1. Waves 1 to 6
take versions from 0.3.8.0.

The spec endpoint: `api/spec.ts` is live on the deployed site with no sign-in and no
rate limit, and any caller can make it run a large model on the project's key. Nothing a
student can reach calls it. 0a makes it answer "not found" unless a server setting
(`SPEC_API_ENABLED`) is on, and makes its error replies generic. A rate limit is a
condition of ever switching it on (see "Not in scope").

### Engine shape

- **One folder per model**: `src/engine/models/<type>/`. Four files are required and make
  the model exist; the others are added as the recipe requires. Every file has one default
  export, checked with `satisfies` against the contract below.

  | File | Default export | Holds |
  |---|---|---|
  | `meta.ts` (required) | `ModelMeta` | pure data: `type`, `label`, `subjects` (one or more; descriptive only, it does not restrict which concepts the model may serve), `category`, `stage`, the optional `board` layout and `aux` stage, `description`, `defaultPreset` |
  | `core.ts` (required) | `ModelCore<Spec, Derived>` | `ranges`, `validate` and `derive`; pure TypeScript, no React or three |
  | `presets.ts` (required) | `Preset<Spec>[]` | each preset's `id`, `label` and `spec`; pure TypeScript |
  | `Widget.tsx` (required) | a component taking `WidgetProps<Spec>` | the stage and its controls, inside the frame's slots; `widget.css` beside it, class names prefixed with the model type |
  | `concepts.ts` | `ConceptMap` | concept id to preset id, with an optional "try this" note of its own when the preset's note does not name that concept; and the concepts the builder declined, each with a reason |
  | `equations.ts` | `ModelEquations<Spec>` | present exactly when the catalog says Eq: the `EquationSet`, and which spec field each of its base symbols is |
  | `parse.ts` | a function returning `ParseResult<Spec>` | present exactly when the catalog says WP |
  | `data.ts` | named tables | reference data, each table with its source |
  | `<type>.test.ts` | none | the model's assertions |

  Shared classes stay in `src/engine/visualize.css`, which new models do not edit.
- **The contract** is `src/engine/models/types.ts`, written in 0b. It is the one file a
  builder reads to know every shape:

  ```ts
  export interface ModelMeta {
    type: string                 // equals the folder name
    label: string
    subjects: Subject[]
    category: string
    stage: 'plot' | '3D' | 'board'
    board?: 'canvas' | 'table'   // board stages only
    aux?: 'plot' | 'board'       // optional second stage
    description: string
    defaultPreset: string
    stub?: true                  // set by the scaffold; the reviewer removes it on a pass
    legacy?: true                // the six adapter models only
  }
  export interface Preset<S> { id: string; label: string; spec: S }
  export interface ValidationResult<S> { valid: boolean; reason?: string; field?: keyof S }
  export interface Range { min: number; max: number; step: number }
  export interface ModelCore<S, D> {
    ranges: { [K in keyof S]?: Range }   // each numeric control's range
    validate(spec: S): ValidationResult<S>
    derive(spec: S): D                   // every number the stage or a readout shows
  }
  export interface WidgetProps<S> {
    spec: S                              // the live spec, owned by the frame
    set(patch: Partial<S>): void         // the only way a widget changes it
  }
  export interface ModelEquations<S> { set: EquationSet; fields: Record<string, keyof S> }
  export interface ConceptMap {
    mapped: Record<string, { preset: string; note?: string }>
    declined?: Record<string, string>   // concept id -> why this model does not show it
  }
  export interface ParseResult<S> {
    outcome: 'filled' | 'partial' | 'none'
    patch: Partial<S>
    message: string
  }
  ```

- **Who owns what** between a widget and `ModelFrame`:
  - The frame owns the live spec. It starts from the preset and changes only through
    `set`. On every change the frame runs `core.validate`; a refusal's `field` is shown
    by the control bound to that field. A control takes its range from `core.ranges`.
  - A widget keeps only state that is not part of the model: the camera, hover, which
    item is selected.
  - A widget returns its content inside the frame's slots, `Frame.Stage`, `Frame.Aux`
    (when `meta.aux` is set), `Frame.Controls` and `Frame.Readouts`. The slots are plain
    wrappers that the frame's grid places at both widths; a widget never positions them.
  - The frame renders the equation panel and the word-problem bar itself. It feeds
    `useEquationPanel` from the live spec through `equations.ts`'s `fields`, puts the
    solved value into the spec the widget receives, and locks the control bound to that
    field. A parser's `patch` is applied with `set`. So anything an equation can solve
    for is a spec field, including the time of a model with `Transport`.
  - The widget is keyed on model type and preset, so a hop to another preset of the same
    model starts clean, and reset restores the preset the same way.
  - The first input event inside the frame is the "interacted" usage event.
  - The six legacy models (`legacy: true`) are the exception. In 0c their existing markup
    is wrapped in the same slots, a mechanical edit, with their own equation panel and
    word-problem bar placed in the frame's regions, so they sit in the same layout. They
    keep their own state, take `{ spec }` only, and are not required to have `ranges` or
    `derive`. `projectile` and `function-grapher` are converted to the full contract in
    0b and 0c, which proves it on the hardest existing widget (equations, a parser,
    transport, 3D, a locked control) before any new model is built.
- **Scaffold**: `bun run new:model <type> [--stage plot|3D|board] [--eq] [--wp]
  [--concepts <id,id,...>]` (`scripts/new-model.ts`) writes the folder with every
  required file in place: a one-slider spec with its range, a default preset, a
  `validate` and a `derive`, a widget on the chosen stage built from the kits and the
  slots, a `concepts.ts` with the given concept ids mapped to the default preset, and a
  test that passes. It takes the stage, Eq and WP from the `PLANNED` list when the type
  is there, so those flags are only needed for a model that is not in the catalog. It
  refuses a type that is not kebab-case or whose folder exists. It marks the model
  `stub: true`, and ends by printing the address to open (`#/try/<type>`) and the
  commands a builder runs next. A stub works in development and is left out of every
  production build's registry and catalog; the check-mode build refuses to pass while
  any model is still a stub, so a scaffold cannot ship as a model. The scaffold ships in
  0d with plot and 3D templates. The board template arrives with `BoardStage` in Wave 1;
  until then `--stage board` is refused with a line saying so.
- **Reference models**: two folders are complete, readable examples of the contract, and
  the scaffold's stubs are cut from them. `function-grapher` is the plot example; its
  expression check is rebuilt on `safeExpression` and the older, looser check in
  `validate.ts` is deleted. `projectile` is the 3D example with an equation set and a
  word-problem parser: its maths, equations, parser and widget move out of the shared
  files into its folder (its schema branch and prompt text stay in `src/engine/spec.ts`).
- **Generated files**: `bun run gen:curriculum` runs `scripts/gen.ts` (TypeScript, run
  with Bun): first the model registry, then the curriculum build. Nothing derived from
  the model folders is committed, so model branches never conflict on it and it cannot
  be stale:
  - `src/engine/models/meta.gen.ts`: imports every `meta.ts` and records which optional
    files each folder has. Small, loaded eagerly by the Try page, the coverage page and
    `specTypes.ts`.
  - `src/engine/models/load.gen.ts`: one lazy loader per model, which brings in that
    model's `core.ts`, `presets.ts`, `Widget.tsx` and, when present, `equations.ts` and
    `parse.ts` together, so a model has one loading state.
  - `src/engine/models/pure.gen.ts`: imports every model's `meta.ts`, `core.ts`,
    `presets.ts` and `concepts.ts`. Used by the curriculum build and the tests only; the
    browser never loads it.
  - `src/data/curriculum/catalog.gen.json`: see "Catalog and mapping".

  A folder is registered only when all four required files exist, matched by exact name
  from the directory listing (so `widget.tsx` fails the same way on macOS as on Linux). A
  folder holding only a `concepts.ts` is skipped, so a half-built sibling never breaks
  another model's branch. Every run prints one line per folder: registered, or skipped
  with the file that is missing. Each output is written to a temporary file and renamed
  into place, and only when its content has changed, so two runs at once are harmless
  and a save does not make the dev server reload for nothing.
- **When the generator runs**: the Vite plugin (`scripts/vite-plugin-models.ts`) runs it
  when the dev server or a build starts, so starting Vite any way at all is enough, and
  again when a file is added to or removed from a model folder or a `meta.ts`,
  `presets.ts` or `concepts.ts` changes. The plugin does not watch the generator's own
  outputs. A failed run in the dev server shows its message in the terminal and the
  browser overlay and keeps the last good output. Outside Vite it runs on `postinstall`
  and before `test`, `lint` and `typecheck` (a new script: the generator, then
  `tsc -b`). A production deploy runs it in check mode.
- **`specTypes.ts` is derived**: built entries come from `meta.gen.ts`. Catalog ids that
  are not built yet come from one static `PLANNED` list
  (`src/data/curriculum/planned.ts`), written once in 0b from this plan's tables with
  each model's `type`, `label`, `subjects`, `stage`, `eq`, `wp` and `wave`. What the app
  shows is built, plus planned and not yet built, so finishing a model needs no edit to
  the list. Where a wave's table has no Subject column the subject is the wave's own
  (chemistry, biology or physics); `ideal-gas` and `nuclear-decay` are chemistry and
  physics. A model's label is its type in words (`ideal-gas` is "Ideal gas"). Board
  layouts are listed in "Stage details".
- **Tests**: `bun run test` runs `scripts/run-tests.ts`, which executes every
  `src/**/*.test.ts` and `scripts/**/*.test.ts` as a standalone script, prints one line
  per file and a summary of every failure, and exits non-zero if any failed. A file
  that makes no assertion, runs past 30 seconds or leaves a rejected promise fails.
  `bun run test <text>` runs only the files whose path contains the text. Assertions use
  `src/engine/testkit.ts` (`check(name, condition)` and
  `near(name, actual, expected, tolerance)`). `bun test`, Bun's own runner, is the wrong
  command for these files: a `bunfig.toml` preload makes it print "Use `bun run test`"
  and exit. `bun run test:catalog` is the curriculum build in check mode (see "Catalog
  and mapping"); CI and the integrator run it, a model branch does not need to.
  `scripts/`, the curriculum build and `api/` get their own TypeScript project
  (`tsconfig.scripts.json`), so `bun run typecheck` covers them; today nothing does.
- **Existing 8 models get a folder**. `function-grapher` and `projectile` are moved in
  whole (above). The other six get `meta.ts`, a `presets.ts` (their example specs move
  here from `VisualizePanel.tsx`), a `core.ts` that re-exports `validate` from
  `validate.ts`, `concepts.ts`, a `Widget.tsx` that re-exports the current component, and
  a test file. Their maths, equations, parsers, widgets, schema branches and prompt text
  stay in the files they are in now, and `physicsModels.test.ts` and
  `circuitNetwork.test.ts` stay where they are. `projectile`, `wave-oscillator`,
  `free-body-diagram`, `circular-motion` and `function-grapher` get the closed-form
  assertions they never had, written in 0a against the code as it is today and moved
  into their folder's test file in 0b; that file is also where each model's Wave 1
  extension assertions go. A test keeps each legacy schema branch in `spec.ts` in step
  with its model's default preset (same keys), and asserts that what `api/spec.ts`
  imports stays free of React and CSS.
- **`VisualizePanel`** drops its import list, `MODEL_TYPES`, `validate` switch and
  `renderModel` switch, and looks models up in the generated registry. Its list of
  supported models in the "No interactive visual yet" message comes from the registry.
  Its layout and states are the "Try page frame" section below.
- **Kits** live in `src/engine/kits/` and are built by the integrator before the wave that
  first needs them. Each kit is imported from its own file; there is no single file that
  re-exports them all, so a model's chunk carries only the kits it uses. 0c builds
  `ModelFrame` and the `controls` kit (see "Try page frame"), `Transport` (play, pause,
  scrub and speed, extracted from the existing `ps__transport` controls, shared by every
  time-based model), `DragHandle` (the one way to drag something on a stage: pointer
  capture, `touch-action` on the handle only, a 44px target, arrow-key nudging and a
  mirrored number field) and `ExpressionInput` (the only typed-maths field: debounced,
  keeps the last valid picture on screen, and names the offending token under the
  field; a preset function with sliders is always the first path and typing the
  second). `BoardStage` is built in Wave 1 with cell, token,
  arrow and label primitives, a 12px minimum text size, the same overlay slot as
  `PlotStage`, and one overflow rule: a table wider than the board scrolls inside it with
  its first column pinned. `PlotStage` is
  built in 0c (extracted from `function-grapher`'s widget; it exposes its x and y
  scales, a pointer-to-data mapping and an overlay slot for children, so a model can draw
  arrows, segments, rays and draggable points on it without editing the kit), `Stepper`
  in Wave 1 (a pure step reducer on top of `Transport`, with a caption line for the
  current step, a "step 7 of 23" counter and replay: play stops at the last step,
  scrubbing pauses, and under reduced motion there is no autoplay, only step buttons),
  `ParticleBox` in Wave 2 (a pure seeded simulation core, a flat renderer that draws to
  a 2D canvas, and a three renderer, each its own file, so a board model never loads
  three; at most 400 particles; under reduced motion it shows a still snapshot with a
  step button).
  Each kit's pure part (plot scales, step reducer, particle core) has its own unit test.
  Before a wave's fan-out, each kit that wave first uses is built against its hardest
  consumers, as working fixtures on `#/kits`: `PlotStage` against `kinematics-1d` (linked
  panels), `vector-field` (an arrow field at equal aspect), `integral-explorer` (regions
  between curves) and `regression` (draggable points); `BoardStage` against
  `triangle-solver` (a geometric figure), `stoichiometry` (a table), `graph-explorer` (a
  node-and-edge drawing) and `reaction-mechanism` (curved arrows); `ParticleBox` against
  `ideal-gas`, `states-of-matter` and `membrane-transport` (two compartments and a
  membrane that passes some particles).
  `safeExpression` also lives here from 0b: the one path by which any model turns
  typed text into a function. It uses its own mathjs instance, parses without
  evaluating raw strings, caps input at 200 characters, allows only number, operator,
  parenthesis and listed-function nodes, refuses any symbol that is not a declared
  variable or `pi` or `e`, and treats a result that is not a plain finite number as
  "undefined here". It has three profiles: one real variable, two real variables, and
  boolean (for `logic-gates`). It returns either a function of the named variables or a
  failure carrying a message, the offending token and its position, which
  `ExpressionInput` shows under the field. Every model that accepts a typed expression
  uses it.
  A shared quantity reader (`quantities.ts`) finds each number with its unit and
  dimension in a word problem, using `units.ts`; a model's `parse.ts` only says which
  quantity is which. Input is cut at 500 characters, and a test feeds each parser
  hostile input under a time limit.
  All KaTeX goes through one kit component with `trust` off.
  Unit families a wave needs and `src/engine/units.ts` lacks (pressure, amount,
  concentration, charge, field, electron-volts and so on) are added by the integrator at
  the same point. Temperature is stored in kelvin and the unit table stays single-factor;
  `units.ts` gains one temperature helper (`toKelvin` and `fromKelvin` for degrees Celsius
  and Fahrenheit) that every parser and the slider row's unit switch use, so no model
  converts temperature by hand. Readouts show kelvin where the equation needs absolute
  temperature (gas laws, Arrhenius) and degrees Celsius elsewhere, with the other on the
  unit switch. Concentration is stored in moles per litre, the second stated exception
  to SI, because every equilibrium constant, pH and cell potential is defined on it; the
  gas constant is a named constant in each of its two unit forms.
- **Using and extending the kits**: every kit's props carry doc comments, and a
  development-only page, `#/kits`, shows each kit rendered beside the code that produced
  it. When a kit cannot do what a model needs, the builder draws it in the stage's
  overlay slot and leaves a `KIT-REQUEST.md` in the model's folder saying what was missing
  and what they did instead. The integrator answers every request between sub-PRs, by
  extending the kit or by accepting the local drawing, and deletes the file; the
  check-mode build fails while one remains.
- **WebGL**: `hasWebGL` is asked once per page and its test context is released; the
  landing page's own copy of the check is replaced by it. A
  context lost because a model was unmounted is ignored. A real loss waits for one
  restore, and falls back to the 2D view only if that does not come.
- **Sweep**: `#/sweep/<type>`, present in development builds and in builds made with
  `VITE_SWEEP=1`, mounts one model's presets in turn inside an error boundary and
  records, per preset, whether it mounted without error, which renderer drew it (three
  or the 2D fallback), whether that renderer drew anything (at least one render call,
  or at least one shape with a non-zero box), and whether the page text contains `NaN`,
  `undefined`, `Infinity` or `[object Object]`. The result is on the page as a table
  and on `window.__sweep` for the script.
  `bun run sweep [type] [--width 375|1280] [--nowebgl]` (`scripts/sweep.ts`, using
  Playwright, a new development dependency) loads one model per page, so the browser's
  limit on live 3D contexts is never reached, prints the table, saves a screenshot per
  preset to `sweep-shots/` (not committed) and exits non-zero on any failure, including
  a 3D model that drew its fallback when 3D was expected. `--nowebgl` turns WebGL off in
  the browser itself, so it exercises the app's real detection. With no flags it runs
  all four combinations of width and WebGL. Locally it uses the dev server, which it
  starts if needed and checks is this worktree's own; in CI it runs against the built
  site (`vite build`, then `vite preview`), so a failure that only a production build
  has is caught. Route options always sit inside the hash, after `?`
  (`#/sweep/projectile?w=375`); `nowebgl=1` on `#/try/...` lets a builder look at one
  model's fallback by hand.

Tests for this section: `src/engine/models/registry.test.ts` asserts every `meta.ts` type
is unique and matches its folder name; the default preset exists; every preset passes its
own `validate`; every preset is the default or mapped to a concept; for every model on
the `PLANNED` list, that its stage matches and that `equations.ts` and `parse.ts` are
present exactly when the list says Eq and WP; and, for every model that is not legacy,
range sampling: with each preset as the base, every control at each end of its range and
at seeded random points in between must pass `validate`, and every number `derive`
returns must be finite or an explicit "undefined here". Model rules are checked two ways.
ESLint, as errors and visible in the editor: a model's pure files (`core`, `presets`,
`meta`, `concepts`, `equations`, `parse`, `data`) import no React, three or CSS; no model
imports another model's folder, `supabase`, `usage` or `Label3D`; no model uses `fetch`,
`localStorage`, `location` or `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`; no model contains a raw `input`
element. `scripts/check-models.test.ts` fails on a literal colour in a model's CSS or
TSX (`none`, `transparent`, `currentColor` and `inherit` are allowed; a line is exempted
by an `allow-raw: <reason>` comment above it, and the check prints how many exemptions
exist). `bun run check:branch <type>` fails if a model branch changed any file outside
its own folder. `scripts/gen.test.ts` covers the four-file rule, the exact-name match
and write-only-on-change. `scripts/new-model.test.ts` scaffolds one model for each stage the
scaffold has a template for into a temporary folder and asserts that the generator
registers it and that its test passes. `bun run check:bundle`, run in CI after the build, reads the build's manifest
and fails if a plot or board model's chunks include three, if a model without a typed
expression includes mathjs, or if the Try page's first load is over the size recorded
in 0c plus a tenth.

### Try page frame

One frame for every model, built once as `ModelFrame` and extracted from the layout the
physics models already share, so old and new models read as one product.

```
1280px (page does not scroll)                    375px (page scrolls, stage stays put)
┌──────────────────────────────────────────┐    ┌────────────────────────┐
│ Saras   AP Physics 1 · Fluids    all topics│    │ Saras       all topics │
│ Buoyancy and Archimedes' principle        │    │ AP Physics 1 · Fluids  │
│ Change the density and watch the block... │    │ Buoyancy and Archim... │
│                     ← prev   2 of 5  next →│    │ ← prev  2 of 5  next → │
├───────────────────────────┬──────────────┤    ├────────────────────────┤
│ word problem (when WP yes)                │    │ stage, pinned to the   │
├───────────────────────────┬──────────────┤    │ top, 45% of the screen │
│                           │ controls     │    ├────────────────────────┤
│   stage                   │ readouts     │    │ try-this note          │
│   (3D, plot or board)     │ equations    │    │ controls               │
│                           │ (when Eq yes)│    │ readouts               │
│                           │   reset      │    │ equations, word problem│
└───────────────────────────┴──────────────┘    └────────────────────────┘
```

- Header, in reading order: the course and unit as a small label; the concept title as
  the page's one heading (the model label when no concept is given); the "try this" note,
  at most two lines; previous and next concept links with "2 of 5". The Saras mark links
  home and is no longer the heading. The tab title is "<concept title> · Saras".
- The header has a fixed height at 1280px, so adding the note never makes the page
  scroll. At 375px the stage is pinned to the top of the screen at 45% of its height and
  the controls scroll beneath it, so a student always sees the picture they are changing.
  The 45% is of the small viewport height (the height with the browser's bars showing),
  with a floor; when the visible height is under about 480px (a phone on its side, or
  the keyboard up) the stage is not pinned and scrolls with the page.
- A model with an `aux` stage shows both. At 1280px the stage column is split, the main
  stage above and the aux stage below it at a third of the column's height. At 375px the
  pinned area holds one of them, chosen by a two-button switch on the pinned stage
  (labelled by the model, "Model" and "Graph" by default); the other sits first in the
  scrolling column.
- Previous and next follow catalog order within the unit and skip unmapped concepts,
  labelled with the concept they lead to. At the last mapped concept of a unit, next
  becomes "Next unit: <its first mapped concept>", or "Back to <course>" when there is
  none.
- "All topics" returns to the topics page with the same subject, search and toggle, and
  the course the student came from open and in view (carried in the link, see Topics).
- The `controls` kit is the only source of controls: a slider row (label, value, unit),
  a choice (segmented buttons, or a list when there are more than five options), a number
  field with a unit, a readout row, a legend, and a reset button that restores the
  preset. A model that needs to switch between a few built-in cases (a molecule, a
  lattice, a mechanism) uses the choice control, never its own picker. There is no
  model picker and no preset picker: other models and presets are reached through
  topics and the previous and next links.
- One number format everywhere, `fmt(value, options)` (the existing `fmt`, extended): by
  default three significant figures, and never fewer digits than the whole-number part
  has (3 is "3.00", 12345.6 is "12346"), so a readout does not change shape as a slider
  crosses a whole number; scientific notation below 0.001 and from 100,000 up; the unit
  after a thin space; an en dash for a value that does not exist. Readouts use
  fixed-width numerals. The three private copies of `fmt` (in `EquationPanel.tsx`,
  `ProjectileSim.tsx` and `FunctionGrapher.tsx`) are deleted in favour of this one. Options cover what three figures cannot:
  `sig` for a value whose point is its precision (109.47 degrees, 68.27 percent),
  `kind: 'int'` for counts and `kind: 'percent'`. A readout row also accepts an exact
  string the model formats itself (a surd, a ratio such as 9:3:3:1, a binary or
  hexadecimal number). The eight existing models move from two decimals to this format;
  the Wave 0 browser pass reads each of their readouts against a hand calculation.
- On a touch screen a 3D stage does not capture one-finger drags (the page scrolls);
  a "Rotate" button on the stage turns orbiting on and off.
- A 3D model that falls back to 2D says so in one line under the stage ("Showing the 2D
  view"). If WebGL is lost mid-session the model switches to its fallback.
- States of the stage:

| State | What the student sees |
|---|---|
| loading | a skeleton with the stage's own outline and fixed size (no layout shift; no shimmer under reduced motion) |
| ready | the model |
| chunk failed | "This model didn't load." and a "Reload" button that reloads the page |
| refused by the gate | "Couldn't verify a correct visual for this" and the reason |
| crashed | "This model hit an error." with "Reset to the starting values" and a link to topics |
| no such model | "No model called X" (X shortened to 40 characters) and a link to topics |

- A model's readouts are announced after the student stops changing a control, not on
  every step of a drag, and not at all while a model is playing.
- A throw anywhere in a page (the address resolver, the header, the topics page) shows
  a one-line notice with a link home, from an error boundary at the route, instead of a
  blank page. After an in-place move to another concept, focus goes to the heading and
  the new title is announced.
- In a development build every silent fallback also writes one console warning naming
  what was asked for and what was shown (an unknown preset, an unknown or unmapped `c`, a
  crashed model with its type, preset and spec), so a builder's broken link does not look
  like a working one.
- Navigation between pages no longer reloads the whole site: `Root` re-renders on a hash
  change, and the Try page re-resolves when its hash changes, so stepping to the next
  concept keeps the header in place and swaps only the stage.
- A footer line on the Try and topics pages: "We count which models get opened. No names,
  no accounts."
- The Try page's body text uses the site's body font variable; the leftover warm tint on
  the old topic pill and the system-font fallback in `.viz` are removed with the rebuild.

Tests: `fmt` cases (three figures, whole-number digits kept, the `sig`, `int` and
`percent` options, both scientific thresholds, non-finite); the previous, next, position
and unit-end lookups. Browser specs (`bun run test:e2e`, Playwright, run in CI against
the built site): the frame at 375px (stage stays visible while the last control is in
view) and 1280px (no page scroll); each of the six stage states, using a fixture model
that exists only in development and sweep builds and can refuse, crash and fail to
load; every row of the deep-link table; the topics filters and empty states; the walk
landing, topics, model, next, "all topics", with no full page load and no canvas left
behind; a hash change away from the sign-in and onboarding pages, which used to rely on
the reload; a tab-order walk of one model per stage kind (board from Wave 1); reduced motion (two screenshots
half a second apart are identical); and an accessibility scan of each model's default
preset (axe, a second new development dependency).

### Usage logging

Breadth is only a discovery instrument if use is measured, so the logging code ships in
Wave 0 (0d).

- `src/lib/usage.ts` records three events per model visit: `open` once a model's address
  has resolved and it renders, `interacted` on the first input, and `engaged` with the
  seconds the tab was visible on it since the last report (each at most 30 minutes; the
  report adds them up), sent when the page is hidden or left and when the student moves
  to another model. On the topics page it records `want` when a student clicks a
  concept that has no model yet. Each row carries the model type, preset, concept id
  when there is one, a random per-tab session id and the app version. No account id, no
  free text.
- Rows are sent with a plain `fetch` to the table's REST address, with the public key
  only and `keepalive`, never through the signed-in Supabase client. A signed-in
  student's rows therefore carry no identity and are accepted by the same rule as
  everyone else's, which is what "no names, no accounts" promises.
- Nothing is recorded from a development build, from `#/sweep` or `#/kits`, or from a
  browser where `localStorage.saras_no_usage` is set (the founder's own; the read is
  guarded, since private windows can refuse it). In those cases each event is written
  to the console instead, so a builder can see it fire.
- Rows go to a Supabase table `model_events`, defined in `supabase/model_events.sql`,
  which the founder runs once and can run again after any change: it creates the table
  if missing and drops and recreates its rules, so the rules can change later. The
  rules: anonymous insert only, no select, update or delete; the event is one of the
  four names; type and preset are short lowercase-and-hyphen strings; the concept id
  has the id shape; seconds are 0 to 1800; the session is a UUID; the time is set by
  the database and cannot be sent. Until the table exists every insert fails and is
  dropped.
- `src/lib/supabase.ts` no longer throws at import when the two environment values are
  missing, as they are in a fresh worktree: the client is built against a placeholder
  address, `hasSupabaseConfig` stays false, `usage.ts` does nothing, and the sign-in
  provider skips its session calls.
- A failed insert never reaches the student. In a development build, or with the
  founder's flag set, the coverage page (`#/coverage`) shows one of five states:
  "usage logging: on", "table missing: run supabase/model_events.sql", "rejected by
  the table's rules", "can't reach Supabase" and "not configured (no .env.local)". It
  learns the state from a probe row whose event name the table's named event rule
  refuses, so the probe never writes anything and the refusal is told apart from a
  permission failure by its code.

`supabase/usage_report.sql` is the query the founder runs to read it: opens and
returning sessions per course, per week, counting only model types that exist. It ends
with the statement that deletes rows older than 180 days, for the founder to run when
wanted.

Tests: a unit test of the payload builder (seconds capped, unknown fields dropped); that
a rejected insert does not throw; that nothing is sent in a development build, on the
sweep page or with the local flag; that each of the probe's five outcomes is read from
the right response; and `bun scripts/usage-smoke.ts`, which the founder runs once after
the SQL: with the public key, an insert of a test row succeeds, select, update and
delete are refused, and an oversized or misnamed row is refused, both signed out and
signed in.

### Continuous integration

`.github/workflows/ci.yml` (0a, growing in 0d) runs on every pull request: install,
`bun run typecheck`, `bun run test`, `bun run test:catalog`, lint, build,
`bun run check:bundle`, and then, against the built site, the browser specs
(`bun run test:e2e`) and the 2D sweep (`bun run sweep --nowebgl`). The workflow has
read-only permissions, its actions are pinned, and the Playwright browser is cached.
Bun's version is pinned in `package.json` (`packageManager`) and CI reads it from
there. `package-lock.json` is deleted; `bun.lock` is the one lockfile. 0b's PR confirms
that the Vercel preview builds with the generator in its build step. If the push is
refused because the token cannot write workflow files, the workflow is saved as
`docs/ci.yml.txt` and a TODOS.md entry asks the founder to add it, so CI cannot end up
silently missing. Because CI may be absent, a production deploy runs the generator in
check mode on its own.

### Catalog and mapping

- `bun run gen:curriculum` runs the model registry and then
  `src/data/curriculum/build.ts`. The build reads `pure.gen.ts` and writes one compact
  catalog file, `src/data/curriculum/catalog.gen.json` (734 rows, about 65KB before
  compression), which the topics page and the Try header load once. One file, because
  the tab counts and the search across subjects need every subject on first paint.
  The catalog is generated and not committed, like the registry files. A model branch's
  own mappings are in its catalog as soon as its `concepts.ts` is saved, so its "Try"
  chip, its `?c=` link and its previous and next links all work in that branch, and
  parallel branches never conflict on it.
- The committed subject JSON (`physics.json` and the rest) no longer carries `specType`.
  It is a function of the raw files alone, so no model branch changes it. Everything that
  read `specType` from it (the curriculum index helpers, the coverage page, the seed
  script, `src/topics/concepts.ts`) reads the mapping from the catalog or from
  `pure.gen.ts` instead. The build sorts by character code, not by locale, so its output
  is the same on every machine.
- Catalog order: courses follow an explicit ordered list of the 23 course slugs in a new
  `src/data/curriculum/courseOrder.ts`; units follow each course's own `units` array;
  concepts keep the order they have in the raw files. Each concept is
  `{ id, title, model? }` where `model` is `[type, preset]`. A unit with no concepts is
  left out of the catalog.
- The 4 existing mappings move into the `concepts.ts` of `projectile`, `orbit-sim` and
  `ray-diagram`. `specType` is removed from `raw/*.json`, and the build fails if a raw file
  sets it. Mapping has one source.
- A builder who finds that the model does not faithfully show an assigned concept moves
  it from `mapped` to `declined` in `concepts.ts`, with a one-line reason. The build's
  report lists declined concepts, so the integrator can reassign them or leave them
  unmapped. A builder does not add mappings: a concept the model turns out to show is
  proposed in the builder's hand-off report and added by the integrator, who knows what
  the sibling models claim.
- The build applies the Goal's gate and prints the Goal's report. The exclusion list lives
  in `src/data/curriculum/exclusions.ts`. The mapping checks are one pure function,
  `validateMappings`, so they can be tested on small fixtures. It also fails when
  `courseOrder.ts` misses a course or names one that does not exist.
- The build has two modes. In the normal mode (the dev server, every model branch) a
  "pending" exclusion for a course that is now mapped, and a model with no mapped
  concept, are warnings; every other failure stops the build. In check mode
  (`bun run test:catalog`, CI, the integrator, and every production deploy) those
  warnings are errors, and the build also fails if a committed subject JSON differs
  from what the raw files produce, if any model is still a stub, if a `KIT-REQUEST.md`
  remains, or if a "pending Wave N" exclusion is still there once every model planned
  for Wave N is built.
- One exemption, so that Wave 0 can pass its own checks: `wave-oscillator`,
  `circuit-diagram`, `free-body-diagram` and `circular-motion` have no mapped concept
  until their Wave 1 extensions. They are named in a short list beside the check, which
  lets them pass unmapped, and the Wave 1 PR deletes the list. Each has only its default
  preset, so the orphan-preset test already passes for them.

Tests for this section, in `registry.test.ts`: every mapped concept id exists in the
curriculum; no concept maps to two models; every mapped preset exists in that model's
`presets.ts`. `validateMappings` is tested on fixtures for each failure it must catch
(unknown concept, duplicate mapping, missing preset, raw `specType`, uncovered course,
stale or unknown exclusion, a concept both mapped and declined, a model with nothing
mapped, a course missing from the order, a pending entry that outlived its wave) and for
both modes. The generator's
complete-folder rule is tested in `scripts/gen.test.ts` (tests under `scripts/` may use
node APIs; the runner executes them too).

- `src/coverage/CurriculumCoverage.tsx` uses the real mapping as the base layer. A real
  mapping wins and is read-only there; the localStorage planning layer applies only to
  concepts with no real mapping.

### Deep links

The address is read again whenever the hash changes; nothing here needs a page reload.

| URL | Resolves to |
|---|---|
| `#/try` | `projectile`, its default preset (today's default); header shows the model label |
| `#/try/<type>` | that model's default preset; header shows the model label |
| `#/try/<type>/<preset>` | that preset; header shows the model label and preset label |
| any of the above with `?c=<conceptId>`, id known and mapped | the `(type, preset)` that concept maps to (the concept wins over the path); header shows concept title, course and unit, and a link to the next mapped concept in the unit when one exists |
| `#/try?c=<conceptId>` with no type in the path | the stage shows its skeleton until the catalog has loaded, then the concept's model (never `projectile` first) |
| `?c=` with an unknown or unmapped id | `c` is ignored; resolved from the path |
| a path or value that cannot be decoded (a stray `%`) | treated as an unknown type; the notice, never a thrown error |
| known type, unknown preset | the type's default preset; header shows the model label |
| unknown type | a "No model called X" notice with a link to topics; nothing is rendered in its place |

The header's concept data comes from the catalog, loaded lazily. The model named by the
path renders at once; when the catalog arrives the header fills in, and the model is
swapped only in the rare case that the concept maps somewhere else. The `open` event is
sent once, after the address has settled. If the catalog fails to load, `c` is treated
as unknown: the header shows the model label and no previous or
next links. Previous, next and position follow catalog order and skip unmapped concepts
(see "Try page frame" for the unit-end behaviour).

Tests: a unit test of the pure resolver with one case per table row, and of the
previous, next and position lookups (middle of unit, first, last, last in course,
unknown id).

### Topics page as a course browser

- Reads the generated catalog (one lazy chunk) instead of Supabase. The topics
  page no longer depends on the database; the seed script keeps mirroring the curriculum
  for relational use. The live-table column check added on this branch goes away with the
  query it guarded.
- Five subject tabs (adds Computing), each showing how many of its concepts are
  interactive. Courses, units and concepts appear in catalog order.
- A concept with a model is the prominent chip: full-strength text, the azure edge it has
  today, and its "Try →" link, `#/try/<type>/<preset>?c=<conceptId>`. A concept without
  one is a quiet chip. Clicking a quiet chip marks it "Noted" for the session and records
  a `want` event; it is a button with a pressed state, so it works by keyboard.
- Search box, labelled "Search concepts": case-insensitive title substring across all
  five subjects. While a query is active each tab shows its match count.
- "Interactive only" toggle, a labelled switch, off by default: hides concepts with no
  model.
- Search and toggle combine with AND. While either is active, units and courses with no
  matching concept are hidden and courses with a match are expanded.
- Empty states, each one line with a way forward:
  - a query that matches nothing in this subject but something elsewhere: "No matches in
    Math. 1 in Physics", linking to that tab;
  - a query that matches nothing anywhere: "No concepts match";
  - a query that matches only concepts without models while the toggle is on: "3 concepts
    match, none interactive yet. Show all", which turns the toggle off;
  - no query, toggle on, nothing interactive: "No interactive models in this subject yet".
- Each course header shows "N of M interactive", counted over the whole course whatever
  the search or toggle says; a course with none shows a muted "none yet".
- The subject, query, toggle and open course live in the address
  (`#/topics?s=math&q=slope&i=1&u=algebra-1`), so "all topics" on the Try page returns
  the student to where they were.
- A built model that no concept links to is still reachable: a subject tab ends with a
  "Not yet linked to a concept" row listing those models by label, each linking to
  `#/try/<type>`, under the model's first subject. The row is absent when it is empty.
  Between 0c and Wave 1 it holds the four legacy models named in "Catalog and mapping";
  after Wave 1 it is empty.
- If the catalog fails to load, the page shows the existing error line with a retry
  button.

Tests: unit tests of the pure filter and count functions (match, no match, empty query,
mixed case, toggle alone, toggle with search, match only in another subject, match with
nothing interactive), of the list of built models with no mapping, of reading and
writing the address state, and a browser check that
walks two next hops through "Forms of linear equations" and returns by "all topics" to
the same open course.

### First real mappings (so Wave 0 is verifiable end to end)

`function-grapher` is brought to the full recipe (folder, presets, `PlotStage`, 375px
pass, `built:true`) with presets for the Algebra 1 concepts it shows faithfully: absolute
value functions, graphing linear equations, slope of a line, slope-intercept form,
point-slope form, standard form of a line, x- and y-intercepts, graphing parabolas, vertex
form of a quadratic, exponential functions, exponential growth and decay models. Its test
asserts every preset passes the model's `validate` (which tolerates asymptotes) and that
these eleven, which have none, are finite at every sample across their domain. The unit
"Forms of linear equations" then has three mapped concepts in a row, which is what the
next-in-unit browser check walks. The presets and mappings are data and land in 0b, the
model reaches the full recipe in 0c, and the browser walk is in 0d.

### The builder's path and the docs

The people who use this structure are the agents that each build one model, the agent
that checks it, the integrator, and the founder. Their first five minutes, from a fresh
git worktree:

| Step | Command | Result |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `bun run setup:worktree` | installs, copies only the browser-safe (`VITE_`) lines of the main checkout's `.env.local` when there is one (never the server key), picks a port from this worktree's path and checks it is free, saves it, runs the generator, prints the address to open |
| 2 | `bun dev` | the dev server on that port |
| 3 | open `#/try/<type>` | the model's stub, live inside the real frame (the integrator scaffolded it with `bun run new:model`) |
| 4 | edit `core.ts` or `Widget.tsx` | the picture changes on save |
| 5 | `bun run test <type>` and `bun run sweep <type>` | the model's own assertions; its presets at both widths, with and without WebGL |
| 6 | `bun run check:branch <type>` | confirms the branch changed nothing outside the model's folder |

Target: steps 1 to 3 in under five minutes, most of it the install. `vite.config.ts` reads
the saved port and refuses to start on a busy one, and the dev server answers a small
identity request with its worktree's path, which the sweep checks, so neither a builder
nor the sweep ends up looking at another worktree's server.

Docs written in Wave 0, because 65 builders will read them and none of them wrote the
foundation:

- `docs/MODELS.md`, the authoring guide: a glossary with one word per thing (model, model
  type, spec, preset, widget, stage, kit, registry, catalog, concept; the older names
  `specTypes.ts` and `VisualizePanel` are mapped onto them); the steps above; a
  copy-paste skeleton for each file; `projectile` and `function-grapher` walked through as
  worked examples; a table of every command; the kit-request and declined-concept rules;
  and one anchored entry per message in "Messages a builder sees". A dated "Changes"
  list at the top records every change to the contract or a kit.
- `docs/MODEL-REVIEW.md`, the second agent's checklist (the recipe's verification and
  design checks and the reference-data check) with a fixed pass/fail report format, so
  every review reads the same.
- `docs/MODEL-BRIEF.md`, the template the integrator fills in for each builder: the
  model's row from the catalog, its assigned concepts, its stage and kits, the files it
  may touch (its own folder only), the commands to run, and what the hand-off report must
  contain (what was built, anything declined, any kit request, and what slowed the
  builder down).
- `README.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and `src/data/curriculum/README.md` are corrected: the project
  structure, the full command list (`test`, `typecheck`, `gen:curriculum`, `new:model`,
  `sweep`, `setup:worktree`), the model count, and how the curriculum is regenerated.
  Counts in docs are copied from the build's report, not typed from memory.

The contract and the kits change only through the integrator. A change to `types.ts`
shows up as a type error in every model it affects. A breaking kit change is made
together with the edits to every model that uses it, in one PR, and noted in the
"Changes" list.

After Wave 1 the integrator records, beside the effort actuals, the time from a fresh
worktree to the stub on screen and the "what slowed me down" lines from the builders'
reports, and fixes the guide or the scaffold before Wave 2.

Tests: `scripts/setup-worktree.test.ts` (the port is free and saved; an existing
`.env.local` is not overwritten).

### Messages a builder sees

Every check that can stop a builder says what is wrong, with the actual value; where, by
file and id; and what to do. Each has an entry in `docs/MODELS.md`.

| Check | Example |
|---|---|
| generator, incomplete folder | `skipped unit-circle: Widget.tsx is missing (found widget.tsx; names are case-sensitive). A model needs meta.ts, core.ts, presets.ts and Widget.tsx.` |
| generator, no default export | `unit-circle/presets.ts has no default export. Expected: export default [...] satisfies Preset<UnitCircleSpec>[]` |
| mapping, unknown concept | `unit-circle/concepts.ts maps "math.algebra-2.unit-circl", which is not a concept. Closest id: "math.algebra-2.unit-circle-a2".` |
| mapping, two models | `"physics.ap-physics-1.buoyancy" is mapped by both buoyancy and fluid-flow. A concept has one model: remove it from one concepts.ts.` |
| mapping, missing preset | `unit-circle/concepts.ts maps "math.algebra-2.unit-circle-a2" to preset "radians", but presets.ts has: default, special-angles.` |
| mapping, raw file | `raw/physics.json sets specType on "physics.ap-physics-1.buoyancy". Mappings live in a model's concepts.ts: delete the field.` |
| gate, uncovered course | `Course "geometry" has no mapped concept and no entry in exclusions.ts. Map a concept, or add an entry with a reason.` |
| gate, pending entry now covered | `exclusions.ts still lists "algebra-2" as pending, but unit-circle maps into it. Delete that entry.` (a warning in a model branch, an error in check mode) |
| gate, unknown course | `exclusions.ts names course "algebra-3", which does not exist. Did you mean "algebra-2"?` |
| check mode, subject JSON | `physics.json differs from what raw/physics.json produces. Run bun run gen:curriculum and commit physics.json.` |
| check mode, stub | `unit-circle is still a scaffold stub. The reviewer removes stub: true from meta.ts when the model passes review.` |
| check mode, kit request | `unit-circle/KIT-REQUEST.md is unanswered. The integrator extends the kit or accepts the local drawing, then deletes the file.` |
| registry, preset refused | `free-body-diagram preset "steep" fails its own validate: "Angle must be between 0 and 90" (field: angle).` |
| registry, orphan preset | `unit-circle preset "radians" is not the default and no concept maps to it. Map a concept to it or delete it.` |
| registry, Eq / WP | `planned.ts says rc-circuit has a word-problem parser, but rc-circuit/parse.ts is missing.` |
| gate, nothing mapped | `unit-circle maps no concept, so nothing links to it. Map a concept, or hold the model out of this PR and report it.` (a warning in a model branch, an error in check mode) |
| gate, pending entry outlived its wave | `exclusions.ts still lists "organic-chemistry" as pending Wave 2, and every Wave 2 model is built. Map a concept into the course, or make the entry a permanent exclusion with a reason.` (check mode) |
| registry, range sampling | `buoyancy: with preset "default" and fluidDensity at its minimum (0), derive().floatingFraction is not finite. Raise the range's minimum or return "undefined here".` |
| model rules | `unit-circle/Widget.tsx:41 uses a raw <input>. Use SliderRow or NumberField from the controls kit, or leave a KIT-REQUEST.md.` |
| model rules, imports | `unit-circle/core.ts imports react. A model's pure files import no React, three or CSS: move the drawing into Widget.tsx.` |
| branch scope | `This branch changed src/engine/kits/PlotStage.tsx, outside src/engine/models/unit-circle/. A model branch changes only its own folder: leave a KIT-REQUEST.md instead.` |
| a test with no checks | `unit-circle.test.ts made no assertions. Add checks, or delete the file.` |
| bundle | `unit-circle (a board model) loads three through kits/ParticleBox/three.tsx. Import the flat renderer instead.` |
| model rules, colour | `unit-circle/widget.css:12 uses the literal colour #7fb2ff. Use a colour variable (see materials.ts).` |
| an assertion | `FAIL projectile range at 45 degrees: expected 40.775 (within 0.004), got 40.12` |
| wrong test command | `These tests are standalone scripts. Use: bun run test` |
| port in use | `Port 5183 is in use. Run bun run setup:worktree to pick another.` |
| no Supabase config | `No .env.local: sign-in and usage logging are off. Models and topics work without it.` |

Tests: each message is asserted by the test of the check that produces it.

## The catalog

Mapping rule, applied to every concept: map it only when the model shows THAT concept
faithfully. Precedent: `optical-instruments` (lens combinations) was left unmapped because
the single-lens widget cannot show a microscope. Unmapped concepts stay honest gaps.

Concept ids are assigned before a wave starts, not by the model builders. The integrator
passes each new model's concept ids to the scaffold before fan-out, so two models never
claim one concept; the builder points each one at the preset that shows it, or declines
it with a reason. Tie-break when two could: the concept goes to the model
whose primary interaction is that concept. The "Concept families" column below is the
input to that assignment, not the assignment itself.

Wave order is the founder's choice at approval (2026-10-01). After Wave 1 the subjects
with nothing built come first: chemistry, then biology. Calculus and geometry, the rest
of physics, and statistics with computing follow.

"Gate" is the deterministic core each model's validator and tests rest on. "Stage" is the
recipe's stage kind. "Eq / WP" says whether the model has an equation set and a
word-problem parser.

### Wave 1: finish the designed vocabulary (7 models, completes the original registry)

| Model | Subject | Stage | Student manipulates | Gate | Eq / WP | Concept families |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `unit-circle` | math | board | an angle on the circle; sine, cosine and tangent traces unroll beside it | exact trig values at special angles; sin^2 + cos^2 = 1 | no / no | unit circle, radians, special angles, reference angles, Pythagorean identity, graphs of sine and cosine |
| `linear-transform` | math | plot | the four entries of a 2x2 matrix; grid, basis vectors and unit square deform | determinant = signed area; eigenpairs from the characteristic polynomial; inverse exists iff det != 0 | no / no | matrices as transformations, determinant as area, eigenvectors, composition, inverses, rotation/reflection/projection matrices |
| `surface-3d` | math | 3D | z = f(x, y) with parameters; a probe point with tangent plane, gradient arrow and contour projection | mathjs evaluation on a sample grid; numeric partials vs symbolic; finite on every cell drawn, with undefined cells left as holes and an en dash at an undefined probe | no / no | 3D graphs, level curves, partial derivatives, gradient, tangent planes, multivariable critical points and the second-derivative test |
| `vector-field` | math | plot | the two components of F(x, y); a probe showing divergence and scalar curl; a dropped particle tracing a flow line | numeric divergence and curl vs closed form on test fields; RK4 flow lines vs known solutions | no / no | vector fields, 2D curl and 2D divergence, divergence of a vector field |
| `molecule-3d` | chemistry | 3D | a molecule from a curated library; rotate; toggle lone pairs, bond dipoles, hybridization labels | VSEPR class from electron domains; ideal angles exact (109.47, 120, 180); net dipole by vector sum; measured angles stored and labelled as measured | no / no | VSEPR, Lewis structures, bond and molecular polarity, hybridization, intermolecular forces |
| `reaction-energy` | chemistry, biology | plot | activation energy, enthalpy change, temperature, catalyst on/off | Arrhenius rate ratio; delta-H = products minus reactants; a catalyst lowers Ea and leaves delta-H alone | yes / yes | activation energy and collision theory, catalysis, enthalpy changes, enzymes and activation energy |
| `step-walkthrough` | computing | board with `Stepper` | an input array or disk count; play and scrub through the steps | steps come from RUNNING the real algorithm, never authored; invariants asserted per step (sorted prefix, search window contains the target, legal Hanoi moves) | no / no | selection, insertion, merge and quick sort; linear and binary search; Towers of Hanoi |

Also in Wave 1: `function-grapher` presets for the Algebra 2, precalculus and trigonometry
graph concepts it shows faithfully (the integrator's concept assignment lists them), and
the extensions below.

### Existing models: extensions and remapping (Wave 1)

A concept is remapped onto an existing model only when the model, with its extension where
one is listed, shows that concept. Each extension carries assertions in that model's test
file. New spec fields are optional with defaults, so the existing schema branches and
prompt text in `src/engine/spec.ts`, and `api/spec.ts`, stay untouched.

| Existing model | Extension | Concepts it then serves |
|---|---|---|
| `circular-motion` | optional mass, and the equation F = m v^2 / r | centripetal force and acceleration; uniform circular motion |
| `wave-oscillator` | the equation F = k x beside the existing period and energy equations | Hooke's law and springs; simple harmonic motion |
| `circuit-diagram` | optional named network preset (single loop, series pair, parallel pair) that seeds the existing builder; test asserts Kirchhoff's current and voltage laws hold in the solver's result | Ohm's law and resistance; series and parallel circuits; Kirchhoff's circuit laws |
| `free-body-diagram` | optional static coefficient (defaults to the existing `friction` field, which stays the kinetic coefficient, so current specs behave exactly as before; a static coefficient below the kinetic one is refused); optional applied force F along the surface; a flat-surface preset. The block holds while abs(m g sin(theta) - F) is at most mu_s m g cos(theta); otherwise it slides with kinetic friction opposing the motion | forces on an inclined plane; friction (static and kinetic); free-body diagrams |
| `orbit-sim` | optional orbiting mass and the readouts F = M m / r^2 and U = -M m / r in its natural units | Newton's law of universal gravitation; gravitational potential energy and escape velocity |

`projectile` and `ray-diagram` keep their current mappings. These five extensions edit the
shared legacy files, so one agent does them in sequence; they are not part of the parallel
fan-out.

### Wave 2: chemistry (12 models, on top of `molecule-3d` and `reaction-energy`)

| Model | Stage | Student manipulates | Gate | Eq / WP | Concept families |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `atom-model` | 3D | element; isotope; ion charge | shell capacities 2 n^2; configuration by filling order with the documented exceptions held in data; average atomic mass = weighted sum | no / no | atomic structure, electron configuration, atomic orbitals, ion formation, isotopes and average atomic mass |
| `titration` | plot | acid and base strength, concentrations, volume added | pH from the charge-balance equation, solved by bounded bisection to a stated tolerance; equivalence at equal moles; Henderson-Hasselbalch inside the buffer region | yes / yes | acids, bases and the pH scale; buffers and titration curves; weak acid-base equilibria |
| `equilibrium` | plot | initial concentrations, K, a stress (add, remove, compress) | ICE table solved by bounded bisection to a stated tolerance; direction of shift from Q against K | yes / no | chemical equilibrium and Le Chatelier's principle, equilibrium constant and reaction quotient |
| `crystal-lattice` | 3D | lattice type (simple cubic, body-centred, face-centred, rock salt) | atoms per cell 1, 2, 4; packing fractions pi/6, pi sqrt(3)/8, pi sqrt(2)/6; rock salt has 4 formula units per cell and 6:6 coordination | no / no | types of solids and crystal lattices, ionic bonding and lattices, metallic bonding |
| `galvanic-cell` | board | two half-cells from a curated table; concentrations | cell potential = cathode minus anode; Nernst equation | yes / yes | galvanic and electrolytic cells; cell potential and the Nernst equation |
| `states-of-matter` | plot with `ParticleBox` | heat added to a sample | q = m c dT on the slopes; q = m L on the plateaus | yes / yes | states of matter and phase changes |
| `stoichiometry` | board | a balanced reaction and starting amounts | atoms balance; limiting reactant by exact rational arithmetic | no / yes | balancing chemical equations, stoichiometry and limiting reactants, the mole |
| `stereochemistry` | 3D | a chiral molecule from a curated set beside its mirror image; swap two substituents | R or S from priorities stored for the curated set, each checked in tests against the atomic-number rule at the first point of difference; the mirror image and any single swap flip it | no / no | chirality and stereoisomers, optical activity and Fischer projections |
| `conformations` | 3D | the dihedral angle of ethane or butane, seen as a Newman projection, with its energy curve | energy curve has period 120 degrees for ethane; staggered minima and eclipsed maxima; butane anti below gauche; barrier values curated and labelled approximate | no / no | conformational analysis and Newman projections |
| `reaction-mechanism` | board with `Stepper` | a mechanism (SN2, SN1, E2, E1, electrophilic addition); step through it | atoms and total charge conserved at every step; SN2 inverts the stereocentre and SN1 gives both; the rate law's order matches the mechanism | no / no | SN1 and SN2 reactions, E1 and E2 elimination, electrophilic addition to alkenes |
| `ideal-gas` | 3D with `ParticleBox` | pressure, volume, temperature, amount; a path on the PV diagram | P V = n R T; v_rms = sqrt(3 R T / M) | yes / yes | kinetic theory and the ideal gas law, PV diagrams, gas laws, kinetic molecular theory |
| `nuclear-decay` | plot | half-life and elapsed time | N = N0 / 2^(t / T) | yes / yes | radioactive decay and half-life, nuclear decay |

`ideal-gas` and `nuclear-decay` are built here because the chemistry courses have a gases
unit and a nuclear chemistry unit that nothing else fills; both also serve physics
(kinetic theory, PV diagrams, nuclear decay). Organic
chemistry's spectroscopy, nomenclature, aromaticity and carbonyl-chemistry concepts stay
unmapped: no model here shows them faithfully.

### Wave 3: biology (11 models)

| Model | Stage | Student manipulates | Gate | Eq / WP | Concept families |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `cell-explorer` | 3D | cell type (animal, plant, prokaryote); a selected organelle | curated organelle sets per cell type, with invariants (a prokaryote has no nucleus; only the plant cell has a wall, chloroplasts and a central vacuole) | no / no | eukaryotic cell organelles, organelle structure and function, cell theory and cell types |
| `membrane-transport` | board with `ParticleBox` | solute concentrations on each side; transport mode | net flux runs down the gradient and is zero at equal concentration; water moves toward higher solute | no / no | diffusion and osmosis, active transport, membrane transport mechanisms, water potential and tonicity |
| `dna-structure` | 3D | a base sequence; unzip to replicate | A pairs with T and G with C; strands antiparallel; each daughter keeps one parent strand | no / no | DNA structure, nucleic acid structure, DNA replication |
| `gene-expression` | board with `Stepper` | a DNA sequence; a point or frameshift mutation | transcription by complement with U for T; translation by the standard 64-codon table; start and stop codons | no / no | transcription and translation, the genetic code, mutations |
| `cell-division` | board with `Stepper` | mitosis or meiosis; step through the stages | chromosome and chromatid counts per stage: mitosis 2n to 2n, meiosis 2n to n | no / no | mitosis, meiosis, the cell cycle, meiosis and genetic diversity |
| `genetics` | board | parent genotypes for one or two genes; dominance pattern | Punnett ratios by enumerating gametes | no / yes | Mendelian genetics and Punnett squares, non-Mendelian inheritance, sex linkage |
| `hardy-weinberg` | plot | allele frequency p | p^2 + 2 p q + q^2 = 1; genotype frequencies from p | yes / yes | Hardy-Weinberg equilibrium |
| `population-growth` | plot | growth rate, carrying capacity, predator and prey parameters | exponential and logistic closed forms; predator-prey by RK4 with its conserved quantity checked | yes / yes | population growth, population ecology and growth models, logistic growth models |
| `enzyme-kinetics` | plot | substrate concentration, Vmax, Km, inhibitor type | v = Vmax S / (Km + S); a competitive inhibitor raises apparent Km, a noncompetitive one lowers Vmax | yes / no | enzyme kinetics and regulation |
| `energy-pathways` | board with `Stepper` | a pathway; step through it | curated steps whose ATP, NADH and FADH2 tallies must sum to the stated net yields | no / no | glycolysis, the Krebs cycle, electron transport, cellular respiration, photosynthesis, the Calvin cycle |
| `natural-selection` | plot | selection strength, starting allele frequency, population size | the deterministic expectation for allele frequency per generation; the seeded simulation's mean tracks it | no / no | natural selection, mechanisms of natural selection |

`solid-geometry` (Wave 4) lists biology as a second subject: the cell-size and
surface-area-to-volume concept is mapped then.

### Wave 4: calculus and geometry (11 models)
