It names \"~12 spec types\" as the\n  near-term build target and says the project is pre-product with no demand evidence.\n\n","newText":"## Where things stand\n\n- 8 spec types are built: `function-grapher` (2D plot, flagged `built:false`, no tests, not\n  reachable from any page) and 7 physics models: `projectile`, `wave-oscillator`,\n  `circuit-diagram`, `free-body-diagram`, `circular-motion`, `orbit-sim`, `ray-diagram`.\n  Of those 7, `orbit-sim`, `ray-diagram` and the circuit solver have tests; `projectile`,\n  `wave-oscillator`, `free-body-diagram` and `circular-motion` have none.\n- 7 spec types in `src/data/curriculum/specTypes.ts` are planned but unbuilt: `surface-3d`,\n  `vector-field`, `unit-circle`, `linear-transform`, `molecule-3d`, `reaction-energy`,\n  `step-walkthrough`.\n- Curriculum: 734 concepts in 23 courses, 230 of them marked high 3D-fit. Mapped to a\n  model: 4 (all physics). Math 438, chemistry 76, biology 71, computing 54 have zero.\n- The mapping has already drifted: `raw/physics.json` sets `specType` on 1 concept while the\n  generated `physics.json` carries 4, so re-running the curriculum build today would drop 3.\n  The build script is run by hand and is not in `package.json`.\n- The browse spine is the topics page (`#/topics`). It reads the concept list from the\n  Supabase `concepts` table and shows \"Try →\" on a chip when the row has `spec_type`. That\n  column is set on 1 row. Only the founder can re-seed it.\n- The Try page (`#/try`) has no text input. The spec endpoint (`api/spec.ts`) has one\n  caller, behind a build flag that is off by default. The classifier is not on any path a\n  student can reach today.\n- `docs/DESIGN.md` (approved): AI emits a validated JSON spec, a deterministic renderer\n  draws it, a deterministic numeric check is the binding correctness gate, unknown input\n  gets a named fallback and never a guessed visual. It names \"~12 spec types\" as the\n  near-term build target and says the project is pre-product with no demand evidence.\n\n"},{"oldText":"## Goal\n\nEvery subject, and every course inside it, has in-depth interactive models, each built to\nthe same bar as the existing physics models, and every concept a model can faithfully show\nis reachable from the topics page.\n\nOut of the goal: the analogy and walkthrough channels (the other two \"ways\" per concept),\nuser accounts/persistence, and the photo-of-a-problem input.\n\n","newText":"## Goal\n\nEvery course that contains a concept a model can faithfully show has in-depth interactive\nmodels for those concepts, each built to the same bar as the existing physics models, and\nevery such concept is reachable from the topics page and walkable in unit order.\n\nOne gate and one report:\n\n- **Gate** (the curriculum build, `bun run gen:curriculum`, fails otherwise): every course\n  has at least one mapped concept or an entry in an explicit exclusion list. Wave 0 seeds\n  that list with one \"pending Wave N\" entry per course not yet covered (table below); each\n  wave deletes the entries it satisfies; after Wave 6 the list may contain only permanent\n  exclusions, each with a reason. The gate also fails on an entry for a course that is\n  already mapped, and on an entry naming a course that does not exist.\n- **Report** (printed by the build, never a gate): per-course coverage, and every high\n  3D-fit concept that is still unmapped.\n\nExpected coverage after all waves is roughly 250 to 300 of 734 concepts: the families\nnamed in the catalog below. The rest are symbolic-manipulation, proof and vocabulary\nconcepts no model shows faithfully. The number is a report, not a target to hit, so that\nnothing pushes toward mapping a concept a model does not really show.\n\nCourses and the wave that first gives each one a mapped concept:\n\n| Wave | Courses first covered |\n|---|---|\n| already mapped | AP Physics 1, AP Physics 2, High school physics, Physics library |\n| 0 | Algebra 1 |\n| 1 | Algebra 2, Precalculus, Trigonometry, Linear algebra, Multivariable calculus, AP Chemistry, High school chemistry, High school biology, Algorithms |\n| 2 | AP Calculus AB, AP Calculus BC, Differential equations, Geometry, AP Biology |\n| 4 | Organic chemistry |\n| 6 | AP Statistics, AP Computer Science Principles |\n| never | Computer programming (permanent exclusion) |\n\n"},{"oldText":"## The recipe (definition of done for one model)\n\nUnchanged from what the 7 physics models established. A model is done when it has:\n\n1. **Spec**: a TypeScript interface, a JSON-schema branch, a classifier-prompt paragraph,\n   and at least one example spec.\n2. **Deterministic core**: the closed-form (or otherwise deterministic) maths in one place,\n   used by BOTH the validation gate and the renderer, so what is drawn is what was checked.\n   `validate(spec)` refuses non-physical or out-of-range input with a plain reason.\n3. **Equation set** (when the topic is equation-shaped): LaTeX/KaTeX display, residual\n   form, solve-for-any-variable through `useEquationPanel`, canonical-SI units with the\n   unit dropdown from `units.ts`.\n4. **Word-problem parser** (when the topic is problem-shaped): regex parser that fills the\n   knowns and selects the asked-for unknown, offline.\n5. **Widget**: `Scene3D` stage in the clean-3D look (matte pearl/chrome, ONE glass-accent\n   focal object, azure / warm-azure vectors from `PALETTE`), a 2D SVG fallback from the same\n   maths when WebGL is missing, `frameloop=\"demand\"` unless animating, no `Label3D` inside a\n   demand-frameloop scene, motion gated on `prefers-reduced-motion`, nothing that jitters on\n   hover. Graph-category models use a flat plot stage in the same palette instead of 3D.\n6. **Registration**: dispatch + validation in the visualize surface, `built:true` in\n   `specTypes.ts`, deep link, concepts mapped.\n7. **Tests**: a standalone `bun` assertion script (same style as\n   `src/engine/physicsModels.test.ts`) covering the closed forms against hand-computed\n   values, the gate's refusals, the equation set's consistency with the core, and the\n   parser. Wired into `bun run test`.\n8. **Verification**: `bun run build`, `bun run lint`, and a browser pass at 375px and\n   1280px: renders, no console errors, controls drive the picture, fallback path renders.\n\n","newText":"## The recipe (definition of done for one model)\n\nThe recipe the 7 physics models established, written down. A model is done when it has:\n\n1. **Spec**: a TypeScript interface and named presets (at least a default). A spec is a flat\n   record of numbers, strings, enums and arrays of those, so a JSON schema can be generated\n   from it later without hand-authoring. Every preset carries a one-sentence \"try this\"\n   prompt in the spec's `notes` field (what to change and what to watch).\n2. **Deterministic core**: the closed-form (or otherwise deterministic) maths in one place,\n   used by BOTH the validation gate and the renderer, so what is drawn is what was checked.\n   `validate(spec)` refuses non-physical or out-of-range input with a plain reason.\n3. **Equation set** where the catalog's \"Eq\" column says yes: LaTeX/KaTeX display, residual\n   form, solve-for-any-variable through `useEquationPanel`, canonical-SI units with the\n   unit dropdown from `units.ts`. LaTeX strings are constants in code and are never built\n   from anything a student typed.\n4. **Word-problem parser** where the catalog's \"WP\" column says yes: a regex parser that\n   fills the knowns and selects the asked-for unknown, offline. Where a model has a parser\n   but no equation set, the parser only fills the widget's inputs.\n5. **Widget** on one of three stages, all in the same palette:\n   - `3D`: `Scene3D` in the clean-3D look (matte pearl/chrome, ONE glass-accent focal\n     object, azure / warm-azure vectors from `PALETTE`), with a 2D SVG fallback from the\n     same maths when WebGL is missing. `frameloop=\"demand\"` unless animating. No `Label3D`\n     inside a demand-frameloop scene; labels there are HTML laid over the canvas or a\n     legend beside it (the existing `ps__legend` pattern).\n   - `plot`: the shared `PlotStage` (axes, curves, probe point, shaded regions) for\n     graph-shaped models.\n   - `board`: a flat SVG board for discrete or tabular models (grids, tables, step lists).\n   Every stage: motion gated on `prefers-reduced-motion`, nothing that jitters on hover,\n   every control reachable by keyboard, usable at 375px.\n6. **One primary interaction per model.** Variations are presets of that interaction. A\n   second, different interaction is a second model.\n7. **Registration**: a `meta.ts` and a `manifest.ts` in the model's folder; the registry is\n   generated from the folder listing. Concepts mapped in the model's own `concepts.ts`.\n8. **Tests**: a standalone `bun` assertion script in the model's folder (same style as\n   `src/engine/physicsModels.test.ts`, no node or bun APIs, so the app typecheck stays\n   clean) covering the closed forms against hand-computed values, the gate's refusals, the\n   equation set's consistency with the core, the parser, and that every preset validates.\n   The test runner picks it up by filename.\n9. **Verification**: `bun run build`, `bun run lint`, the scripted sweep (below) at 375px\n   and 1280px with and without WebGL, and a hands-on browser pass of the new model:\n   controls drive the picture, readouts match a hand calculation. A second agent that did\n   not build the model repeats the hands-on pass before the model is merged.\n10. **Reference data**: any table of facts the maths cannot derive (bond angles, standard\n    potentials, the codon table, torsional barriers, organelle sets, pathway and mechanism\n    steps) lives in the model's `data.ts`, with the source cited beside each table. The\n    second agent checks each table against its cited source. Where sources disagree (ATP\n    yields, for one), the model shows the value with the convention it follows.\n\n"},{"oldText":"## Wave 0: foundation (no new visuals)\n\nToday a new model edits six shared files (`spec.ts`, `validate.ts`, `equations.ts`,\n`wordProblems.ts`, `VisualizePanel.tsx`, `specTypes.ts`) and `VisualizePanel` imports every\nwidget eagerly. That does not survive going from 8 models to 50+. Wave 0 fixes the shape\nonce so every later model is one self-contained folder.\n\n- **One folder per model**: `src/engine/models/<type>/` with `spec.ts`, `core.ts`\n  (maths + `validate`), `equations.ts`, `parse.ts`, `Widget.tsx`, `concepts.ts`,\n  `<type>.test.ts`, and an `index.ts` exporting a `ModelDefinition`\n  (`type`, `label`, `subject`, `presets`, `validate`, lazy `Widget`, schema branch, prompt\n  paragraph).\n- **Registry**: `src/engine/models/registry.ts`, one import line per model.\n  `VisualizePanel` drops its `switch` statements and looks models up in the registry;\n  widgets load with `React.lazy`, so each model is its own chunk.\n- **Existing 8 models are registered through thin adapters**; their internals stay where\n  they are. No rewrite of working, tested physics.\n- **Presets**: a model exposes named presets (`presets: Record<string, Spec>`). A concept\n  maps to `(type, preset)`, so \"Vertex form of a quadratic\" and \"Exponential functions\"\n  both use `function-grapher` but open on their own correct expression and sliders. Deep\n  link becomes `#/try/<type>/<preset>`; `#/try/<type>` keeps working.\n- **Concept mapping lives with the model**: each model's `concepts.ts` lists the concept\n  ids it serves and the preset for each. `src/data/curriculum/build.ts` reads the\n  aggregate, stamps `specType` into the generated subject JSON, and writes a compact\n  `modelMap.json` (concept id to type + preset, mapped concepts only).\n- **Topics page reads `modelMap.json`** for its \"Try →\" links instead of the database\n  `spec_type` column, so links are correct without re-seeding Supabase. The seed script\n  still mirrors `spec_type` for relational queries. Topics gains the Computing tab.\n- **Classifier in two stages** (`api/spec.ts`): stage 1 picks a spec type from the enum\n  (small schema); stage 2 fills that one type's schema. A single `anyOf` with 50 branches\n  is not a safe structured-output schema. The deterministic gate is unchanged.\n- **Shared kits** (built once, used by many models): `PlotStage` (2D plot with axes,\n  probe point, shaded regions, same palette), `ParticleBox` (seeded particle animation for\n  gases / diffusion / states of matter; readouts always come from the closed form, never\n  from the animation), `Stepper` (step list + play/scrub transport for procedural models).\n- **Invariant tests** (`src/engine/models/registry.test.ts`): every registered model has a\n  `specTypes.ts` entry with `built:true` and vice versa; every preset passes its own\n  `validate`; every mapped concept id exists in the curriculum; no concept maps to two\n  models; every concept's preset exists.\n- `function-grapher` is promoted to a first-class model (registry, presets, `built:true`).\n- Carries the topics-page fix already on this branch (`src/topics/concepts.ts`).\n\n","newText":"## Wave 0: foundation and browse\n\nToday a new model edits six shared files (`spec.ts`, `validate.ts`, `equations.ts`,\n`wordProblems.ts`, `VisualizePanel.tsx`, `specTypes.ts`), `VisualizePanel` imports every\nwidget eagerly, and the `test` script is a hand-written `&&` chain. That does not survive\ngoing from 8 models to 73. Wave 0 fixes the shape once, so a new model touches only its\nown folder, and makes the topics page a working course browser.\n\n### Engine shape\n\n- **One folder per model**: `src/engine/models/<type>/` containing\n  - `meta.ts`: a few lines of pure data. `type`, `label`, `subjects` (one or more;\n    descriptive only, it does not restrict which concepts the model may serve),\n    `category`, `stage`, `description`, `defaultPreset`, and preset ids with their labels.\n  - `manifest.ts`: pure TypeScript, no React or three. The preset specs and `validate`.\n  - `core.ts` (maths), `equations.ts`, `parse.ts` as the recipe requires.\n  - `Widget.tsx` and `widget.css` (class names prefixed with the model type). Shared\n    classes stay in `src/engine/visualize.css`, which new models do not edit.\n  - `concepts.ts`: pure data, `Record<conceptId, presetId>`.\n  - `<type>.test.ts`.\n- **Generated registry**: `scripts/gen-models.mjs` (plain Node, no dependencies) scans the\n  folders and writes three files into `src/engine/models/`:\n  - `meta.gen.ts`: imports every `meta.ts`. Small, loaded eagerly by the Try page, the\n    coverage page and `specTypes.ts`.\n  - `load.gen.ts`: one dynamic import per `manifest.ts` and one `React.lazy` per\n    `Widget.tsx`, so a model's maths, presets and widget are its own chunks.\n  - `concepts.gen.ts`: imports every `concepts.ts`. Used by the curriculum build and the\n    tests only; the browser never loads it.\n  A folder is registered only when its `meta.ts`, `manifest.ts` and `Widget.tsx` all\n  exist; a folder holding just a pre-assigned `concepts.ts` is ignored by all three files,\n  so a half-built sibling never breaks another model's branch. These three files are\n  gitignored and produced by `predev`, `prebuild`, `pretest` and `gen:curriculum`, so they\n  cannot be stale and model branches never conflict on them.\n- **`specTypes.ts` is derived**: built entries come from `meta.gen.ts`; ids in this plan's\n  catalog that are not built yet come from one static `PLANNED` list written once in\n  Wave 0.\n- **Test runner**: `bun run test` runs `scripts/run-tests.ts`, which executes every\n  `src/**/*.test.ts` as a standalone script and fails on the first non-zero exit. A\n  separate `bun run test:catalog` re-runs the curriculum build in check mode and fails if\n  the committed catalog or subject JSON differs from what the build produces; the\n  integrator runs it on the combined tree, model branches do not.\n- **Existing 8 models get a folder** with `meta.ts`, a `manifest.ts` (their example specs\n  move here from `VisualizePanel.tsx` as presets; `validate` is re-exported from\n  `validate.ts`), `concepts.ts`, a `Widget.tsx` that re-exports the current component, and\n  a test file. Their maths, equations, parsers, widgets, schema branches and prompt text\n  stay in the files they are in now, and `physicsModels.test.ts` and\n  `circuitNetwork.test.ts` stay where they are. `projectile`, `wave-oscillator`,\n  `free-body-diagram`, `circular-motion` and `function-grapher` get the closed-form\n  assertions they never had, in their folder's test file; that file is also where each\n  model's Wave 1 extension assertions go.\n- **`VisualizePanel`** drops its import list, `MODEL_TYPES`, `validate` switch and\n  `renderModel` switch, and looks models up in the generated registry. Its list of\n  supported models in the \"No interactive visual yet\" message comes from the registry.\n  Under the header it shows the preset's \"try this\" note, and it sets the browser tab\n  title to the concept title (or the model label).\n  The Try page has no model or preset picker: models are reached from the topics page,\n  and the existing \"all topics\" link stays. While a model's chunks load, the stage shows\n  a skeleton in the stage's own shape. If a chunk fails to load, the stage shows \"This model didn't load\" with a retry button. The\n  widget renders inside an error boundary: if it throws, the stage shows \"This model hit\n  an error\" with a link back to topics instead of a blank page.\n- **Kits** live in `src/engine/kits/` and are built by the integrator before the wave that\n  first needs them: `PlotStage` in Wave 0 (extracted from `widgets/FunctionGrapher.tsx`;\n  it exposes its x and y scales, a pointer-to-data mapping and an overlay slot for\n  children, so a model can draw arrows, segments, rays and draggable points on it without\n  editing the kit), `Stepper` in Wave 1 (a pure step reducer plus transport: play stops at the\n  last step, scrubbing pauses, and under reduced motion there is no autoplay, only step\n  buttons), `ParticleBox` in Wave 3 (a pure seeded simulation core with an SVG renderer\n  and a three renderer, so it serves 3D, plot and board stages; at most 400 particles).\n  Each kit's pure part (plot scales, step reducer, particle core) has its own unit test.\n  `safeExpression` also lives here from Wave 0: the one path by which any model turns\n  typed text into a function (mathjs parse, an allowlist of node types and function\n  names, a 200-character cap, no `evaluate` of raw strings). Every model that accepts a\n  typed expression uses it.\n  Unit families a wave needs and `src/engine/units.ts` lacks (pressure, amount,\n  concentration, charge, field, electron-volts and so on) are added by the integrator at\n  the same point. Temperature stays in kelvin; the unit table is single-factor.\n- **Sweep page**: `#/sweep`, present only in development builds, mounts every model and\n  preset in turn inside an error boundary and lists, per preset, whether it mounted\n  without error and drew a non-empty stage. `?nowebgl` forces the 2D fallback path.\n  Tests: none of its own; it is the verification tool.\n\nTests for this section: `src/engine/models/registry.test.ts` asserts every `meta.ts` type\nis unique and matches its folder name; preset ids in `meta.ts` equal those in\n`manifest.ts`; every preset passes its own `validate`; the default preset exists.\n\n### Usage logging\n\nBreadth is only a discovery instrument if use is measured, so the logging code ships in\nWave 0.\n\n- `src/lib/usage.ts` records two events per model visit: `open` when a model renders, and\n  `engaged` with the seconds the tab was visible on it (capped at 30 minutes), sent with\n  `keepalive` when the page is hidden or left. Each row carries the model type, preset,\n  concept id when there is one, and a random per-tab session id. No account id, no free\n  text.\n- Rows go to a Supabase table `model_events`. The table and its row-level security\n  (anonymous insert only, no select, length and range checks) are defined in\n  `supabase/model_events.sql`, which the founder runs once. Until the table exists every\n  insert fails and is dropped.\n- A failed insert never reaches the student. In development it logs one console warning.\n  The coverage page (`#/coverage`) shows \"usage logging: on\" or \"usage logging: table\n  missing\", from a probe insert.\n\n`supabase/usage_report.sql` is the query the founder runs to read it: opens and\nreturning sessions per course, per week.\n\nTests: a unit test of the payload builder (seconds capped, unknown fields dropped) and\nthat a rejected insert does not throw.\n\n### Continuous integration\n\n`.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs install, `bun run test`, `bun run test:catalog`, lint and\nbuild on every pull request. If the push is refused because the token cannot write\nworkflow files, the file is left out of the PR and handed to the founder to add.\n\n### Catalog and mapping\n\n- `bun run gen:curriculum` runs `scripts/gen-models.mjs` and then\n  `src/data/curriculum/build.ts`. The build reads `concepts.gen.ts` and `meta.gen.ts`,\n  stamps `specType` into the generated subject JSON, and writes one compact catalog file\n  per subject, `src/data/curriculum/catalog/<subject>.json`. Catalog\n  and subject JSON are committed; the integrator regenerates them when a wave is merged.\n- Catalog order: courses follow an explicit ordered list of the 23 course slugs in a new\n  `src/data/curriculum/courseOrder.ts`; units follow each course's own `units` array;\n  concepts keep the order they have in the raw files. Each concept is\n  `{ id, title, model? }` where `model` is `[type, preset]`. A unit with no concepts is\n  left out of the catalog.\n- The 4 existing mappings move into the `concepts.ts` of `projectile`, `orbit-sim` and\n  `ray-diagram`. `specType` is removed from `raw/*.json`, and the build fails if a raw file\n  sets it. Mapping has one source.\n- The build applies the Goal's gate and prints the Goal's report. The exclusion list lives\n  in `src/data/curriculum/exclusions.ts`. The mapping checks are one pure function,\n  `validateMappings`, so they can be tested on small fixtures.\n\nTests for this section, in `registry.test.ts`: every mapped concept id exists in the\ncurriculum; no concept maps to two models; every mapped preset exists in that model's\n`meta.ts`. `validateMappings` is tested on fixtures for each failure it must catch\n(unknown concept, duplicate mapping, missing preset, raw `specType`, uncovered course,\nstale or unknown exclusion). The generator's complete-folder rule is tested in\n`scripts/gen-models.test.ts` (tests under `scripts/` may use node APIs; the runner\nexecutes them too). `bun run test:catalog` covers staleness.\n\n- `src/coverage/CurriculumCoverage.tsx` uses the real mapping as the base layer. A real\n  mapping wins and is read-only there; the localStorage planning layer applies only to\n  concepts with no real mapping.\n\n### Deep links\n\nThe page reloads on every hash change (`src/main.tsx`), so the hash is parsed once at load.\n\n| URL | Resolves to |\n|---|---|\n| `#/try` | `projectile`, its default preset (today's default); header shows the model label |\n| `#/try/<type>` | that model's default preset; header shows the model label |\n| `#/try/<type>/<preset>` | that preset; header shows the model label and preset label |\n| any of the above with `?c=<conceptId>`, id known and mapped | the `(type, preset)` that concept maps to (the concept wins over the path); header shows concept title, course and unit, and a link to the next mapped concept in the unit when one exists |\n| `?c=` with an unknown or unmapped id | `c` is ignored; resolved from the path |\n| known type, unknown preset | the type's default preset; header shows the model label |\n| unknown type | a \"No model called X\" notice with a link to topics; nothing is rendered in its place |\n\nThe header's concept data comes from `catalog/<subject>.json`, loaded lazily using the\nsubject prefix of the concept id. When `c` is present the stage shows its skeleton until\nthat catalog has loaded, then resolves. If the catalog fails to load, or the prefix is not\na subject, `c` is treated as unknown. \"Next mapped concept in the unit\" follows catalog order\nand skips unmapped concepts. The last mapped concept in a unit shows no next link.\n\nTests: a unit test of the pure resolver with one case per table row, and of the\nnext-in-unit lookup (middle of unit, last in unit, unknown id).\n\n### Topics page as a course browser\n\n- Reads the generated catalog (one lazy chunk per subject) instead of Supabase. The topics\n  page no longer depends on the database; the seed script keeps mirroring the curriculum\n  for relational use. The live-table column check added on this branch goes away with the\n  query it guarded.\n- Five subject tabs (adds Computing). Courses, units and concepts appear in catalog order.\n- Each mapped concept's \"Try →\" link is `#/try/<type>/<preset>?c=<conceptId>`.\n- Search box: case-insensitive title substring over the active subject.\n- \"Interactive only\" toggle: hides concepts with no model.\n- Search and toggle combine with AND. While either is active, units and courses with no\n  matching concept are hidden and courses with a match are expanded.\n- Empty states: with a non-empty query and no results, \"No concepts match\". With an empty\n  query, the toggle on and no results, \"No interactive models in this subject yet\".\n- Each course header shows \"N of M interactive\", counted over the whole course whatever\n  the search or toggle says.\n- If a subject's catalog fails to load, the panel shows the existing error line with a\n  retry button.\n\nTests: unit tests of the pure filter and count functions (match, no match, empty query,\nmixed case, toggle alone, toggle with search), and a browser check that walks two next\nhops through \"Forms of linear equations\".\n\n### First real mappings (so Wave 0 is verifiable end to end)\n\n`function-grapher` is brought to the full recipe (folder, presets, `PlotStage`, 375px\npass, `built:true`) with presets for the Algebra 1 concepts it shows faithfully: absolute\nvalue functions, graphing linear equations, slope of a line, slope-intercept form,\npoint-slope form, standard form of a line, x- and y-intercepts, graphing parabolas, vertex\nform of a quadratic, exponential functions, exponential growth and decay models. Its test\nasserts every preset passes `validateFunctionGrapher` (which tolerates asymptotes) and that\nthese eleven, which have none, are finite at every sample across their domain. The unit\n\"Forms of linear equations\" then has three mapped concepts in a row, which is what the\nnext-in-unit browser check walks.\n\n"},{"oldText":"## The catalog\n\nMapping rule, applied to every concept: map it only when the model shows THAT concept\nfaithfully. Precedent: `optical-instruments` (lens combinations) was left unmapped because\nthe single-lens widget cannot show a microscope. Unmapped concepts stay honest gaps.\n\n\"Gate\" below is the deterministic core each model's validator and tests rest on.\n\n### Wave 1: finish the designed vocabulary (7 models, completes the 15-type registry)\n\n| Model | Student manipulates | Gate | Concept families |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `unit-circle` | angle on the circle; sine/cosine/tangent traces unroll beside it | exact trig at special angles, identities | unit circle, radians, special angles, reference angles, Pythagorean identity, sine/cosine graphs |\n| `linear-transform` | the 2x2 (and 3x3) matrix entries; grid, basis vectors and unit square deform | determinant = signed area, eigenpairs from the characteristic polynomial, inverse exists iff det != 0 | matrices as transformations, determinant as area, eigenvectors, composition, inverses, rotation/reflection/projection |\n| `surface-3d` | z = f(x, y) with parameters; a probe point with tangent plane, gradient arrow, contour projection | mathjs evaluation on a sample grid, numeric partials vs symbolic, finite everywhere drawn | 3D graphs, level curves, partial derivatives, gradient, tangent planes, critical points and the second-derivative test |\n| `vector-field` | F(x, y) components; probe shows divergence and curl; drop a particle to trace a flow line; slope-field mode with Euler steps | numeric div/curl vs closed form on test fields, RK4 flow lines vs known solutions | vector fields, divergence, curl, line integrals and work, conservative fields, slope fields, Euler's method, phase plane |\n| `molecule-3d` | pick a molecule from a curated library; rotate; toggle lone pairs, bond dipoles, hybridization | VSEPR class from electron domains; ideal angles exact (109.47, 120, 180); net dipole by vector sum | VSEPR, Lewis structures, bond and molecular polarity, hybridization, intermolecular forces, chirality, conformations |\n| `reaction-energy` | activation energy, enthalpy change, temperature, catalyst on/off | Arrhenius rate ratio, delta-H = products minus reactants, catalyst lowers Ea and leaves delta-H alone | activation energy and collision theory, catalysis, enthalpy, enzymes and activation energy |\n| `step-walkthrough` | an input (array, tree, disk count); play/scrub through steps | steps are produced by RUNNING the real algorithm, never authored; invariants asserted per step | selection/insertion/merge/quick sort, linear and binary search, breadth-first search, Towers of Hanoi and recursion |\n\nAlso in Wave 1: `function-grapher` presets for the algebra, precalculus and function-graph\nconcepts it shows faithfully, and remapping physics concepts that the 7 existing models\nalready cover (free-body diagrams, friction, centripetal force, Hooke's law, Ohm's law,\nseries/parallel, Kirchhoff, universal gravitation, escape velocity).\n\n### Wave 2: calculus and geometry (10 models)\n\n| Model | Student manipulates | Gate |\n|---|---|---|\n| `derivative-explorer` | point and step h on f(x); secant collapses to tangent; f' drawn beneath | numeric derivative vs mathjs symbolic derivative |\n| `integral-explorer` | bounds and rectangle count; left/right/mid/trapezoid; accumulation function | Riemann sum converges to the closed-form integral on test functions |\n| `taylor-series` | centre and order; polynomial hugs the function; error band | coefficients vs symbolic derivatives; remainder bound holds |\n| `solid-of-revolution` | region, axis, disk/washer/shell slicing in 3D | numeric volume vs closed form (cone, sphere, paraboloid) |\n| `solid-geometry` | dimensions of prism, cylinder, cone, pyramid, sphere; cross-section plane | volume and surface-area formulas; Cavalieri equality |\n| `conic-sections` | tilt of a plane through a double cone; the cut curve and its equation | eccentricity from the tilt; focus-directrix property holds |\n| `transformations-2d` | rotate, reflect, translate, dilate a figure; compose two | matrix form of each transform; distances preserved for rigid motions |\n| `triangle-solver` | sides and angles; right-triangle ratios, law of sines/cosines, the SSA ambiguous case | angle sum, law-of-cosines consistency, 0/1/2 solution count for SSA |\n| `parametric-polar` | parametric and polar curves, a moving point with velocity vector; complex-plane mode | arc length and speed vs closed form; polar/rectangular round-trip |\n| `vectors-3d` | two or three vectors: sum, dot, cross, projection, span | dot/cross identities, projection orthogonality |\n\n### Wave 3: finish physics (13 models)\n\n`kinematics-1d` (linked x-t / v-t / a-t graphs and a moving cart), `collisions`\n(1D and 2D, elastic to perfectly inelastic; momentum conserved, KE conserved only when\nelastic), `energy-conservation` (pendulum and track with KE/PE bars), `torque-balance`\n(beam in static equilibrium), `rotational-dynamics` (rolling bodies, moment of inertia,\nangular momentum), `buoyancy` (Archimedes), `fluid-flow` (continuity and Bernoulli),\n`wave-superposition` (travelling, standing, interference, beats, Doppler),\n`electric-field` (point charges: field, potential, Coulomb force, dipoles),\n`magnetic-force` (charge in a B field; field of a wire), `refraction` (Snell's law and\ntotal internal reflection), `double-slit` (interference and diffraction pattern),\n`ideal-gas` (particles in a box; PV = nRT; PV-diagram processes), plus `rc-circuit`,\n`atomic-energy-levels` (Bohr transitions, photoelectric effect) and `nuclear-decay`\n(half-life) if their gates hold up in planning. Each has a closed-form gate from the\nstandard result named in parentheses.\n\n### Wave 4: chemistry (7 models, on top of `molecule-3d` and `reaction-energy`)\n\n`atom-model` (shells, electron configuration, orbital shapes, isotopes and ions),\n`titration` (pH curve, buffers, weak-acid equilibria from Ka), `equilibrium`\n(Q vs K, Le Chatelier shifts), `crystal-lattice` (ionic lattice and SC/BCC/FCC unit\ncells; packing fractions exact), `galvanic-cell` (cell potential, Nernst),\n`states-of-matter` (heating curve with a particle view), `stoichiometry`\n(mole ratios, limiting reactant by exact rational arithmetic). `ideal-gas` and\n`nuclear-decay` are shared with physics.\n\n### Wave 5: biology (9 models)\n\n`cell-explorer` (3D animal/plant/prokaryote cell, selectable organelles),\n`membrane-transport` (diffusion, osmosis and tonicity, active transport),\n`dna-central-dogma` (double helix, replication, transcription, translation with the\nstandard 64-codon table), `cell-division` (mitosis and meiosis stages; chromosome counts\nconserved or halved exactly), `genetics` (Punnett squares by enumeration; Hardy-Weinberg),\n`population-growth` (exponential, logistic, predator-prey), `enzyme-kinetics`\n(Michaelis-Menten, inhibition), `energy-pathways` (photosynthesis and respiration on the\n`Stepper` kit with curated, cited steps), `natural-selection` (seeded population sim;\nallele frequencies against the deterministic expectation).\n\n### Wave 6: statistics and computing (6 models)\n\n`distribution` (normal curve, z-scores, areas; binomial), `sampling` (central limit\ntheorem simulation, seeded), `regression` (least-squares line, residuals, r), `binary-\nnumbers` (bits, bytes, base conversion), `graph-explorer` (adjacency list/matrix,\nBFS/DFS on the `Stepper` kit), `logic-gates` (boolean expressions and truth tables).\n\nTotal: 52 new models across six waves, on top of the 8 that exist.\n\n","newText":"## The catalog\n\nMapping rule, applied to every concept: map it only when the model shows THAT concept\nfaithfully. Precedent: `optical-instruments` (lens combinations) was left unmapped because\nthe single-lens widget cannot show a microscope. Unmapped concepts stay honest gaps.\n\nConcept ids are assigned before a wave starts, not by the model builders. The integrator\nwrites each new model's `concepts.ts` (concept id to preset id) before fan-out, so two\nmodels never claim one concept. Tie-break when two could: the concept goes to the model\nwhose primary interaction is that concept. The \"Concept families\" column below is the\ninput to that assignment, not the assignment itself.\n\n\"Gate\" is the deterministic core each model's validator and tests rest on. \"Stage\" is the\nrecipe's stage kind. \"Eq / WP\" says whether the model has an equation set and a\nword-problem parser.\n\n### Wave 1: finish the designed vocabulary (7 models, completes the original registry)\n\n| Model | Subject | Stage | Student manipulates | Gate | Eq / WP | Concept families |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `unit-circle` | math | board | an angle on the circle; sine, cosine and tangent traces unroll beside it | exact trig values at special angles; sin^2 + cos^2 = 1 | no / no | unit circle, radians, special angles, reference angles, Pythagorean identity, graphs of sine and cosine |\n| `linear-transform` | math | plot | the four entries of a 2x2 matrix; grid, basis vectors and unit square deform | determinant = signed area; eigenpairs from the characteristic polynomial; inverse exists iff det != 0 | no / no | matrices as transformations, determinant as area, eigenvectors, composition, inverses, rotation/reflection/projection matrices |\n| `surface-3d` | math | 3D | z = f(x, y) with parameters; a probe point with tangent plane, gradient arrow and contour projection | mathjs evaluation on a sample grid; numeric partials vs symbolic; finite everywhere drawn | no / no | 3D graphs, level curves, partial derivatives, gradient, tangent planes, multivariable critical points and the second-derivative test |\n| `vector-field` | math | plot | the two components of F(x, y); a probe showing divergence and scalar curl; a dropped particle tracing a flow line | numeric divergence and curl vs closed form on test fields; RK4 flow lines vs known solutions | no / no | vector fields, 2D curl and 2D divergence, divergence of a vector field |\n| `molecule-3d` | chemistry | 3D | a molecule from a curated library; rotate; toggle lone pairs, bond dipoles, hybridization labels | VSEPR class from electron domains; ideal angles exact (109.47, 120, 180); net dipole by vector sum; measured angles stored and labelled as measured | no / no | VSEPR, Lewis structures, bond and molecular polarity, hybridization, intermolecular forces |\n| `reaction-energy` | chemistry, biology | plot | activation energy, enthalpy change, temperature, catalyst on/off | Arrhenius rate ratio; delta-H = products minus reactants; a catalyst lowers Ea and leaves delta-H alone | yes / yes | activation energy and collision theory, catalysis, enthalpy changes, enzymes and activation energy |\n| `step-walkthrough` | computing | board with `Stepper` | an input array or disk count; play and scrub through the steps | steps come from RUNNING the real algorithm, never authored; invariants asserted per step (sorted prefix, search window contains the target, legal Hanoi moves) | no / no | selection, insertion, merge and quick sort; linear and binary search; Towers of Hanoi |\n\nAlso in Wave 1: `function-grapher` presets for the Algebra 2, precalculus and trigonometry\ngraph concepts it shows faithfully (the integrator's concept assignment lists them), and\nthe extensions below.\n\n### Existing models: extensions and remapping (Wave 1)\n\nA concept is remapped onto an existing model only when the model, with its extension where\none is listed, shows that concept. Each extension carries assertions in that model's test\nfile. New spec fields are optional with defaults, so the existing schema branches and\nprompt text in `src/engine/spec.ts`, and `api/spec.ts`, stay untouched.\n\n| Existing model | Extension | Concepts it then serves |\n|---|---|---|\n| `circular-motion` | optional mass, and the equation F = m v^2 / r | centripetal force and acceleration; uniform circular motion |\n| `wave-oscillator` | the equation F = k x beside the existing period and energy equations | Hooke's law and springs; simple harmonic motion |\n| `circuit-diagram` | optional named network preset (single loop, series pair, parallel pair) that seeds the existing builder; test asserts Kirchhoff's current and voltage laws hold in the solver's result | Ohm's law and resistance; series and parallel circuits; Kirchhoff's circuit laws |\n| `free-body-diagram` | optional static coefficient (defaults to the existing `friction` field, which stays the kinetic coefficient, so current specs behave exactly as before); optional applied force F along the surface; a flat-surface preset. The block holds while abs(m g sin(theta) - F) is at most mu_s m g cos(theta); otherwise it slides with kinetic friction opposing the motion | forces on an inclined plane; friction (static and kinetic); free-body diagrams |\n| `orbit-sim` | optional orbiting mass and the readouts F = M m / r^2 and U = -M m / r in its natural units | Newton's law of universal gravitation; gravitational potential energy and escape velocity |\n\n`projectile` and `ray-diagram` keep their current mappings. These five extensions edit the\nshared legacy files, so one agent does them in sequence; they are not part of the parallel\nfan-out.\n\n### Wave 2: calculus and geometry (11 models)\n\n| Model | Subject | Stage | Student manipulates | Gate | Eq / WP | Concept families |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `derivative-explorer` | math | plot | a point and a step h on f(x); the secant collapses to the tangent; f' is drawn beneath | numeric derivative vs mathjs symbolic derivative | no / no | derivative as a limit and as a function, average vs instantaneous rate, linear approximation, critical points, concavity, second-derivative test, mean value theorem |\n| `integral-explorer` | math | plot | bounds and rectangle count; left, right, midpoint, trapezoid; the accumulation function | Riemann sums converge to the closed-form integral on test functions | no / no | Riemann sums, the definite integral, fundamental theorem of calculus, accumulation functions, area between curves |\n| `taylor-series` | math | plot | centre and order; the polynomial hugs the function; an error band | coefficients vs symbolic derivatives; the Lagrange remainder bound holds | no / no | Taylor and Maclaurin series, Maclaurin series of common functions, Taylor error bounds |\n| `slope-field` | math | plot | dy/dx = f(x, y); an initial point; Euler step size | Euler's method vs the exact solution on separable test equations; error shrinks linearly with step | no / no | slope fields, Euler's method, step size and error, autonomous equations and equilibria, the logistic differential equation |\n| `solid-of-revolution` | math | 3D | a region and an axis; disk, washer or shell slices | numeric volume vs closed form (cone, sphere, paraboloid) | no / no | volumes of revolution, cylindrical shells, known cross sections, cross-sections and solids of revolution |\n| `solid-geometry` | math, biology | 3D | the dimensions of a prism, cylinder, cone, pyramid or sphere; a cross-section plane | volume and surface-area formulas; Cavalieri equality | yes / yes | volume and surface area of solids, Cavalieri's principle, scale factor and area/volume ratios, cell size and surface-area-to-volume ratio |\n| `conic-sections` | math | 3D | the tilt of a plane through a double cone; the cut curve and its equation | eccentricity from the tilt; the focus-directrix property holds on sampled points | no / no | conic sections from a cone, ellipses, hyperbolas, parabolas, eccentricity |\n| `transformations-2d` | math | board | rotate, reflect, translate or dilate a figure; compose two | matrix form of each transform; distances preserved exactly for rigid motions | no / no | translations, reflections, rotations, dilations, rigid transformations, composing transformations, symmetry |\n| `triangle-solver` | math | board | sides and angles of a triangle | angle sum; law-of-cosines consistency; 0, 1 or 2 solutions for SSA | yes / yes | Pythagorean theorem, SOH-CAH-TOA, solving right triangles, special right triangles, law of sines, law of cosines, the ambiguous case, area by trig |\n| `parametric-polar` | math | plot | a parametric or polar curve; a moving point with its velocity vector; a complex-plane preset | arc length and speed vs closed form; polar/rectangular round trip | no / no | parametric equations, polar coordinates, polar/Cartesian conversion, vector-valued functions and motion, the complex plane, polar form, De Moivre's theorem |\n| `vectors-3d` | math, physics | 3D | two or three vectors: sum, dot, cross, projection, span | dot and cross identities; projection residual is orthogonal | no / no | vectors and operations, magnitude and unit vectors, dot product, cross product, linear combinations and span, vectors and components |\n\n### Wave 3: finish physics (20 models)\n\n| Model | Stage | Student manipulates | Gate | Eq / WP | Concept families |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `kinematics-1d` | plot | initial position, velocity, acceleration; a cart moves under linked x-t, v-t, a-t graphs | x = x0 + v0 t + a t^2 / 2; v = v0 + a t | yes / yes | position, velocity and acceleration; motion graphs; kinematic equations; free fall |\n| `collisions` | 3D | two masses, their velocities, the restitution coefficient | momentum conserved for every restitution; kinetic energy conserved only when elastic | yes / yes | conservation of momentum, elastic and inelastic collisions, momentum and impulse, centre of mass |\n| `energy-conservation` | 3D | release height on a track or pendulum; mass; friction | speed from height, v = sqrt(2 g (h0 - h)); energy lost equals friction work | yes / yes | conservation of mechanical energy, kinetic and potential energy, work-energy theorem, energy in oscillators |\n| `torque-balance` | 3D | weights and their positions on a pivoted beam | net torque and net force both zero at balance | yes / yes | torque, static equilibrium and torque balance |\n| `rolling-motion` | 3D | shape (hoop, disk, sphere), mass, radius, incline angle | a = g sin(theta) / (1 + I / (m r^2)); translational plus rotational kinetic energy equals the potential energy lost | yes / yes | moment of inertia, Newton's second law for rotation, rotational kinetic energy and rolling |\n| `angular-momentum` | 3D | a spinning platform; move masses in or out | I1 w1 = I2 w2; rotational kinetic energy changes by the work done moving the masses | yes / yes | angular momentum and its conservation, angular kinematics |\n| `buoyancy` | 3D | object density and volume, fluid density | buoyant force = fluid density x g x submerged volume; floating fraction = density ratio | yes / yes | buoyancy and Archimedes' principle, fluid density and pressure |\n| `fluid-flow` | 3D | pipe areas and heights, inlet speed | A1 v1 = A2 v2; P + rho v^2 / 2 + rho g h constant | yes / yes | continuity and Bernoulli's principle |\n| `wave-superposition` | plot | amplitude, frequency and phase of two waves; their sum | nodes at half-wavelength spacing; beat frequency = abs(f1 - f2) | yes / no | wave properties, the travelling wave equation, interference and superposition, standing waves, beats |\n| `doppler` | plot | source and observer speeds | f' = f (v + v_o) / (v - v_s) with signed speeds | yes / yes | Doppler effect |\n| `electric-field` | plot | point charges placed on a plane; a test charge | E = k q / r^2 by superposition; V = k q / r | yes / yes | electric charge and Coulomb's law, electric fields, electric potential, dipoles and superposition |\n| `magnetic-force` | 3D | charge, speed, field strength and angle | F = q v B sin(theta); r = m v / (q B) | yes / yes | magnetic force on moving charges |\n| `magnetic-field-wire` | 3D | current and distance from a straight wire | B = mu0 I / (2 pi r); direction by the right-hand rule | yes / yes | magnetic fields from currents, electromagnetism |\n| `refraction` | plot | incident angle and the two refractive indices | n1 sin(t1) = n2 sin(t2); critical angle = asin(n2 / n1) | yes / yes | refraction and Snell's law |\n| `double-slit` | plot | slit separation, wavelength, screen distance | bright fringes at d sin(theta) = m lambda | yes / yes | interference and diffraction of light |\n| `ideal-gas` | 3D with `ParticleBox` | pressure, volume, temperature, amount; a path on the PV diagram | P V = n R T; v_rms = sqrt(3 R T / M) | yes / yes | kinetic theory and the ideal gas law, PV diagrams, gas laws, kinetic molecular theory |\n| `rc-circuit` | plot | resistance, capacitance, source voltage; charge or discharge | charging V0 (1 - exp(-t / RC)); discharging V0 exp(-t / RC); time constant = R C | yes / yes | RC circuits |\n| `atomic-energy-levels` | board | initial and final level of a hydrogen atom | E_n = -13.6 eV / n^2; photon energy = h c / lambda | yes / yes | atomic energy levels and spectra |\n| `photoelectric-effect` | board | light frequency and intensity; the metal's work function | KE_max = h f - phi; no electrons below the threshold frequency at any intensity | yes / yes | the photoelectric effect and photons |\n| `nuclear-decay` | plot | half-life and elapsed time | N = N0 / 2^(t / T) | yes / yes | radioactive decay and half-life, nuclear decay |\n\n`rc-circuit` is a separate model with a closed-form gate. The circuit builder's solver is\nsteady-state DC and is not extended to transients.\n\n### Wave 4: chemistry (10 models, on top of `molecule-3d` and `reaction-energy`)\n\n| Model | Stage | Student manipulates | Gate | Eq / WP | Concept families |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `atom-model` | 3D | element; isotope; ion charge | shell capacities 2 n^2; configuration by filling order with the documented exceptions held in data; average atomic mass = weighted sum | no / no | atomic structure, electron configuration, atomic orbitals, ion formation, isotopes and average atomic mass |\n| `titration` | plot | acid and base strength, concentrations, volume added | pH from the exact charge-balance equation; equivalence at equal moles; Henderson-Hasselbalch inside the buffer region | yes / yes | acids, bases and the pH scale; buffers and titration curves; weak acid-base equilibria |\n| `equilibrium` | plot | initial concentrations, K, a stress (add, remove, compress) | ICE table solved exactly; direction of shift from Q against K | yes / no | chemical equilibrium and Le Chatelier's principle, equilibrium constant and reaction quotient |\n| `crystal-lattice` | 3D | lattice type (simple cubic, body-centred, face-centred, rock salt) | atoms per cell 1, 2, 4; packing fractions pi/6, pi sqrt(3)/8, pi sqrt(2)/6; rock salt has 4 formula units per cell and 6:6 coordination | no / no | types of solids and crystal lattices, ionic bonding and lattices, metallic bonding |\n| `galvanic-cell` | board | two half-cells from a curated table; concentrations | cell potential = cathode minus anode; Nernst equation | yes / yes | galvanic and electrolytic cells; cell potential and the Nernst equation |\n| `states-of-matter` | plot with `ParticleBox` | heat added to a sample | q = m c dT on the slopes; q = m L on the plateaus | yes / yes | states of matter and phase changes |\n| `stoichiometry` | board | a balanced reaction and starting amounts | atoms balance; limiting reactant by exact rational arithmetic | no / yes | balancing chemical equations, stoichiometry and limiting reactants, the mole |\n| `stereochemistry` | 3D | a chiral molecule from a curated set beside its mirror image; swap two substituents | R or S from priorities computed by atomic number for the curated set; the mirror image and any single swap flip it | no / no | chirality and stereoisomers, optical activity and Fischer projections |\n| `conformations` | 3D | the dihedral angle of ethane or butane, seen as a Newman projection, with its energy curve | energy curve has period 120 degrees for ethane; staggered minima and eclipsed maxima; butane anti below gauche; barrier values curated and labelled approximate | no / no | conformational analysis and Newman projections |\n| `reaction-mechanism` | board with `Stepper` | a mechanism (SN2, SN1, E2, E1, electrophilic addition); step through it | atoms and total charge conserved at every step; SN2 inverts the stereocentre and SN1 gives both; the rate law's order matches the mechanism | no / no | SN1 and SN2 reactions, E1 and E2 elimination, electrophilic addition to alkenes |\n\n`ideal-gas` and `nuclear-decay` (Wave 3) list chemistry as a second subject. Organic\nchemistry's spectroscopy, nomenclature, aromaticity and carbonyl-chemistry concepts stay\nunmapped: no model here shows them faithfully.\n\n### Wave 5: biology (11 models)\n\n| Model | Stage | Student manipulates | Gate | Eq / WP | Concept families |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `cell-explorer` | 3D | cell type (animal, plant, prokaryote); a selected organelle | curated organelle sets per cell type, with invariants (a prokaryote has no nucleus; only the plant cell has a wall, chloroplasts and a central vacuole) | no / no | eukaryotic cell organelles, organelle structure and function, cell theory and cell types |\n| `membrane-transport` | board with `ParticleBox` | solute concentrations on each side; transport mode | net flux runs down the gradient and is zero at equal concentration; water moves toward higher solute | no / no | diffusion and osmosis, active transport, membrane transport mechanisms, water potential and tonicity |\n| `dna-structure` | 3D | a base sequence; unzip to replicate | A pairs with T and G with C; strands antiparallel; each daughter keeps one parent strand | no / no | DNA structure, nucleic acid structure, DNA replication |\n| `gene-expression` | board with `Stepper` | a DNA sequence; a point or frameshift mutation | transcription by complement with U for T; translation by the standard 64-codon table; start and stop codons | no / no | transcription and translation, the genetic code, mutations |\n| `cell-division` | board with `Stepper` | mitosis or meiosis; step through the stages | chromosome and chromatid counts per stage: mitosis 2n to 2n, meiosis 2n to n | no / no | mitosis, meiosis, the cell cycle, meiosis and genetic diversity |\n| `genetics` | board | parent genotypes for one or two genes; dominance pattern | Punnett ratios by enumerating gametes | no / yes | Mendelian genetics and Punnett squares, non-Mendelian inheritance, sex linkage |\n| `hardy-weinberg` | plot | allele frequency p | p^2 + 2 p q + q^2 = 1; genotype frequencies from p | yes / yes | Hardy-Weinberg equilibrium |\n| `population-growth` | plot | growth rate, carrying capacity, predator and prey parameters | exponential and logistic closed forms; predator-prey by RK4 with its conserved quantity checked | yes / yes | population growth, population ecology and growth models, logistic growth models |\n| `enzyme-kinetics` | plot | substrate concentration, Vmax, Km, inhibitor type | v = Vmax S / (Km + S); a competitive inhibitor raises apparent Km, a noncompetitive one lowers Vmax | yes / no | enzyme kinetics and regulation |\n| `energy-pathways` | board with `Stepper` | a pathway; step through it | curated steps whose ATP, NADH and FADH2 tallies must sum to the stated net yields | no / no | glycolysis, the Krebs cycle, electron transport, cellular respiration, photosynthesis, the Calvin cycle |\n| `natural-selection` | plot | selection strength, starting allele frequency, population size | the deterministic expectation for allele frequency per generation; the seeded simulation's mean tracks it | no / no | natural selection, mechanisms of natural selection |\n\n### Wave 6: statistics and computing (6 models)\n\n| Model | Subject | Stage | Student manipulates | Gate | Eq / WP | Concept families |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `distribution` | math | plot | mean, standard deviation, a z interval; a binomial preset | normal areas 68.27, 95.45, 99.73 percent; binomial probabilities sum to 1 | yes / yes | the normal distribution and the empirical rule, z-scores, normal probability calculations, binomial distribution |\n| `sampling` | math | plot | sample size, population shape; draw samples (seeded) | standard error = sigma / sqrt(n) | yes / no | sampling distributions, central limit theorem |\n| `regression` | math | plot | draggable data points | least-squares slope = r s_y / s_x; residuals sum to zero | no / no | scatterplots and correlation, least-squares regression line, residuals and R-squared |\n| `binary-numbers` | computing | board | a number; toggle bits | base conversion round trip across binary, decimal and hexadecimal | no / no | binary numbers, bits and bytes |\n| `graph-explorer` | computing | board with `Stepper` | a small graph; a start node | breadth-first search visits nodes in non-decreasing distance; adjacency list and matrix describe the same graph | no / no | graph representation, breadth-first search |\n| `logic-gates` | computing | board | a boolean expression | truth table by exhaustive evaluation; De Morgan equivalences hold | no / no | boolean logic and expressions |\n\nTotal: 65 new models across six waves (7, 11, 20, 10, 11, 6), on top of the 8 that exist:\n73.\n\nPermanent exclusion: the \"Computer programming\" course (JavaScript drawing, HTML/CSS and\nSQL). Its concepts are language syntax and tooling; no model here shows them faithfully.\nEvery other course gets at least one mapped concept by the wave named in its pending entry.\n\n"},{"oldText":"## Order and delivery\n\n- Wave 0 ships first, alone, as its own PR. Nothing else starts until it is merged, because\n  every later model depends on the folder shape and the registry.\n- Each later wave is one PR and one version bump (the existing per-feature cadence:\n  0.3.4.0, 0.3.5.0, ...), through `/review` then `/ship`.\n- Inside a wave the models are independent folders, so they are built in parallel (one\n  agent per model, each in its own git worktree), then integrated and verified together.\n  The only shared-file touches per model are one line in `registry.ts` and one entry in\n  `specTypes.ts`.\n- Waves run in the order above. The work can stop cleanly at any wave boundary with\n  everything shipped up to that point.\n\n","newText":"## Order and delivery\n\n- Wave 0 ships first, alone, as its own PR and version (0.3.4.0). Nothing else starts\n  until it is merged, because every later model depends on the folder shape, the generator\n  and the catalog.\n- No PR carries more than seven new models, so one person can review it. Wave 1 is one\n  PR; Wave 2 is two (6 and 5); Wave 3 is three (7, 7, 6); Wave 4 is two (5 and 5); Wave 5\n  is two (6 and 5); Wave 6 is one. Each PR is one version bump (0.3.5.0 onward), through\n  `/review` then `/ship`.\n- After Wave 1 the per-model and per-wave estimates are replaced with what Wave 1\n  actually took, before Wave 2 starts.\n- When the plan is approved, a dated note is added to `docs/DESIGN.md` recording the\n  founder's decision to build the prebuilt model library past the original ~12 types, so\n  the approved design and this plan do not contradict each other.\n- Before a wave's fan-out the integrator builds any kit the wave first needs, adds the\n  unit families it needs, and creates each new model's folder with its `concepts.ts`\n  filled in.\n- Inside a wave the new models are built in parallel (one agent per model, each in its own\n  git worktree). A new model's branch touches only its own folder and runs its own test\n  file, `registry.test.ts`, build, lint and the sweep for its model. Two things are not part\n  of the fan-out and are done by one agent in sequence: kits (`src/engine/kits/`) and\n  extensions to the existing 8 models, whose code lives in shared files.\n- The integrator merges the folders, deletes the exclusion entries the wave satisfied,\n  runs `bun run gen:curriculum`, then runs `bun run test`, `bun run test:catalog`, build,\n  lint and the full sweep on the combined tree.\n- Waves run in the order above. The work can stop cleanly at any wave boundary with\n  everything shipped up to that point. How many waves this run covers is the founder's\n  decision at plan approval.\n\n"},{"oldText":"## Effort\n\n| | Human team | CC + gstack |\n|---|---|---|\n| Wave 0 | ~1 week | ~2 hours |\n| One model, to the recipe | ~2 to 3 days | ~30 to 45 min |\n| Wave 1 (7 models + remapping) | ~4 weeks | ~3 to 4 hours |\n| All six waves (52 models) | ~6 to 8 months | ~25 to 35 hours, across several sessions |\n\n","newText":"## Effort\n\nAgent time is the sum of work across parallel agents; wall-clock is elapsed time with four\nto six models in flight at once.\n\n| | Human team | CC + gstack agent time | CC + gstack wall-clock |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Wave 0 | ~3 weeks | ~6 to 8 hours | ~6 to 8 hours |\n| One model, to the recipe, with the second agent's check | ~2 to 3 days | ~45 to 75 min | n/a |\n| Integration, sweep and hands-on pass, per wave | ~3 days | ~1.5 to 3 hours | ~1.5 to 3 hours |\n| Wave 1 (7 models, presets, 5 extensions) | ~5 weeks | ~9 to 13 hours | ~4 to 6 hours |\n| All six waves (65 models, 11 PRs) | ~9 to 11 months | ~70 to 110 hours | ~30 to 45 hours, across several sessions |\n\n"},{"oldText":"## Risks\n\n1. **Factual content outside physics.** Bond angles, standard potentials, the codon table\n   and pathway steps come from reference knowledge, not from a derivation. The registry invariant tests and the per-model closed-form tests are what\n   keep quality from decaying as the count grows.\n3. **Simulated models** (`ParticleBox`, `natural-selection`, `sampling`) can show a picture\n   that disagrees with the number beside it. Each such table gets cross-checks in tests where one exists (64\n   codons, angle sums, charge balance, net yields), is checked against its cited source by\n   a second agent, and is listed in the PR for a founder spot-check.\n2. **Depth against breadth.** 65 models at the physics-model bar is a lot of surface to\n   keep correct. The registry tests, the per-model closed-form tests and the sweep are what\n   keep quality from decaying as the count grows.\n3. **Simulated models** (`ParticleBox`, `natural-selection`, `sampling`) can show a picture\n   that disagrees with the number beside it. ### CEO phase: Step 0 (mode: SELECTIVE EXPANSION, set by /autoplan)

Review depth: implementation-ready. Storage: this file (plan + report), CEO summary in
`~/.gstack/projects/patelmemail0-sys-Saras/ceo-plans/2026-10-01-model-buildout.md`.

#### System audit

- Branch `feat/model-buildout` is `origin/main` (58fc6ad) plus an uncommitted topics fix
  (5 files). No stashes, no open PRs, no TODOS.md, no TODO/FIXME markers in source.
- Hot files over the project's life: `VisualizePanel.tsx` (6 touches), `validate.ts` (5),
  `spec.ts` (4), `visualize.css` (7). These are exactly the shared files every new model
  edits today, which is the evidence for Wave 0.
- Retrospective: the last three ships each added models by editing the same six shared
  files and each needed a follow-up fix commit (b093f24 canvas height, f0bce57 adversarial
  hardening). Prior learnings applied: `r3f-canvas-half-height-stacked-layout`
  (confidence 6/10, 2026-06-29) and `supabase-concepts-spec-type-stale` (9/10, 2026-10-01).
- Taste calibration. Good references: `src/engine/widgets/RaySim.tsx` (one closed-form
  source shared by gate and renderer, clamped drawing with true readout, 2D fallback from
  the same maths) and `src/engine/useEquationPanel.ts` (generic over the equation set, no
  per-widget solver code). Pattern to avoid: `VisualizePanel.tsx` lines 11 to 46 and 127 to
  147 and 278 to 297, three parallel hand-maintained lists (imports, `MODEL_TYPES`,
  `validate` switch, `renderModel` switch) that must agree.
- UI scope: yes (new widgets, topics page changes, Try page header). Section 11 runs.

#### Landscape (WebSearch; Aside unavailable)

- Layer 1 (tried and true): PhET, Desmos and GeoGebra are the free, trusted simulation
  libraries. They are concept-generic and hand-built, one sim per topic, and PhET added
  AI-guided inquiry in 2026.
- Layer 2 (new): ChatGPT ships interactive visuals for 70+ math and science concepts
  (Pythagorean theorem, Charles' law, Coulomb's law, Hooke's law, Ohm's law, exponential
  decay, linear equations) to every logged-in user, and says more are coming.
- Layer 3 (first principles): a 73-model library does not beat PhET on library size or
  ChatGPT on reach. What neither has is the same idea shown as an explorable model tied to
  the equation and to the student's own word problem, across a whole course sequence, with
  a correctness gate. Breadth matters here as coverage of a course (a student can stay in
  the tool for a unit), not as a count.

#### 0A. Premise challenge

- Real problem: the topics page promises "Every concept, three ways" and 4 of 734 concepts
  open anything. A student in any course outside one corner of physics hits a dead end.
- Target outcome: a student in any listed course finds working models for the concepts in
  their current unit.
- Do-nothing cost: the browse spine stays empty, and the DESIGN.md validation step (put
  it in front of students, see which subject pulls repeat use) cannot run, because breadth
  is the stated discovery instrument and there is no breadth.
- Direct or proxy: direct for the browse gap. It is a proxy for the DESIGN.md thesis
  (three representations, the student's exact problem), which this plan does not advance.

Premises, with disposition:

| # | Premise | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| P1 | "All the models" means template models covering concept families, not one bespoke model per concept | Accepted. It is the DESIGN.md architecture (spec types cover families). |
| P2 | Building 65 models is the right next step even though DESIGN.md says validate first | Founder's stated direction. Not auto-decided; carried to the final gate with the cost of being wrong. |
| P3 | Every model needs the full physics-model depth | Accepted with the recipe's own qualifiers: equation set only when equation-shaped, parser only when problem-shaped. |
| P4 | The model channel alone is worth shipping without analogy and walkthrough | Accepted for this plan; the other channels are named as not in scope. |
| P5 | Breadth works as a discovery instrument | Only if usage is measured. The plan has no instrumentation. See proposal E1. |

#### 0B. Existing code leverage

| Sub-problem | Existing code | Reuse |
|---|---|---|
| 3D stage, look, camera, reduced motion | `widgets/three/Scene3D.tsx`, `materials.ts`, `primitives.tsx`, `useReducedMotion.ts`, `hasWebGL.ts` | As is |
| Solve-for-any-variable panel, units | `useEquationPanel.ts`, `EquationPanel.tsx`, `equations.ts` (`solveFor`), `units.ts` | As is |
| Word-problem input row and parser shape | `WordProblemBar.tsx`, `wordProblems.ts` (`ParsedProblem`) | As is |
| 2D plot of y = f(x) with sliders | `widgets/FunctionGrapher.tsx`, `validateFunctionGrapher` (mathjs) | `PlotStage` is extracted from this, not written fresh |
| RC circuits | `circuitNetwork.ts` (MNA solver) is steady-state DC only | Not reusable for transients. `rc-circuit` is a separate closed-form model (corrected after spec review) |
| Curriculum validation and generated JSON | `src/data/curriculum/build.ts`, `specTypes.ts` | Extended to stamp mappings and write `modelMap.json` |
| Browse and deep link | `src/topics/TopicsPage.tsx`, `src/topics/concepts.ts`, `modelFromHash` in `VisualizePanel.tsx` | Extended |
| Planning view of coverage | `src/coverage/CurriculumCoverage.tsx` | Reads generated `specType`, so it reflects new mappings with no change |

Nothing is rebuilt. The founder's separate Cytonaut project (a three.js cell explorer) is
a reference for `cell-explorer` geometry, not a dependency.

#### 0C. Dream state

```
CURRENT STATE                 THIS PLAN                      12-MONTH IDEAL
8 models, physics only.       73 models across 5 subjects.   Each concept opens up to 3
4 of 734 concepts mapped.     Roughly 250 to 300 of 734          distinct representations.
Adding a model edits 6        concepts mapped (the ones a    The student's own worksheet
shared files. Topics page     model shows faithfully). A     problem drives the model.
is a list with 4 live         model is one folder. Topics    Usage data shows which course
links.                        page is a working course       pulls repeat use. Students
                              browser.                       return unprompted.
```

The plan moves toward the ideal on breadth and on engineering shape. It does not move on
the second and third representation, on instrumentation, or on the problem-from-photo
input.

#### 0D and 0E

- Approach options considered: A) this plan (foundation plus family-template models in
  waves), B) finish only the 7 registry types, C) generate a per-concept visual with an
  LLM. C is the approach DESIGN.md rejected (unverifiable correctness). B covers math and
  chemistry partly and leaves biology with a generic stepper. A is the request. Selected:
  A (highest completeness, P1). Note: options differ in kind, not coverage.
- Mode: SELECTIVE EXPANSION (fixed by /autoplan). The plain 0E rule would recommend SCOPE
  REDUCTION because the plan changes far more than 15 files; that tension is carried to
  the final gate as the sequencing choice, not resolved here.

#### 0F and 0G. Scope proposals (cherry-pick ceremony, auto-decided)

HOLD checks first. Complexity: the plan's moving parts are already consolidated into three
kits and one registry; no simpler shape reaches the goal. Deferrable without blocking the
goal: nothing in Wave 0 (each item removes a per-model cost or a correctness hole).

| # | Proposal | Effort (human / CC) | Risk | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | Usage instrumentation: record model opened, preset, seconds engaged | M (2 days / 1 hour) | med | DEFERRED, surfaced as a taste call | Makes breadth a discovery instrument (DESIGN.md P2), but needs a new Supabase table that only the founder can create. New infra, outside the blast radius. |
| E2 | Try page shows the concept's title, course and unit, with a link to the next mapped concept in the unit | S (4 hours / 20 min) | low | ACCEPTED | In blast radius (`VisualizePanel.tsx`, `modelMap.json`). Turns 73 isolated demos into a course a student can walk. |
| E3 | Topics page search box filtering concepts by title | S (3 hours / 15 min) | low | ACCEPTED | In blast radius (`TopicsPage.tsx`). 734 chips are not browsable by eye. |
| E4 | Topics page "interactive only" toggle and "N of M interactive" count per course | S (3 hours / 15 min) | low | ACCEPTED | In blast radius. Honest about coverage, and the fastest route to something that works. |
| E5 | Shareable model state in the URL | L (1 week / 3 hours) | med | DEFERRED | Touches every widget's state. Outside the blast radius. |
| E6 | Guided "try this" challenges per preset | M (per model) | med | DEFERRED | New authored content across 73 models with its own correctness burden; each spec already carries a `notes` line. |
| E7 | Coverage dashboard shows real mappings | S (1 hour / 5 min) | low | ACCEPTED | Spec review showed it reads only its localStorage layer, never `specType`. One-line base layer, in blast radius. |
| E8 | Model and concept counts in page copy derive from the registry | S | low | SKIPPED | Spec review showed the live landing page has no counts (the hardcoded ones are in `src/App.tsx`, which nothing imports). The one live list, in the Try page's fallback message, comes from the registry anyway once `VisualizePanel` is rewired. |
| E9 | CI workflow running `bun run test` and `bun run build` on pull requests | S (2 hours / 15 min) | low | DEFERRED | New infrastructure (the repo has no workflows). Worth doing; not auto-approved. |

#### 0I. Temporal interrogation

```
HOUR 1 (foundations)   The ModelDefinition contract; how an adapter wraps a legacy model
                       without moving its code; that main.tsx reloads the page on every
                       hashchange, so #/try/<type>/<preset> is parsed once at load.
HOUR 2-3 (core)        Typing a registry of heterogeneous spec types without a 62-arm
                       union by hand. build.ts must import concept maps without importing
                       React or three: concepts.ts files are pure data, aggregated in a
                       conceptMap.ts that registry.ts does not pull into the browser.
HOUR 4-5 (integration) tsc -b typechecks every file under src with vite/client types
                       only: tests are standalone scripts and may import core.ts, never
                       Widget.tsx. drei <Text> suspends inside a demand frameloop and
                       blanks the scene. The react-refresh lint rule objects to files
                       that export both components and constants.
HOUR 6+ (polish)       A scaffold script for a new model folder; a browser sweep that
                       opens every #/try/<type>/<preset>; keyboard access to every
                       slider; the 375px stacked layout for plot-stage models.
```

Feasibility blockers: (1) re-seeding Supabase needs the founder's key; removed from the
critical path by `modelMap.json`. (2) The two-stage classifier cannot be exercised under
`bun dev`; it is verified by a direct script against the API and by `vercel dev`.
(3) The whole catalog does not fit one working session; waves are the unit of delivery.
Effort, whole plan: human ~6 to 8 months / CC ~25 to 35 hours.
