Where sources disagree (ATP\n    yields, for one), the model shows the value with the convention it follows.\n\n"},{"oldText":"### Engine shape\n\n- **One folder per model**: `src/engine/models/<type>/` containing\n  - `meta.ts`: a few lines of pure data. `type`, `label`, `subjects` (one or more;\n    descriptive only, it does not restrict which concepts the model may serve),\n    `category`, `stage`, `description`, `defaultPreset`, and preset ids with their labels.\n  - `manifest.ts`: pure TypeScript, no React or three. The preset specs and `validate`.\n  - `core.ts` (maths), `equations.ts`, `parse.ts` as the recipe requires.\n  - `Widget.tsx` and `widget.css` (class names prefixed with the model type). Shared\n    classes stay in `src/engine/visualize.css`, which new models do not edit.\n  - `concepts.ts`: pure data, each concept id mapped to a preset id, with an optional\n    \"try this\" note of its own when the preset's note does not name that concept.\n  - `<type>.test.ts`.\n- **Generated registry**: `scripts/gen-models.mjs` (plain Node, no dependencies) scans the\n  folders and writes three files into `src/engine/models/`:\n  - `meta.gen.ts`: imports every `meta.ts`. Small, loaded eagerly by the Try page, the\n    coverage page and `specTypes.ts`.\n  - `load.gen.ts`: one dynamic import per `manifest.ts` and one `React.lazy` per\n    `Widget.tsx`, so a model's maths, presets and widget are its own chunks.\n  - `concepts.gen.ts`: imports every `concepts.ts`. Used by the curriculum build and the\n    tests only; the browser never loads it.\n  A folder is registered only when its `meta.ts`, `manifest.ts` and `Widget.tsx` all\n  exist; a folder holding just a pre-assigned `concepts.ts` is ignored by all three files,\n  so a half-built sibling never breaks another model's branch. These three files are\n  gitignored and produced by `predev`, `prebuild`, `pretest` and `gen:curriculum`, so they\n  cannot be stale and model branches never conflict on them.\n- **`specTypes.ts` is derived**: built entries come from `meta.gen.ts`; ids in this plan's\n  catalog that are not built yet come from one static `PLANNED` list written once in\n  Wave 0.\n- **Test runner**: `bun run test` runs `scripts/run-tests.ts`, which executes every\n  `src/**/*.test.ts` as a standalone script and fails on the first non-zero exit. A\n  separate `bun run test:catalog` re-runs the curriculum build in check mode and fails if\n  the committed catalog or subject JSON differs from what the build produces; the\n  integrator runs it on the combined tree, model branches do not.\n- **Existing 8 models get a folder** with `meta.ts`, a `manifest.ts` (their example specs\n  move here from `VisualizePanel.tsx` as presets; `validate` is re-exported from\n  `validate.ts`), `concepts.ts`, a `Widget.tsx` that re-exports the current component, and\n  a test file. Their maths, equations, parsers, widgets, schema branches and prompt text\n  stay in the files they are in now, and `physicsModels.test.ts` and\n  `circuitNetwork.test.ts` stay where they are. `projectile`, `wave-oscillator`,\n  `free-body-diagram`, `circular-motion` and `function-grapher` get the closed-form\n  assertions they never had, in their folder's test file; that file is also where each\n  model's Wave 1 extension assertions go.\n- **`VisualizePanel`** drops its import list, `MODEL_TYPES`, `validate` switch and\n  `renderModel` switch, and looks models up in the generated registry. Its list of\n  supported models in the \"No interactive visual yet\" message comes from the registry.\n  Its layout and states are the \"Try page frame\" section below.\n- **Kits** live in `src/engine/kits/` and are built by the integrator before the wave that\n  first needs them. Wave 0 also builds `ModelFrame` and the `controls` kit (see \"Try page\n  frame\"), `Transport` (play, pause, scrub and speed, extracted from the existing\n  `ps__transport` controls, shared by every time-based model) and `ExpressionInput` (the\n  only typed-maths field: debounced, keeps the last valid picture on screen, and names\n  the offending token under the field; a preset function with sliders is always the\n  first path and typing the second). `BoardStage` is built in Wave 1 with cell, token,\n  arrow and label primitives, a 12px minimum text size, and one overflow rule: a table\n  wider than the board scrolls inside it with its first column pinned. `PlotStage` is\n  built in Wave 0 (extracted from `widgets/FunctionGrapher.tsx`; it exposes its x and y\n  scales, a pointer-to-data mapping and an overlay slot for children, so a model can draw\n  arrows, segments, rays and draggable points on it without editing the kit), `Stepper`\n  in Wave 1 (a pure step reducer on top of `Transport`, with a caption line for the\n  current step, a \"step 7 of 23\" counter and replay: play stops at the last step,\n  scrubbing pauses, and under reduced motion there is no autoplay, only step buttons),\n  `ParticleBox` in Wave 3 (a pure seeded simulation core with an SVG renderer\n  and a three renderer, so it serves 3D, plot and board stages; at most 400 particles;\n  under reduced motion it shows a still snapshot with a step button).\n  Each kit's pure part (plot scales, step reducer, particle core) has its own unit test.\n  `safeExpression` also lives here from Wave 0: the one path by which any model turns\n  typed text into a function (mathjs parse, an allowlist of node types and function\n  names, a 200-character cap, no `evaluate` of raw strings). Every model that accepts a\n  typed expression uses it.\n  Unit families a wave needs and `src/engine/units.ts` lacks (pressure, amount,\n  concentration, charge, field, electron-volts and so on) are added by the integrator at\n  the same point. Temperature stays in kelvin; the unit table is single-factor.\n- **Sweep page**: `#/sweep`, present only in development builds, mounts every model and\n  preset in turn inside an error boundary and lists, per preset, whether it mounted\n  without error and drew a non-empty stage. `?nowebgl` forces the 2D fallback path.\n  Tests: none of its own; it is the verification tool.\n\nTests for this section: `src/engine/models/registry.test.ts` asserts every `meta.ts` type\nis unique and matches its folder name; preset ids in `meta.ts` equal those in\n`manifest.ts`; every preset passes its own `validate`; the default preset exists; every\npreset is the default or mapped to a concept. `scripts/check-models.test.ts` fails on a\nraw range or number input, or a literal colour, inside a model folder.\n\n","newText":"### Engine shape\n\n- **One folder per model**: `src/engine/models/<type>/`. Four files are required and make\n  the model exist; the others are added as the recipe requires. Every file has one default\n  export, checked with `satisfies` against the contract below.\n\n  | File | Default export | Holds |\n  |---|---|---|\n  | `meta.ts` (required) | `ModelMeta` | pure data: `type`, `label`, `subjects` (one or more; descriptive only, it does not restrict which concepts the model may serve), `category`, `stage`, the optional `board` layout and `aux` stage, `description`, `defaultPreset` |\n  | `core.ts` (required) | `ModelCore<Spec>` | the maths and `validate`; pure TypeScript, no React or three |\n  | `presets.ts` (required) | `Preset<Spec>[]` | each preset's `id`, `label` and `spec`; pure TypeScript |\n  | `Widget.tsx` (required) | a component taking `WidgetProps<Spec>` | the stage and its controls; `widget.css` beside it, class names prefixed with the model type |\n  | `concepts.ts` | `ConceptMap` | concept id to preset id, with an optional \"try this\" note of its own when the preset's note does not name that concept; and the concepts the builder declined, each with a reason |\n  | `equations.ts` | `EquationSet` | present exactly when the catalog says Eq |\n  | `parse.ts` | a function returning `ParseResult<Spec>` | present exactly when the catalog says WP |\n  | `data.ts` | named tables | reference data, each table with its source |\n  | `<type>.test.ts` | none | the model's assertions |\n\n  Shared classes stay in `src/engine/visualize.css`, which new models do not edit.\n- **The contract** is `src/engine/models/types.ts`, written in Wave 0. It is the one file\n  a builder reads to know every shape:\n\n  ```ts\n  export interface ModelMeta {\n    type: string                 // equals the folder name\n    label: string\n    subjects: Subject[]\n    category: string\n    stage: 'plot' | '3D' | 'board'\n    board?: 'canvas' | 'table'   // board stages only\n    aux?: 'plot' | 'board'       // optional second stage\n    description: string\n    defaultPreset: string\n    stub?: true                  // set by the scaffold, removed when the model meets the recipe\n  }\n  export interface Preset<S> { id: string; label: string; spec: S }\n  export interface ValidationResult<S> { valid: boolean; reason?: string; field?: keyof S }\n  export interface ModelCore<S> { validate(spec: S): ValidationResult<S> }\n  export interface WidgetProps<S> { spec: S }\n  export interface ConceptMap {\n    mapped: Record<string, { preset: string; note?: string }>\n    declined?: Record<string, string>   // concept id -> why this model does not show it\n  }\n  export interface ParseResult<S> {\n    outcome: 'filled' | 'partial' | 'none'\n    patch: Partial<S>\n    message: string\n  }\n  ```\n\n  A widget owns its control state, seeded from `spec`, as the eight existing widgets do.\n  `ModelFrame` supplies everything around it through context: reset (it remounts the\n  widget), the \"interacted\" usage event (the controls kit reports the first change), and\n  the concept's note.\n- **Scaffold**: `bun run new:model <type> [--stage plot|3D|board] [--eq] [--wp]\n  [--concepts <id,id,...>]` (`scripts/new-model.mjs`) writes the folder with every\n  required file in place: a one-slider spec, a default preset, a `validate`, a widget on\n  the chosen stage built from the kits, a `concepts.ts` with the given concept ids mapped\n  to the default preset, and a test that passes. It takes the stage, Eq and WP from the\n  `PLANNED` list when the type is there, so those flags are only needed for a model that\n  is not in the catalog. It refuses a type that is not kebab-case or whose folder exists.\n  It marks the model `stub: true`, and ends by printing the address to open\n  (`#/try/<type>`) and the commands a builder runs next. A stub works in development; the\n  check-mode build refuses to pass while any model is still a stub, so a scaffold cannot\n  ship as a model.\n- **Reference models**: two folders are complete, readable examples of the contract, and\n  the scaffold's stubs are cut from them. `function-grapher` is the plot example.\n  `projectile` is the 3D example with an equation set and a word-problem parser: Wave 0\n  moves its maths, equations, parser and widget out of the shared files into its folder\n  (its schema branch and prompt text stay in `src/engine/spec.ts`).\n- **Generated files**: `bun run gen:curriculum` runs `scripts/gen-models.mjs` (plain Node,\n  no dependencies) and then the curriculum build. Nothing derived from the model folders\n  is committed, so model branches never conflict on it and it cannot be stale:\n  - `src/engine/models/meta.gen.ts`: imports every `meta.ts` and records which optional\n    files each folder has. Small, loaded eagerly by the Try page, the coverage page and\n    `specTypes.ts`.\n  - `src/engine/models/load.gen.ts`: one dynamic import each for `core.ts` and\n    `presets.ts` and one `React.lazy` per `Widget.tsx`, so a model's maths, presets and\n    widget are its own chunks.\n  - `src/engine/models/concepts.gen.ts`: imports every `concepts.ts` and `presets.ts`.\n    Used by the curriculum build and the tests only; the browser never loads it.\n  - `src/data/curriculum/catalog/<subject>.json`: see \"Catalog and mapping\".\n\n  A folder is registered only when all four required files exist, matched by exact name\n  from the directory listing (so `widget.tsx` fails the same way on macOS as on Linux). A\n  folder holding only a `concepts.ts` is skipped, so a half-built sibling never breaks\n  another model's branch. Every run prints one line per folder: registered, or skipped\n  with the file that is missing.\n- **When the generator runs**: on `postinstall`, and before `dev`, `build`, `test`, `lint`\n  and `typecheck` (a new script: the generator, then `tsc -b`). While the dev server is\n  up, a small Vite plugin (`scripts/vite-plugin-models.mjs`) re-runs it when a file is\n  added to or removed from a model folder, or a `meta.ts`, `presets.ts` or `concepts.ts`\n  changes, so a new model, preset or mapping appears without a restart. A failed run in\n  the dev server shows its message in the terminal and the browser overlay and keeps the\n  last good output.\n- **`specTypes.ts` is derived**: built entries come from `meta.gen.ts`. Catalog ids that\n  are not built yet come from one static `PLANNED` list\n  (`src/data/curriculum/planned.ts`), written once in Wave 0 from this plan's tables with\n  each model's `type`, `label`, `subject`, `stage`, `eq`, `wp` and `wave`. What the app\n  shows is built, plus planned and not yet built, so finishing a model needs no edit to\n  the list.\n- **Tests**: `bun run test` runs `scripts/run-tests.ts`, which executes every\n  `src/**/*.test.ts` and `scripts/**/*.test.ts` as a standalone script, prints one line\n  per file and a summary of every failure, and exits non-zero if any failed.\n  `bun run test <text>` runs only the files whose path contains the text. Assertions use\n  `src/engine/testkit.ts` (`check(name, condition)` and\n  `near(name, actual, expected, tolerance)`). `bun test`, Bun's own runner, is the wrong\n  command for these files: a `bunfig.toml` preload makes it print \"Use `bun run test`\"\n  and exit. `bun run test:catalog` is the curriculum build in check mode (see \"Catalog\n  and mapping\"); CI and the integrator run it, a model branch does not need to.\n- **Existing 8 models get a folder**. `function-grapher` and `projectile` are moved in\n  whole (above). The other six get `meta.ts`, a `presets.ts` (their example specs move\n  here from `VisualizePanel.tsx`), a `core.ts` that re-exports `validate` from\n  `validate.ts`, `concepts.ts`, a `Widget.tsx` that re-exports the current component, and\n  a test file. Their maths, equations, parsers, widgets, schema branches and prompt text\n  stay in the files they are in now, and `physicsModels.test.ts` and\n  `circuitNetwork.test.ts` stay where they are. `projectile`, `wave-oscillator`,\n  `free-body-diagram`, `circular-motion` and `function-grapher` get the closed-form\n  assertions they never had, in their folder's test file; that file is also where each\n  model's Wave 1 extension assertions go.\n- **`VisualizePanel`** drops its import list, `MODEL_TYPES`, `validate` switch and\n  `renderModel` switch, and looks models up in the generated registry. Its list of\n  supported models in the \"No interactive visual yet\" message comes from the registry.\n  Its layout and states are the \"Try page frame\" section below.\n- **Kits** live in `src/engine/kits/` and are built by the integrator before the wave that\n  first needs them. Wave 0 also builds `ModelFrame` and the `controls` kit (see \"Try page\n  frame\"), `Transport` (play, pause, scrub and speed, extracted from the existing\n  `ps__transport` controls, shared by every time-based model) and `ExpressionInput` (the\n  only typed-maths field: debounced, keeps the last valid picture on screen, and names\n  the offending token under the field; a preset function with sliders is always the\n  first path and typing the second). `BoardStage` is built in Wave 1 with cell, token,\n  arrow and label primitives, a 12px minimum text size, the same overlay slot as\n  `PlotStage`, and one overflow rule: a table wider than the board scrolls inside it with\n  its first column pinned. `PlotStage` is\n  built in Wave 0 (extracted from `widgets/FunctionGrapher.tsx`; it exposes its x and y\n  scales, a pointer-to-data mapping and an overlay slot for children, so a model can draw\n  arrows, segments, rays and draggable points on it without editing the kit), `Stepper`\n  in Wave 1 (a pure step reducer on top of `Transport`, with a caption line for the\n  current step, a \"step 7 of 23\" counter and replay: play stops at the last step,\n  scrubbing pauses, and under reduced motion there is no autoplay, only step buttons),\n  `ParticleBox` in Wave 3 (a pure seeded simulation core with an SVG renderer\n  and a three renderer, so it serves 3D, plot and board stages; at most 400 particles;\n  under reduced motion it shows a still snapshot with a step button).\n  Each kit's pure part (plot scales, step reducer, particle core) has its own unit test.\n  `safeExpression` also lives here from Wave 0: the one path by which any model turns\n  typed text into a function (mathjs parse, an allowlist of node types and function\n  names, a 200-character cap, no `evaluate` of raw strings). It returns either a function\n  of the named variables or a failure carrying a message, the offending token and its\n  position, which `ExpressionInput` shows under the field. Every model that accepts a\n  typed expression uses it.\n  Unit families a wave needs and `src/engine/units.ts` lacks (pressure, amount,\n  concentration, charge, field, electron-volts and so on) are added by the integrator at\n  the same point. Temperature is stored in kelvin and the unit table stays single-factor;\n  `units.ts` gains one temperature helper (`toKelvin` and `fromKelvin` for degrees Celsius\n  and Fahrenheit) that every parser and the slider row's unit switch use, so no model\n  converts temperature by hand. Readouts show kelvin where the equation needs absolute\n  temperature (gas laws, Arrhenius) and degrees Celsius elsewhere, with the other on the\n  unit switch.\n- **Using and extending the kits**: every kit's props carry doc comments, and a\n  development-only page, `#/kits`, shows each kit rendered beside the code that produced\n  it. When a kit cannot do what a model needs, the builder draws it in the stage's\n  overlay slot and leaves a `KIT-REQUEST.md` in the model's folder saying what was missing\n  and what they did instead. The integrator answers every request between sub-PRs, by\n  extending the kit or by accepting the local drawing, and deletes the file; the\n  check-mode build fails while one remains.\n- **Sweep**: `#/sweep` and `#/sweep/<type>`, present only in development builds, mount\n  each preset in turn inside an error boundary and record, per preset, whether it mounted\n  without error and drew a non-empty stage, with the error text when it did not. The\n  result is on the page as a table and on `window.__sweep` for the script.\n  `bun run sweep [type] [--width 375|1280] [--nowebgl]` (`scripts/sweep.mjs`, using\n  Playwright, a new development dependency) starts the dev server if it is not running,\n  drives the page, prints the table, saves a screenshot per preset to `sweep-shots/` (not\n  committed) and exits non-zero on any failure. With no flags it runs all four\n  combinations of width and WebGL. Route options always sit inside the hash, after `?`\n  (`#/sweep/projectile?w=375&nowebgl=1`); `nowebgl=1` works on `#/try/...` too, so a\n  builder can look at one model's fallback.\n  Tests: none of its own; it is the verification tool.\n\nTests for this section: `src/engine/models/registry.test.ts` asserts every `meta.ts` type\nis unique and matches its folder name; the default preset exists; every preset passes its\nown `validate`; every preset is the default or mapped to a concept; and, for every model\non the `PLANNED` list, that its stage matches and that `equations.ts` and `parse.ts` are\npresent exactly when the list says Eq and WP. `scripts/check-models.test.ts` fails on a\nraw `input` element or a literal colour inside a model folder (`none`, `transparent`,\n`currentColor` and `inherit` are allowed; a line is exempted by an `allow-raw: <reason>`\ncomment above it, and the check prints how many exemptions exist). The raw-input rule\nalso shows in the editor as an ESLint warning. `scripts/gen-models.test.ts` covers the\nfour-file rule and the exact-name match. `scripts/new-model.test.ts` scaffolds one model\nper stage into a temporary folder and asserts that the generator registers it and that\nits test passes.\n\n"},{"oldText":"### Try page frame\n\nOne frame for every model, built once as `ModelFrame` and extracted from the layout the\nphysics models already share, so old and new models read as one product.\n\n```\n1280px (page does not scroll)                    375px (page scrolls, stage stays put)\n┌──────────────────────────────────────────┐    ┌────────────────────────┐\n│ Saras   AP Physics 1 · Fluids    all topics│    │ Saras       all topics │\n│ Buoyancy and Archimedes' principle        │    │ AP Physics 1 · Fluids  │\n│ Change the density and watch the block... │    │ Buoyancy and Archim... │\n│                     ← prev   2 of 5  next →│    │ ← prev  2 of 5  next → │\n├───────────────────────────┬──────────────┤    ├────────────────────────┤\n│ word problem (when WP yes)                │    │ stage, pinned to the   │\n├───────────────────────────┬──────────────┤    │ top, 45% of the screen │\n│                           │ controls     │    ├────────────────────────┤\n│   stage                   │ readouts     │    │ try-this note          │\n│   (3D, plot or board)     │ equations    │    │ controls               │\n│                           │ (when Eq yes)│    │ readouts               │\n│                           │   reset      │    │ equations, word problem│\n└───────────────────────────┴──────────────┘    └────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n- Header, in reading order: the course and unit as a small label; the concept title as\n  the page's one heading (the model label when no concept is given); the \"try this\" note,\n  at most two lines; previous and next concept links with \"2 of 5\". The Saras mark links\n  home and is no longer the heading. The tab title is \"<concept title> · Saras\".\n- The header has a fixed height at 1280px, so adding the note never makes the page\n  scroll. At 375px the stage is pinned to the top of the screen at 45% of its height and\n  the controls scroll beneath it, so a student always sees the picture they are changing.\n- Previous and next follow catalog order within the unit and skip unmapped concepts,\n  labelled with the concept they lead to. At the last mapped concept of a unit, next\n  becomes \"Next unit: <its first mapped concept>\", or \"Back to <course>\" when there is\n  none.\n- \"All topics\" returns to the topics page with the same subject, search and toggle, and\n  the course the student came from open and in view (carried in the link, see Topics).\n- The `controls` kit is the only source of controls: a slider row (label, value, unit),\n  a choice (segmented buttons, or a list when there are more than five options), a number\n  field with a unit, a readout row, a legend, and a reset button that restores the\n  preset. A model that needs to switch between a few built-in cases (a molecule, a\n  lattice, a mechanism) uses the choice control, never its own picker. There is no\n  model picker and no preset picker: other models and presets are reached through\n  topics and the previous and next links.\n- One number format everywhere (the existing `fmt`, extended): three significant figures,\n  scientific notation below 0.001 and from 100,000 up, the unit after a thin space, and\n  an en dash for a value that does not exist.\n- On a touch screen a 3D stage does not capture one-finger drags (the page scrolls);\n  a \"Rotate\" button on the stage turns orbiting on and off.\n- A 3D model that falls back to 2D says so in one line under the stage (\"Showing the 2D\n  view\"). If WebGL is lost mid-session the model switches to its fallback.\n- States of the stage:\n\n| State | What the student sees |\n|---|---|\n| loading | a skeleton with the stage's own outline and fixed size (no layout shift; no shimmer under reduced motion) |\n| ready | the model |\n| chunk failed | \"This model didn't load.\" and a \"Reload\" button that reloads the page |\n| refused by the gate | \"Couldn't verify a correct visual for this\" and the reason |\n| crashed | \"This model hit an error.\" with \"Reset to the starting values\" and a link to topics |\n| no such model | \"No model called X\" (X shortened to 40 characters) and a link to topics |\n\n- Navigation between pages no longer reloads the whole site: `Root` re-renders on a hash\n  change, and the Try page re-resolves when its hash changes, so stepping to the next\n  concept keeps the header in place and swaps only the stage.\n- A footer line on the Try and topics pages: \"We count which models get opened. No names,\n  no accounts.\"\n- The Try page's body text uses the site's body font variable; the leftover warm tint on\n  the old topic pill and the system-font fallback in `.viz` are removed with the rebuild.\n\nTests: `fmt` cases (three figures, both scientific thresholds, non-finite); the\nprevious, next, position and unit-end lookups; a browser check of the frame at 375px\n(stage stays visible while the last control is in view) and 1280px (no page scroll).\n\n","newText":"### Try page frame\n\nOne frame for every model, built once as `ModelFrame` and extracted from the layout the\nphysics models already share, so old and new models read as one product.\n\n```\n1280px (page does not scroll)                    375px (page scrolls, stage stays put)\n┌──────────────────────────────────────────┐    ┌────────────────────────┐\n│ Saras   AP Physics 1 · Fluids    all topics│    │ Saras       all topics │\n│ Buoyancy and Archimedes' principle        │    │ AP Physics 1 · Fluids  │\n│ Change the density and watch the block... │    │ Buoyancy and Archim... │\n│                     ← prev   2 of 5  next →│    │ ← prev  2 of 5  next → │\n├───────────────────────────┬──────────────┤    ├────────────────────────┤\n│ word problem (when WP yes)                │    │ stage, pinned to the   │\n├───────────────────────────┬──────────────┤    │ top, 45% of the screen │\n│                           │ controls     │    ├────────────────────────┤\n│   stage                   │ readouts     │    │ try-this note          │\n│   (3D, plot or board)     │ equations    │    │ controls               │\n│                           │ (when Eq yes)│    │ readouts               │\n│                           │   reset      │    │ equations, word problem│\n└───────────────────────────┴──────────────┘    └────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n- Header, in reading order: the course and unit as a small label; the concept title as\n  the page's one heading (the model label when no concept is given); the \"try this\" note,\n  at most two lines; previous and next concept links with \"2 of 5\". The Saras mark links\n  home and is no longer the heading. The tab title is \"<concept title> · Saras\".\n- The header has a fixed height at 1280px, so adding the note never makes the page\n  scroll. At 375px the stage is pinned to the top of the screen at 45% of its height and\n  the controls scroll beneath it, so a student always sees the picture they are changing.\n- A model with an `aux` stage shows both. At 1280px the stage column is split, the main\n  stage above and the aux stage below it at a third of the column's height. At 375px the\n  pinned area holds one of them, chosen by a two-button switch on the pinned stage\n  (labelled by the model, \"Model\" and \"Graph\" by default); the other sits first in the\n  scrolling column.\n- Previous and next follow catalog order within the unit and skip unmapped concepts,\n  labelled with the concept they lead to. At the last mapped concept of a unit, next\n  becomes \"Next unit: <its first mapped concept>\", or \"Back to <course>\" when there is\n  none.\n- \"All topics\" returns to the topics page with the same subject, search and toggle, and\n  the course the student came from open and in view (carried in the link, see Topics).\n- The `controls` kit is the only source of controls: a slider row (label, value, unit),\n  a choice (segmented buttons, or a list when there are more than five options), a number\n  field with a unit, a readout row, a legend, and a reset button that restores the\n  preset. A model that needs to switch between a few built-in cases (a molecule, a\n  lattice, a mechanism) uses the choice control, never its own picker. There is no\n  model picker and no preset picker: other models and presets are reached through\n  topics and the previous and next links.\n- One number format everywhere, `fmt(value, options)` (the existing `fmt`, extended): by\n  default three significant figures, except that an integer below 100,000 is shown in\n  full; scientific notation below 0.001 and from 100,000 up; the unit after a thin space;\n  an en dash for a value that does not exist. Options cover what three figures cannot:\n  `sig` for a value whose point is its precision (109.47 degrees, 68.27 percent),\n  `kind: 'int'` for counts and `kind: 'percent'`. A readout row also accepts an exact\n  string the model formats itself (a surd, a ratio such as 9:3:3:1, a binary or\n  hexadecimal number). The eight existing models move from two decimals to this format;\n  the Wave 0 browser pass reads each of their readouts against a hand calculation.\n- On a touch screen a 3D stage does not capture one-finger drags (the page scrolls);\n  a \"Rotate\" button on the stage turns orbiting on and off.\n- A 3D model that falls back to 2D says so in one line under the stage (\"Showing the 2D\n  view\"). If WebGL is lost mid-session the model switches to its fallback.\n- States of the stage:\n\n| State | What the student sees |\n|---|---|\n| loading | a skeleton with the stage's own outline and fixed size (no layout shift; no shimmer under reduced motion) |\n| ready | the model |\n| chunk failed | \"This model didn't load.\" and a \"Reload\" button that reloads the page |\n| refused by the gate | \"Couldn't verify a correct visual for this\" and the reason |\n| crashed | \"This model hit an error.\" with \"Reset to the starting values\" and a link to topics |\n| no such model | \"No model called X\" (X shortened to 40 characters) and a link to topics |\n\n- In a development build every silent fallback also writes one console warning naming\n  what was asked for and what was shown (an unknown preset, an unknown or unmapped `c`, a\n  crashed model with its type, preset and spec), so a builder's broken link does not look\n  like a working one.\n- Navigation between pages no longer reloads the whole site: `Root` re-renders on a hash\n  change, and the Try page re-resolves when its hash changes, so stepping to the next\n  concept keeps the header in place and swaps only the stage.\n- A footer line on the Try and topics pages: \"We count which models get opened. No names,\n  no accounts.\"\n- The Try page's body text uses the site's body font variable; the leftover warm tint on\n  the old topic pill and the system-font fallback in `.viz` are removed with the rebuild.\n\nTests: `fmt` cases (three figures, integers in full, the `sig`, `int` and `percent`\noptions, both scientific thresholds, non-finite); the\nprevious, next, position and unit-end lookups; a browser check of the frame at 375px\n(stage stays visible while the last control is in view) and 1280px (no page scroll).\n\n"},{"oldText":"### Usage logging\n\nBreadth is only a discovery instrument if use is measured, so the logging code ships in\nWave 0.\n\n- `src/lib/usage.ts` records three events per model visit: `open` when a model renders,\n  `interacted` the first time a control changes, and `engaged` with the seconds the tab\n  was visible on it (capped at 30 minutes), sent with `keepalive` when the page is hidden\n  or left. On the topics page it records `want` when a student clicks a concept that has\n  no model yet. Each row carries the model type, preset, concept id when there is one,\n  and a random per-tab session id. No account id, no free text.\n- Rows go to a Supabase table `model_events`. The table and its row-level security\n  (anonymous insert only, no select, length and range checks) are defined in\n  `supabase/model_events.sql`, which the founder runs once. Until the table exists every\n  insert fails and is dropped.\n- A failed insert never reaches the student. In development it logs one console warning.\n  The coverage page (`#/coverage`) shows \"usage logging: on\" or \"usage logging: table\n  missing\", from a probe insert.\n\n`supabase/usage_report.sql` is the query the founder runs to read it: opens and\nreturning sessions per course, per week.\n\nTests: a unit test of the payload builder (seconds capped, unknown fields dropped) and\nthat a rejected insert does not throw.\n\n","newText":"### Usage logging\n\nBreadth is only a discovery instrument if use is measured, so the logging code ships in\nWave 0.\n\n- `src/lib/usage.ts` records three events per model visit: `open` when a model renders,\n  `interacted` the first time a control changes, and `engaged` with the seconds the tab\n  was visible on it (capped at 30 minutes), sent with `keepalive` when the page is hidden\n  or left. On the topics page it records `want` when a student clicks a concept that has\n  no model yet. Each row carries the model type, preset, concept id when there is one,\n  a random per-tab session id and the app version. No account id, no free text.\n- Nothing is recorded from a development build, from `#/sweep` or `#/kits`, or from a\n  browser where `localStorage.saras_no_usage` is set (the founder's own). In those cases\n  each event is written to the console instead, so a builder can see it fire. Builders,\n  sweeps and the founder's own testing therefore never reach the table.\n- Rows go to a Supabase table `model_events`. The table and its row-level security\n  (anonymous insert only, no select, length and range checks) are defined in\n  `supabase/model_events.sql`, which the founder runs once and can run again safely (it\n  creates only what is missing). Until the table exists every insert fails and is\n  dropped.\n- `src/lib/supabase.ts` no longer throws at import when the two environment values are\n  missing, as they are in a fresh worktree: the client is built against a placeholder\n  address, `hasSupabaseConfig` stays false, and `usage.ts` does nothing.\n- A failed insert never reaches the student. The coverage page (`#/coverage`) shows one\n  of five states: \"usage logging: on\", \"table missing: run supabase/model_events.sql\",\n  \"rejected by the table's rules\", \"can't reach Supabase\" and \"not configured (no\n  .env.local)\". It learns the state from a probe row that the table's own checks refuse,\n  so the probe never writes anything.\n\n`supabase/usage_report.sql` is the query the founder runs to read it: opens and\nreturning sessions per course, per week.\n\nTests: a unit test of the payload builder (seconds capped, unknown fields dropped); that\na rejected insert does not throw; that nothing is sent in a development build, on the\nsweep page or with the local flag; and that each of the probe's five outcomes is read\nfrom the right response.\n\n"},{"oldText":"### Continuous integration\n\n`.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs install, `bun run test`, `bun run test:catalog`, lint and\nbuild on every pull request. If the push is refused because the token cannot write\nworkflow files, the file is left out of the PR and handed to the founder to add.\n\n","newText":"### Continuous integration\n\n`.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs install, `bun run test`, `bun run test:catalog`, lint,\nbuild and the 2D sweep (`bun run sweep --nowebgl`) on every pull request. Bun's version is\npinned in `package.json` (`packageManager`) and CI reads it from there.\n`package-lock.json` is deleted; `bun.lock` is the one lockfile. Wave 0's PR confirms that\nthe Vercel preview builds with the generator in its build step. If the push is refused\nbecause the token cannot write workflow files, the workflow is saved as `docs/ci.yml.txt`\nand a TODOS.md entry asks the founder to add it, so CI cannot end up silently missing.\n\n"},{"oldText":"### Catalog and mapping\n\n- `bun run gen:curriculum` runs `scripts/gen-models.mjs` and then\n  `src/data/curriculum/build.ts`. The build reads `concepts.gen.ts` and `meta.gen.ts`,\n  stamps `specType` into the generated subject JSON, and writes one compact catalog file\n  per subject, `src/data/curriculum/catalog/<subject>.json`. Catalog\n  and subject JSON are committed; the integrator regenerates them when a wave is merged.\n- Catalog order: courses follow an explicit ordered list of the 23 course slugs in a new\n  `src/data/curriculum/courseOrder.ts`; units follow each course's own `units` array;\n  concepts keep the order they have in the raw files. Each concept is\n  `{ id, title, model? }` where `model` is `[type, preset]`. A unit with no concepts is\n  left out of the catalog.\n- The 4 existing mappings move into the `concepts.ts` of `projectile`, `orbit-sim` and\n  `ray-diagram`. `specType` is removed from `raw/*.json`, and the build fails if a raw file\n  sets it. Mapping has one source.\n- The build applies the Goal's gate and prints the Goal's report. The exclusion list lives\n  in `src/data/curriculum/exclusions.ts`. The mapping checks are one pure function,\n  `validateMappings`, so they can be tested on small fixtures.\n\nTests for this section, in `registry.test.ts`: every mapped concept id exists in the\ncurriculum; no concept maps to two models; every mapped preset exists in that model's\n`meta.ts`. `validateMappings` is tested on fixtures for each failure it must catch\n(unknown concept, duplicate mapping, missing preset, raw `specType`, uncovered course,\nstale or unknown exclusion). The generator's complete-folder rule is tested in\n`scripts/gen-models.test.ts` (tests under `scripts/` may use node APIs; the runner\nexecutes them too). `bun run test:catalog` covers staleness.\n\n- `src/coverage/CurriculumCoverage.tsx` uses the real mapping as the base layer. A real\n  mapping wins and is read-only there; the localStorage planning layer applies only to\n  concepts with no real mapping.\n\n","newText":"### Catalog and mapping\n\n- `bun run gen:curriculum` runs `scripts/gen-models.mjs` and then\n  `src/data/curriculum/build.ts`. The build reads `concepts.gen.ts` and `meta.gen.ts` and\n  writes one compact catalog file per subject, `src/data/curriculum/catalog/<subject>.json`.\n  The catalog is generated and not committed, like the registry files. A model branch's\n  own mappings are in its catalog as soon as its `concepts.ts` is saved, so its \"Try\"\n  chip, its `?c=` link and its previous and next links all work in that branch, and\n  parallel branches never conflict on it.\n- The committed subject JSON (`physics.json` and the rest) no longer carries `specType`.\n  It is a function of the raw files alone, so no model branch changes it. Everything that\n  read `specType` from it (the curriculum index helpers, the coverage page, the seed\n  script, `src/topics/concepts.ts`) reads the mapping from the catalog or from\n  `concepts.gen.ts` instead.\n- Catalog order: courses follow an explicit ordered list of the 23 course slugs in a new\n  `src/data/curriculum/courseOrder.ts`; units follow each course's own `units` array;\n  concepts keep the order they have in the raw files. Each concept is\n  `{ id, title, model? }` where `model` is `[type, preset]`. A unit with no concepts is\n  left out of the catalog.\n- The 4 existing mappings move into the `concepts.ts` of `projectile`, `orbit-sim` and\n  `ray-diagram`. `specType` is removed from `raw/*.json`, and the build fails if a raw file\n  sets it. Mapping has one source.\n- A builder who finds that the model does not faithfully show an assigned concept moves\n  it from `mapped` to `declined` in `concepts.ts`, with a one-line reason. The build's\n  report lists declined concepts, so the integrator can reassign them or leave them\n  unmapped. A builder does not add mappings: a concept the model turns out to show is\n  proposed in the builder's hand-off report and added by the integrator, who knows what\n  the sibling models claim.\n- The build applies the Goal's gate and prints the Goal's report. The exclusion list lives\n  in `src/data/curriculum/exclusions.ts`. The mapping checks are one pure function,\n  `validateMappings`, so they can be tested on small fixtures.\n- The build has two modes. In the normal mode (every hook, every model branch) a\n  \"pending\" exclusion for a course that is now mapped is a warning naming the entry to\n  delete; every other failure stops the build. In check mode (`bun run test:catalog`, run\n  by CI and by the integrator) that warning is an error, and the build also fails if a\n  committed subject JSON differs from what the raw files produce, if any model is still\n  a stub, or if a `KIT-REQUEST.md` remains.\n\nTests for this section, in `registry.test.ts`: every mapped concept id exists in the\ncurriculum; no concept maps to two models; every mapped preset exists in that model's\n`presets.ts`. `validateMappings` is tested on fixtures for each failure it must catch\n(unknown concept, duplicate mapping, missing preset, raw `specType`, uncovered course,\nstale or unknown exclusion, a concept both mapped and declined) and for both modes. The\ngenerator's complete-folder rule is tested in `scripts/gen-models.test.ts` (tests under\n`scripts/` may use node APIs; the runner executes them too).\n\n- `src/coverage/CurriculumCoverage.tsx` uses the real mapping as the base layer. A real\n  mapping wins and is read-only there; the localStorage planning layer applies only to\n  concepts with no real mapping.\n\n"},{"oldText":"### First real mappings (so Wave 0 is verifiable end to end)\n\n`function-grapher` is brought to the full recipe (folder, presets, `PlotStage`, 375px\npass, `built:true`) with presets for the Algebra 1 concepts it shows faithfully: absolute\nvalue functions, graphing linear equations, slope of a line, slope-intercept form,\npoint-slope form, standard form of a line, x- and y-intercepts, graphing parabolas, vertex\nform of a quadratic, exponential functions, exponential growth and decay models. Its test\nasserts every preset passes `validateFunctionGrapher` (which tolerates asymptotes) and that\nthese eleven, which have none, are finite at every sample across their domain. The unit\n\"Forms of linear equations\" then has three mapped concepts in a row, which is what the\nnext-in-unit browser check walks.\n\n","newText":"### First real mappings (so Wave 0 is verifiable end to end)\n\n`function-grapher` is brought to the full recipe (folder, presets, `PlotStage`, 375px\npass, `built:true`) with presets for the Algebra 1 concepts it shows faithfully: absolute\nvalue functions, graphing linear equations, slope of a line, slope-intercept form,\npoint-slope form, standard form of a line, x- and y-intercepts, graphing parabolas, vertex\nform of a quadratic, exponential functions, exponential growth and decay models. Its test\nasserts every preset passes `validateFunctionGrapher` (which tolerates asymptotes) and that\nthese eleven, which have none, are finite at every sample across their domain. The unit\n\"Forms of linear equations\" then has three mapped concepts in a row, which is what the\nnext-in-unit browser check walks.\n\n### The builder's path and the docs\n\nThe people who use this structure are the agents that each build one model, the agent\nthat checks it, the integrator, and the founder. Their first five minutes, from a fresh\ngit worktree:\n\n| Step | Command | Result |\n|---|---|---|\n| 1 | `bun run setup:worktree` | installs, copies `.env.local` from the main checkout when there is one, picks a free port and saves it for this worktree, runs the generator, prints the address to open |\n| 2 | `bun dev` | the dev server on that port |\n| 3 | open `#/try/<type>` | the model's stub, live inside the real frame (the integrator scaffolded it with `bun run new:model`) |\n| 4 | edit `core.ts` or `Widget.tsx` | the picture changes on save |\n| 5 | `bun run test <type>` and `bun run sweep <type>` | the model's own assertions; its presets at both widths, with and without WebGL |\n\nTarget: steps 1 to 3 in under five minutes, most of it the install. `vite.config.ts` reads\nthe saved port and refuses to start on a busy one, so a builder never ends up looking at\nanother worktree's server.\n\nDocs written in Wave 0, because 65 builders will read them and none of them wrote the\nfoundation:\n\n- `docs/MODELS.md`, the authoring guide: a glossary with one word per thing (model, model\n  type, spec, preset, widget, stage, kit, registry, catalog, concept; the older names\n  `specTypes.ts` and `VisualizePanel` are mapped onto them); the five steps above; a\n  copy-paste skeleton for each file; `projectile` and `function-grapher` walked through as\n  worked examples; a table of every command; the kit-request and declined-concept rules;\n  and one anchored entry per message in \"Messages a builder sees\". A dated \"Changes\"\n  list at the top records every change to the contract or a kit.\n- `docs/MODEL-REVIEW.md`, the second agent's checklist (the recipe's verification and\n  design checks and the reference-data check) with a fixed pass/fail report format, so\n  every review reads the same.\n- `docs/MODEL-BRIEF.md`, the template the integrator fills in for each builder: the\n  model's row from the catalog, its assigned concepts, its stage and kits, the files it\n  may touch (its own folder only), the commands to run, and what the hand-off report must\n  contain (what was built, anything declined, any kit request, and what slowed the\n  builder down).\n- `README.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and `src/data/curriculum/README.md` are corrected: the project\n  structure, the full command list (`test`, `typecheck`, `gen:curriculum`, `new:model`,\n  `sweep`, `setup:worktree`), the model count, and how the curriculum is regenerated.\n  Counts in docs are copied from the build's report, not typed from memory.\n\nThe contract and the kits change only through the integrator. A change to `types.ts`\nshows up as a type error in every model it affects. A breaking kit change is made\ntogether with the edits to every model that uses it, in one PR, and noted in the\n\"Changes\" list.\n\nAfter Wave 1 the integrator records, beside the effort actuals, the time from a fresh\nworktree to the stub on screen and the \"what slowed me down\" lines from the builders'\nreports, and fixes the guide or the scaffold before Wave 2.\n\nTests: `scripts/setup-worktree.test.ts` (the port is free and saved; an existing\n`.env.local` is not overwritten).\n\n### Messages a builder sees\n\nEvery check that can stop a builder says what is wrong, with the actual value; where, by\nfile and id; and what to do. Each has an entry in `docs/MODELS.md`.\n\n| Check | Example |\n|---|---|\n| generator, incomplete folder | `skipped unit-circle: Widget.tsx is missing (found widget.tsx; names are case-sensitive). A model needs meta.ts, core.ts, presets.ts and Widget.tsx.` |\n| generator, no default export | `unit-circle/presets.ts has no default export. Expected: export default [...] satisfies Preset<UnitCircleSpec>[]` |\n| mapping, unknown concept | `unit-circle/concepts.ts maps \"math.algebra-2.unit-circl\", which is not a concept. Closest id: \"math.algebra-2.unit-circle-a2\".` |\n| mapping, two models | `\"physics.ap-physics-1.buoyancy\" is mapped by both buoyancy and fluid-flow. A concept has one model: remove it from one concepts.ts.` |\n| mapping, missing preset | `unit-circle/concepts.ts maps \"math.algebra-2.unit-circle-a2\" to preset \"radians\", but presets.ts has: default, special-angles.` |\n| mapping, raw file | `raw/physics.json sets specType on \"physics.ap-physics-1.buoyancy\". Mappings live in a model's concepts.ts: delete the field.` |\n| gate, uncovered course | `Course \"geometry\" has no mapped concept and no entry in exclusions.ts. Map a concept, or add an entry with a reason.` |\n| gate, pending entry now covered | `exclusions.ts still lists \"algebra-2\" as pending, but unit-circle maps into it. Delete that entry.` (a warning in a model branch, an error in check mode) |\n| gate, unknown course | `exclusions.ts names course \"algebra-3\", which does not exist. Did you mean \"algebra-2\"?` |\n| check mode, subject JSON | `physics.json differs from what raw/physics.json produces. Run bun run gen:curriculum and commit physics.json.` |\n| check mode, stub | `unit-circle is still a scaffold stub. Remove stub: true from meta.ts when the model meets the recipe.` |\n| check mode, kit request | `unit-circle/KIT-REQUEST.md is unanswered. The integrator extends the kit or accepts the local drawing, then deletes the file.` |\n| registry, preset refused | `free-body-diagram preset \"steep\" fails its own validate: \"Angle must be between 0 and 90\" (field: angle).` |\n| registry, orphan preset | `unit-circle preset \"radians\" is not the default and no concept maps to it. Map a concept to it or delete it.` |\n| registry, Eq / WP | `planned.ts says rc-circuit has a word-problem parser, but rc-circuit/parse.ts is missing.` |\n| model rules | `unit-circle/Widget.tsx:41 uses a raw <input>. Use SliderRow or NumberField from the controls kit, or leave a KIT-REQUEST.md.` |\n| model rules, colour | `unit-circle/widget.css:12 uses the literal colour #7fb2ff. Use a colour variable (see materials.ts).` |\n| an assertion | `FAIL projectile range at 45 degrees: expected 40.775 (within 0.004), got 40.12` |\n| wrong test command | `These tests are standalone scripts. Use: bun run test` |\n| port in use | `Port 5183 is in use. Run bun run setup:worktree to pick another.` |\n| no Supabase config | `No .env.local: sign-in and usage logging are off. Models and topics work without it.` |\n\nTests: each message is asserted by the test of the check that produces it.\n\n"},{"oldText":"## The catalog\n\nMapping rule, applied to every concept: map it only when the model shows THAT concept\nfaithfully. Precedent: `optical-instruments` (lens combinations) was left unmapped because\nthe single-lens widget cannot show a microscope. Unmapped concepts stay honest gaps.\n\nConcept ids are assigned before a wave starts, not by the model builders. The integrator\nwrites each new model's `concepts.ts` (concept id to preset id) before fan-out, so two\nmodels never claim one concept. Tie-break when two could: the concept goes to the model\nwhose primary interaction is that concept. The \"Concept families\" column below is the\ninput to that assignment, not the assignment itself.\n\n\"Gate\" is the deterministic core each model's validator and tests rest on. \"Stage\" is the\nrecipe's stage kind. \"Eq / WP\" says whether the model has an equation set and a\nword-problem parser.\n\n","newText":"## The catalog\n\nMapping rule, applied to every concept: map it only when the model shows THAT concept\nfaithfully. Precedent: `optical-instruments` (lens combinations) was left unmapped because\nthe single-lens widget cannot show a microscope. Unmapped concepts stay honest gaps.\n\nConcept ids are assigned before a wave starts, not by the model builders. The integrator\npasses each new model's concept ids to the scaffold before fan-out, so two models never\nclaim one concept; the builder points each one at the preset that shows it, or declines\nit with a reason. Tie-break when two could: the concept goes to the model\nwhose primary interaction is that concept. The \"Concept families\" column below is the\ninput to that assignment, not the assignment itself.\n\n\"Gate\" is the deterministic core each model's validator and tests rest on. \"Stage\" is the\nrecipe's stage kind. \"Eq / WP\" says whether the model has an equation set and a\nword-problem parser.\n\n"},{"oldText":"### Stage details\n\nColour. Before Wave 1 the palette gains what the new subjects need, in one place\n(`materials.ts` and the `.viz` variables): a six-step series ramp for plots, a\npositive/negative pair, a reactant/product pair, and element colours. Where a field has a\nconvention (red oxygen, red for positive charge) the hue follows the convention and the\nlightness and saturation follow the house palette. Nothing is told apart by colour alone:\nevery coloured thing also has a label, a sign or a shape.\n\n3D models, their one glass object, and what the 2D fallback shows:\n\n| Model | Glass object | 2D fallback |\n|---|---|---|\n| `surface-3d` | the surface | contour map with the probe point and gradient arrow |\n| `molecule-3d` | the central atom | wedge-and-dash structure with bond angles labelled |\n| `solid-of-revolution` | the solid | the region and one slice in cross-section |\n| `solid-geometry` | the solid | outline drawing with dimensions labelled |\n| `conic-sections` | the cutting plane | the cut curve in its own plane with its foci |\n| `vectors-3d` | the plane the vectors span | component table beside an x-y projection |\n| `collisions` | the first cart | side view |\n| `energy-conservation` | the ball | side view with the energy bars |\n| `torque-balance` | the beam | side view |\n| `rolling-motion` | the rolling body | side view |\n| `angular-momentum` | the platform | top view |\n| `buoyancy` | the fluid | side cross-section |\n| `fluid-flow` | the pipe | side cross-section |\n| `magnetic-force` | the charge | view along the field, showing the circular path |\n| `magnetic-field-wire` | the wire | cross-section with the field circles |\n| `ideal-gas` | the box | flat box of particles |\n| `atom-model` | the nucleus | shell diagram |\n| `crystal-lattice` | the unit cell | plan view, layer by layer |\n| `stereochemistry` | the stereocentre | wedge-and-dash drawings of both mirror images |\n| `conformations` | the front carbon | Newman projection |\n| `cell-explorer` | the selected organelle | labelled cross-section |\n| `dna-structure` | one base pair | untwisted ladder |\n\nGestures. Where the stage itself is the control: `linear-transform` (drag the tips of the\ntwo basis vectors), `regression` (drag data points), `electric-field` (drag charges),\n`unit-circle` (drag the point round the circle), `triangle-solver` (drag vertices),\n`transformations-2d` (drag the figure), `conic-sections` (drag the plane's tilt). Each\nhas arrow-key nudging and mirrored number fields. Vectors in `vectors-3d` are edited by\ntheir component fields, not dragged.\n\nBoundaries that are the lesson, drawn and labelled rather than refused:\n\n| Model | Boundary | What is drawn |\n|---|---|---|\n| `refraction` | past the critical angle | the reflected ray, labelled \"total internal reflection\" |\n| `triangle-solver` | SSA with no solution, or two | no triangle with the gap shown, or both triangles |\n| `doppler` | source at or above wave speed | the wavefronts piling into a cone; frequency shown as an en dash |\n| `photoelectric-effect` | below the threshold frequency | no electrons at any intensity, labelled |\n| `linear-transform` | determinant zero | the grid collapsed onto a line, labelled \"no inverse\" |\n| `galvanic-cell` | negative cell potential | the cell labelled \"not spontaneous as drawn\" |\n| `free-body-diagram` | static friction holding | the block at rest with friction equal to the pull |\n| `ray-diagram` | object at the focal point | parallel rays, \"image at infinity\" (already built) |\n| `orbit-sim` | at or above escape speed | the open path, labelled \"unbound\" (already built) |\n\nPermanent exclusion: the \"Computer programming\" course (JavaScript drawing, HTML/CSS and\nSQL). Its concepts are language syntax and tooling; no model here shows them faithfully.\nEvery other course gets at least one mapped concept by the wave named in its pending entry.\n\n","newText":"### Stage details\n\nColour. Before Wave 1 the palette gains what the new subjects need, in one place\n(`materials.ts` and the `.viz` variables): a six-step series ramp for plots, a\npositive/negative pair, a reactant/product pair, and element colours. Where a field has a\nconvention (red oxygen, red for positive charge) the hue follows the convention and the\nlightness and saturation follow the house palette. Nothing is told apart by colour alone:\nevery coloured thing also has a label, a sign or a shape.\n\n3D models, their one glass object, and what the 2D fallback shows:\n\n| Model | Glass object | 2D fallback |\n|---|---|---|\n| `surface-3d` | the surface | contour map with the probe point and gradient arrow |\n| `molecule-3d` | the central atom | wedge-and-dash structure with bond angles labelled |\n| `solid-of-revolution` | the solid | the region and one slice in cross-section |\n| `solid-geometry` | the solid | outline drawing with dimensions labelled |\n| `conic-sections` | the cutting plane | the cut curve in its own plane with its foci |\n| `vectors-3d` | the plane the vectors span | component table beside an x-y projection |\n| `collisions` | the first cart | side view |\n| `energy-conservation` | the ball | side view with the energy bars |\n| `torque-balance` | the beam | side view |\n| `rolling-motion` | the rolling body | side view |\n| `angular-momentum` | the platform | top view |\n| `buoyancy` | the fluid | side cross-section |\n| `fluid-flow` | the pipe | side cross-section |\n| `magnetic-force` | the charge | view along the field, showing the circular path |\n| `magnetic-field-wire` | the wire | cross-section with the field circles |\n| `ideal-gas` | the box | flat box of particles |\n| `atom-model` | the nucleus | shell diagram |\n| `crystal-lattice` | the unit cell | plan view, layer by layer |\n| `stereochemistry` | the stereocentre | wedge-and-dash drawings of both mirror images |\n| `conformations` | the front carbon | Newman projection |\n| `cell-explorer` | the selected organelle | labelled cross-section |\n| `dna-structure` | one base pair | untwisted ladder |\n\nSecond stages. These models declare an `aux` stage:\n\n| Model | Main stage | Aux stage |\n|---|---|---|\n| `unit-circle` | the circle | the sine, cosine and tangent traces (plot) |\n| `kinematics-1d` | the three linked graphs | the cart on its track (board) |\n| `energy-conservation` | the track | the energy bars (board) |\n| `ideal-gas` | the box of particles | the PV diagram (plot) |\n| `states-of-matter` | the heating curve | the particles (board) |\n| `conformations` | the molecule | the energy curve (plot) |\n\nGestures. Where the stage itself is the control: `linear-transform` (drag the tips of the\ntwo basis vectors), `regression` (drag data points), `electric-field` (drag charges),\n`unit-circle` (drag the point round the circle), `triangle-solver` (drag vertices),\n`transformations-2d` (drag the figure), `conic-sections` (drag the plane's tilt). Each\nhas arrow-key nudging and mirrored number fields. Vectors in `vectors-3d` are edited by\ntheir component fields, not dragged.\n\nBoundaries that are the lesson, drawn and labelled rather than refused:\n\n| Model | Boundary | What is drawn |\n|---|---|---|\n| `refraction` | past the critical angle | the reflected ray, labelled \"total internal reflection\" |\n| `triangle-solver` | SSA with no solution, or two | no triangle with the gap shown, or both triangles |\n| `doppler` | source at or above wave speed | the wavefronts piling into a cone; frequency shown as an en dash |\n| `photoelectric-effect` | below the threshold frequency | no electrons at any intensity, labelled |\n| `linear-transform` | determinant zero | the grid collapsed onto a line, labelled \"no inverse\" |\n| `galvanic-cell` | negative cell potential | the cell labelled \"not spontaneous as drawn\" |\n| `free-body-diagram` | static friction holding | the block at rest with friction equal to the pull |\n| `ray-diagram` | object at the focal point | parallel rays, \"image at infinity\" (already built) |\n| `orbit-sim` | at or above escape speed | the open path, labelled \"unbound\" (already built) |\n\nPermanent exclusion: the \"Computer programming\" course (JavaScript drawing, HTML/CSS and\nSQL). Its concepts are language syntax and tooling; no model here shows them faithfully.\nEvery other course gets at least one mapped concept by the wave named in its pending entry.\n\n"},{"oldText":"## Order and delivery\n\n- Wave 0 ships first, alone, as its own PR and version (0.3.4.0). Nothing else starts\n  until it is merged, because every later model depends on the folder shape, the generator\n  and the catalog.\n- No PR carries more than seven new models, so one person can review it. Wave 1 is one\n  PR; Wave 2 is two (6 and 5); Wave 3 is three (7, 7, 6); Wave 4 is two (5 and 5); Wave 5\n  is two (6 and 5); Wave 6 is one. Mode: DX POLISH (set by /autoplan). Product type: an internal authoring framework (a
model-folder contract, a scaffold, shared kits, generators and checks) used to build a
student-facing app. The "developer" in this review is whoever adds a model. Prior DX
reviews on this project: none.

#### Developer persona card

```
TARGET DEVELOPER PERSONA
Who:       a coding agent given one model to build, in its own git worktree, from a brief
Context:   starts cold; did not build the foundation; holds a catalog row and a concept list
Tolerance: reads what it is pointed at; guesses when a shape is not written down;
           cannot ask a neighbour; 65 of them will each make the same guess differently
Expects:   one command to a running stub, typed contracts, errors that name the fix,
           a checklist to finish against
Also:      the second agent who reviews the model, the integrator who merges a wave,
           and the founder who spot-checks
```

Evidence: the plan's "Order and delivery" (one agent per model, each in its own worktree,
a second agent that repeats the hands-on pass). `README.md` has no "who is this for"
language. Auto-decided (P6): the most common user of the structure, by a factor of 65.

#### Developer empathy narrative

The plan as it stood when this phase began, in the builder's words:

> I'm handed `unit-circle` and a worktree. The plan says my folder needs a `meta.ts`, a
> `manifest.ts` and a `Widget.tsx`, but not what any of them exports, so I open
> `projectile` to copy it. Its folder is a set of re-exports; the real code is in four
> shared files I'm told not to touch. I guess at the shapes. I run `bun dev` and the app
> throws before it renders: this worktree has no `.env.local`, and the Supabase client
> refuses an empty address. I copy the file across by hand. The server comes up on a
> different port from the one in the docs, because another builder has that one. My model
> isn't in the app, because the generated registry only refreshes when the server
> starts. I restart. `#/try/unit-circle` renders, but the recipe says the concept a link
> names must be visible, and `?c=` is ignored: the catalog is a committed file only the
> integrator regenerates. I run the generator myself, and its gate fails on an exclusion
> list I'm not allowed to edit. I have a stub on screen and three things I can't verify.

Observed in the plan and the repo: no contract, adapter folders with nothing to copy,
`src/lib/supabase.ts` building a client from an empty address, port 5180 with no strict
setting, a committed catalog, generation only in pre-hooks. Predicted, not measured: the
elapsed time (the independent reviewer put it at 20 to 40 minutes).

After this phase's changes the same builder runs `bun run setup:worktree` and `bun dev`,
opens the address the script printed, and sees a scaffolded stub of their own model in
the real frame, with their assigned concepts already linked.

#### Competitive DX benchmark

The clock: a fresh worktree to the builder's own model rendering inside the real Try
frame, install included. Research ran through web search (Aside is not installed). No
peer publishes a measured time for this boundary, so the comparison is of DX choices.

| Tool | Start to result | Time and evidence type | DX choice | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| PhET (`simula-rasa` template, `grunt create-sim`) | clone the template and libraries, install, one generator command | not stated; several clones and installs (reported steps) | a blank-sim template repo plus one command with `--repo` and `--author` | github.com/phetsims/phet-info (development overview), github.com/phetsims/simula-rasa |
| Plop, Hygen, Nx generators | one command writes files from templates kept in the repo | not stated; no measurement found | in-repo templates, so every new unit has the same shape | npm-compare.com (hygen, plop, yeoman-generator), nx.dev/docs |
| Saras, plan as reviewed | worktree to stub at `#/try/<type>` | 20 to 40 minutes, estimated by the independent reviewer | three hand-written files, no written contract, restart to register | this plan and the repo |
| Saras, plan as amended | the same | under 5 minutes, estimated, most of it the install; measured in Wave 1 | a setup command, a scaffold, registration without a restart | this review |

Target tier: Competitive (2 to 5 minutes). Auto-decided (P5): Champion (under 2 minutes)
is bounded by the install in a fresh worktree, which this plan does not control.

#### Magical moment

The builder runs one command and sees their own model, not a demo, live inside the real
frame with their concepts linked, and it changes when they save. Delivery vehicle: the
scaffold plus the address the setup script prints. It uses only what exists (Vite, the
generator, `ModelFrame`); no hosted playground or new service.

#### Developer journey map

| Stage | Developer does | Friction found | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Discover | reads the brief the integrator wrote | the brief was not specified; the recipe lived only in the plan | fixed: `docs/MODEL-BRIEF.md` template |
| 2. Orient | reads how a model is shaped | no contract; eight adapter folders and nothing whole to copy; six words for one thing | fixed: `types.ts`, two reference models, a glossary in `docs/MODELS.md` |
| 3. Install | prepares the worktree | no `.env.local`, so the app throws at import; the port drifts when 5180 is taken | fixed: `bun run setup:worktree`, saved strict port, `supabase.ts` no longer throws |
| 4. Hello world | gets a model on screen | three files by hand; restart to register | fixed: `bun run new:model`; the Vite plugin re-runs the generator |
| 5. Build | writes the maths, presets and widget | one stage slot for two-picture models; flat-only specs; one number format that mangles exact values; no temperature helper | fixed: `aux` stage, arrays of flat records, `fmt` options, `toKelvin` |
| 6. Test | runs the model's assertions | failures printed a name and no values; `bun test` ran the wrong runner; fail-fast hid later failures | fixed: `testkit.ts`, summary runner, a filter, the `bun test` guard |
| 7. Debug | reads what a check says | six mapping failures and five other checks had no message text; an incomplete folder was skipped silently; broken links fell back silently | fixed: "Messages a builder sees"; per-folder generator output; development console warnings |
| 8. Verify and hand off | sweeps, then hands to the second agent | "the scripted sweep" had no script; the concept link could not work in a model branch; no review format | fixed: `bun run sweep`; the catalog is generated in every branch; `docs/MODEL-REVIEW.md` |
| 9. Maintain | lives with contract and kit changes across six waves | no rule for a kit that falls short or changes; no way to decline a mapping | fixed: overlay slot, `KIT-REQUEST.md`, `declined`, a dated "Changes" list |

#### First-time developer confusion report

```
FIRST-TIME DEVELOPER REPORT
Persona: a coding agent building unit-circle from a brief, cold
Attempting: the plan as it stood when this phase began

T+0:00  Reads the recipe. "a meta.ts and a manifest.ts": which is which? No export
        names, no prop types.                                  -> addressed: types.ts
T+0:04  Opens projectile/ to copy it. Re-exports only.         -> addressed: reference models
T+0:09  bun dev. White screen: "supabaseUrl is required."      -> addressed: setup script, import guard
T+0:13  Server is on 5181, the brief said 5180.                -> addressed: saved strict port
T+0:15  New folder is not in the app. Restarts the server.     -> addressed: Vite plugin
T+0:17  Typo: widget.tsx. "No model called unit-circle".       -> addressed: generator names the file
T+0:24  ?c= does nothing in this branch.                       -> addressed: generated catalog
T+0:27  Runs gen:curriculum. Gate fails on exclusions.ts.      -> addressed: warning in a model branch
T+0:31  bun test. "0 tests". Thinks the model has none.        -> addressed: bun test guard
T+0:34  Test fails: "FAIL sin at 30". Expected what?           -> addressed: near()
T+0:38  "Run the scripted sweep." There is no command.         -> addressed: bun run sweep
T+0:40  Stub on screen, three checks unverifiable. Asks for help.
```

#### Dual voices

CODEX SAYS (DX, developer experience challenge): unavailable. The `codex` CLI is not
installed on this machine, so there is no outside review in this phase.

CLAUDE SUBAGENT (DX, independent review), input `dx a02c89f6938a…6866`, read in full.
Verdict: "the architecture is sound for developers, but the authoring loop is
under-specified." 25 findings: 2 critical, 11 high, 12 medium (the reviewer's summary
line says 9 high and 10 medium; its list has 11 and 12). Each was checked against the repo before a
decision.

| # | Severity | Finding | Decision |
|---|---|---|---|
| F1 | critical | The model contract is never specified | accepted: `types.ts`, four required files, default exports |
| F2 | critical | Recipe step 9 cannot be done in a model branch (committed catalog, gate on a shared list) | accepted: catalog generated in every branch; stale pending entry is a warning outside check mode |
| F3 | high | No scaffold and no whole example | accepted: `bun run new:model`; `projectile` moved whole (taste) |
| F4 | high | A fresh worktree crashes at import; the port drifts | accepted: setup script, strict saved port, import guard. Confirmed in `src/lib/supabase.ts` and `vite.config.ts` |
| F5 | high | Generated files exist only behind three pre-hooks; no watch | accepted: `postinstall`, five pre-hooks, a Vite plugin, a `typecheck` script |
| F6 | high | "The scripted sweep" has no script | accepted: `bun run sweep` on Playwright; 2D sweep in CI (taste) |
| F7 | high | One stage slot, many two-picture models | accepted: `aux` stage |
| F8 | high | One number format contradicts the catalog's own gates | accepted: `fmt` options; integers in full. Confirmed: `fmt` rounds to two decimals today |
| F9 | medium | Flat specs force parallel arrays | accepted: arrays of flat records, one level |
| F10 | medium | Preset ids duplicated in `meta.ts` and `manifest.ts` | accepted: `presets.ts` is the only list; `meta.ts` names the default |
| F11 | medium | `bun test` is a trap; fail-fast; no shared helpers | accepted: guard, summary runner, filter, `testkit.ts` |
| F12 | medium | Eq / WP requirement lives only in the plan's tables | accepted: `planned.ts` carries stage, eq, wp; the registry test enforces them |
| F13 | medium | Vocabulary drift | accepted: glossary; no renames of existing files |
| F14 | high | Incomplete folders are skipped silently; case-insensitive match | accepted: per-folder line; exact-name match |
| F15 | high | No message text for developer-facing failures | accepted: "Messages a builder sees" |
| F16 | medium | `validate` cannot say which control | accepted: `field`; `safeExpression` failure shape |
| F17 | high | No development opt-out for usage logging; probe writes junk | accepted: off in development, sweep and with a local flag; five states; a probe that writes nothing |
| F18 | medium | Silent fallbacks hide broken links | accepted: development console warnings |
| F19 | medium | CI can end up silently absent; two lockfiles | accepted: pinned Bun, one lockfile, a fallback file and TODOS entry. Confirmed: both lockfiles are tracked |
| F20 | high | The plan ships no developer docs; existing docs are stale | accepted: three new docs, three corrected |
| F21 | medium | Kits have no reference | accepted: doc comments and `#/kits` |
| F22 | high | No path when a kit falls short | accepted: overlay slot in every stage, `KIT-REQUEST.md` |
| F23 | medium | `check-models` has no override and no editor feedback | accepted in part: allowlist, `allow-raw` comment, ESLint warning. A custom ESLint plugin is deferred |
| F24 | medium | Builders cannot push back on mappings; one model per concept is baked in | accepted in part: `declined`. The `models: [...]` array is rejected: the three representations are channels, not three models, and the catalog is generated, so the shape can change when a second channel exists |
| F25 | medium | Kelvin-only units | accepted: one temperature helper and a display rule |

```

  3. Error messages actionable?        no       N/A     N/A (one voice)

  6. Dev environment friction-free?    no       N/A     N/A (one voice)
Single-voice critical findings: F1 (no contract), F2 (step 9 unverifiable in a branch).
```

#### Passes

Each pass: the score when the phase began, what a 10 looks like here, and what changed.

1. **Getting started: 3 to 8.** A 10 is one command from a fresh worktree to the
   builder's own model on screen. Was: three hand-written files, an import crash, a
   restart. Now: `bun run setup:worktree`, `bun dev`, the printed address. Not a 10
   because the install still dominates and the first measurement comes in Wave 1.
2. **API and CLI design: 5 to 8.** A 10 is a contract a builder uses correctly after one
   example. Was: a sound folder idea with no shapes. Now: `types.ts`, default exports
   checked with `satisfies`, one preset list, `aux`, arrays of flat records, `fmt`
   options, one command per job (`new:model`, `sweep`, `test <text>`, `typecheck`), route
   options always inside the hash.
3. **Error messages: 4 to 8.** Three paths traced. An unknown concept id: was a bare
   failure, now names the file, the id and the closest id. An incomplete folder: was
   silence and "No model called X", now a line naming the missing file. A failed
   assertion: was "FAIL name", now expected and actual. The student-facing states were
   already specified by the design phase. Tier reached: Elm-style sentences with the fix;
   no error codes.
4. **Documentation: 2 to 8.** A 10 is a guide a cold builder finishes from without
   opening the plan. Was: a dated note and TODOS entries, with README, CLAUDE.md and the
   curriculum README already wrong. Now: `docs/MODELS.md`, `docs/MODEL-REVIEW.md`,
   `docs/MODEL-BRIEF.md`, a `#/kits` page, and the three stale files corrected.
5. **Upgrade path: 5 to 7.** The thing that upgrades is the contract and the kits, under
   73 models. Now: only the integrator changes them; a contract change is a type error in
   every affected model; a breaking kit change lands with its model edits in one PR and a
   line in the "Changes" list. The eight existing models keep working (adapters, optional
   new spec fields, `specTypes.ts` exports kept). The change to `fmt` alters their
   readouts on purpose and is checked by hand in Wave 0.
6. **Developer environment: 4 to 8.** Was: generated files missing for lint, the
   editor and `tsc -b`; no watch; a fixed port for parallel servers; two lockfiles; a
   sweep that could never run in CI. Now: generation on `postinstall` and every
   pre-hook, a Vite plugin, a saved strict port, one lockfile and a pinned Bun, a sweep
   script with an exit code whose 2D pass runs in CI.
7. **Community and ecosystem: 5 to 6.** A private, pre-product repo; an open-source
   community is not a goal. What stands in for it is specified: the brief, the kit
   request, the hand-off report, the review format. Not scored higher, by design.
8. **Measurement: 4 to 7.** The plan already replaces estimates with Wave 1's actuals.
   Added: the time from worktree to stub and each builder's "what slowed me down" line,
   recorded after Wave 1 and acted on before Wave 2. No telemetry and no recurring
   process is added. `/devex-review` can measure the result after Wave 1.

#### What already exists (reused, not rebuilt)

- `.env.example`, and the console line `supabase.ts` already prints when the values are
  missing.
- The standalone-script test style of `physicsModels.test.ts` and `circuitNetwork.test.ts`
  (its `check` and `approx` become `testkit.ts`).
- `eslint.config.js` (the model rule is one `no-restricted-syntax` entry).
- The student-facing stage states and parser messages from the design phase.
- `src/data/curriculum/build.ts` as the correctness gate; it already fails with no files
  written.

#### NOT in scope (DX)

- The WebGL sweep in CI: headless GPU rendering is not dependable there.
- A custom ESLint plugin for the model rules: the script is the gate.
- More than one model per concept in the catalog.
- Moving the other six existing models into their folders.
- Error codes with a documentation link per code: plain sentences with the fix are
  enough for 21 messages.
- Timing instrumentation beyond the Wave 1 record.

is below 6. TTHW is an estimate on both sides until Wave 1 measures it.

```
DX IMPLEMENTATION CHECKLIST
============================
[ ] Fresh worktree to own model on screen in under 5 minutes (measured in Wave 1)
[ ] Preparing a worktree is one command (bun run setup:worktree)
[ ] The first run shows the builder's own model in the real frame
[ ] Magical moment delivered via the scaffold and the printed address
[ ] Every message in "Messages a builder sees" has problem, place and fix, and a test
[ ] Command and file names are guessable; one glossary; route options inside the hash
[ ] Every scaffold flag has a default taken from planned.ts
[ ] docs/MODELS.md skeletons compile as pasted
[ ] projectile and function-grapher are whole, readable reference models
[ ] Contract and kit changes are recorded in the "Changes" list
[ ] A breaking kit change lands with its model edits in one PR
[ ] TypeScript: every model file is checked with satisfies against types.ts
[ ] CI runs test, test:catalog, lint, build and the 2D sweep with a pinned Bun
[ ] n/a: free tier, public changelog, docs search, community channel (private repo)
```

#### Implementation tasks (DX)

Synthesized from this review's findings. All are Wave 0 unless noted.

- [ ] **T1 (P1, human: ~4h / CC: ~20min)**, engine: write `src/engine/models/types.ts` and the four-file contract
  - Surfaced by: F1, F10. Files: `src/engine/models/types.ts`, `scripts/gen-models.mjs`. Verify: `bun run typecheck`.
