- Spec endpoint: `api/spec.ts` answers "not found" unless `SPEC_API_ENABLED` is set and returns generic errors; a rate limit is a condition of enabling it.
- Contract: `ModelCore<S, D>` has `ranges`, `validate` and `derive`; `WidgetProps<S>` is `{ spec, set }`; `equations.ts` exports `ModelEquations<S>` (`set`, `fields`). The frame owns the live spec, runs `validate` on every change and shows a refusal by the control bound to its `field`; controls take ranges from `core.ranges`; a widget keeps only non-model state; a widget renders inside `Frame.Stage`, `Frame.Aux`, `Frame.Controls` and `Frame.Readouts`; the frame renders the equation panel (feeding `useEquationPanel` from the live spec, substituting the solved value, locking that control) and the word-problem bar (applying `patch` with `set`); anything an equation solves for is a spec field; the widget is keyed on type and preset; the first input event is "interacted". The stage and readouts draw `derive`'s numbers. Symbolic-against-numeric comparisons are test-time only.
- Legacy six (`legacy: true`): slot-wrapped in 0c, keeping their own state, `{ spec }` props, their own equation panel and word-problem bar; exempt from `ranges`, `derive` and range sampling. `projectile` and `function-grapher` take the full contract.
- Generator: `scripts/gen.ts` (TypeScript, Bun) writes `meta.gen.ts`, `load.gen.ts` (one loader per model), `pure.gen.ts` (every `meta`, `core`, `presets`, `concepts`; build and tests only) and `catalog.gen.json`, each renamed into place and only when changed. The Vite plugin runs it at dev-server and build start and on model-folder changes, and ignores its own outputs; hooks remain for `postinstall`, `test`, `lint` and `typecheck`. A production deploy runs check mode. Stubs are left out of every production build. `tsconfig.scripts.json` typechecks `scripts/`, the curriculum build and `api/`.
- Catalog: one file for all subjects; character-code sorting; `validateMappings` also fails on a course missing from or unknown to `courseOrder.ts`; a model with no mapped concept is a warning in normal mode and an error in check mode.
- Model rules: ESLint errors for a model's pure files importing React, three or CSS, for a model importing another model, `supabase`, `usage` or `Label3D`, for `fetch`, `localStorage`, `location` or `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` in a model, and for a raw `input`; `check-models` covers literal colours in CSS and TSX; `bun run check:branch <type>` fails a model branch that changed a file outside its folder; `bun run check:bundle` fails when a plot or board model loads three, a model without a typed expression loads mathjs, or the Try page's first load exceeds the 0c figure by more than a tenth.
- Tests: range sampling in `registry.test.ts` (each control at both ends and at seeded points, every preset as base: `validate` passes and every `derive` number is finite or an explicit "undefined here"); the runner fails a file with no assertion, over 30 seconds, or with a rejected promise; a schema-drift test for the legacy branches in `spec.ts` and a check that `api/spec.ts`'s imports stay free of React and CSS; `scripts/gen.test.ts` covers write-on-change.
- Browser specs (`bun run test:e2e`, Playwright, in CI against the built site): the frame at both widths; the six stage states through a fixture model; every deep-link row; topics filters and empty states; the in-place walk, including leaving the sign-in and onboarding pages; a tab-order walk per stage kind; reduced motion; an accessibility scan of each model's default preset (axe).
- Sweep: `#/sweep/<type>` in development builds and builds made with `VITE_SWEEP=1`; one model per page; records mount, which renderer drew, that it drew, and a scan for `NaN`, `undefined`, `Infinity` and `[object Object]`; fails on an unexpected fallback; `--nowebgl` disables WebGL in the browser; CI runs it against `vite build` then `vite preview`; locally it checks the dev server is this worktree's own.
- Routing: a route error boundary; decode failures treated as an unknown type; `#/try?c=<id>` with no type shows the skeleton until the catalog loads; focus moves to the heading and the title is announced after an in-place move; `open` is sent once, after the address settles.
- Frame details: the pinned stage uses the small viewport height with a floor and is unpinned under about 480px of visible height; readouts are announced after a control settles and not while playing; `fmt` keeps three significant figures and never fewer digits than the whole-number part, with fixed-width numerals; the three private copies of `fmt` are deleted; WebGL is probed once, an unmount's context loss is ignored, a real loss waits for one restore.
- Kits: no barrel file; `DragHandle`; each kit built against its named hardest consumers as fixtures on `#/kits` before its wave's fan-out; `ParticleBox` as core, canvas renderer and three renderer in separate files; `safeExpression` with its own mathjs instance, a node allowlist, declared symbols only, non-number results as "undefined here", and three profiles; `function-grapher` rebuilt on it and the old check deleted; a shared quantity reader, a 500-character cap and a hostile-input time test for parsers; KaTeX through one kit component with trust off; concentration stored in moles per litre.
- Usage logging: sent with plain `fetch` and the public key only; `engaged` is a delta sent on hide and on moving to another model; `supabase/model_events.sql` creates the table if missing and drops and recreates its rules (event one of four names, shaped type, preset and concept, seconds 0 to 1800, UUID session, database-set time, insert only); the probe uses a named event rule; the status shows only in development or with the founder's flag; the storage read is guarded; the sign-in provider skips its session calls without configuration; `bun scripts/usage-smoke.ts` is run by the founder signed out and signed in; the report counts only known model types and carries the statement that deletes rows older than 180 days.
- CI: typecheck, test, `test:catalog`, lint, build, `check:bundle`, browser specs and the 2D sweep; read-only permissions, pinned actions, cached browser.
- Worktree setup copies only `VITE_` lines of `.env.local`, derives the port from the worktree's path and checks it is free; the dev server answers an identity request.
- Catalog corrections: `surface-3d` leaves undefined cells as holes; `free-body-diagram` refuses a static coefficient below the kinetic one; `titration` and `equilibrium` solve by bounded bisection to a stated tolerance; `stereochemistry` stores priorities for its curated set and tests them against the atomic-number rule; `conic-sections` tilts by slider; the nine edges in "Stage details" behave as listed.
- Review: the second agent, not the builder, removes `stub` on a pass; `data.ts` sources are addresses that can be opened.
- Messages: the table gains five rows (nothing mapped, range sampling, imports, branch scope, a test with no checks) and a bundle row, 27 in all.
- Replaced from the earlier records: Wave 0 is four PRs, not one, and the total is 15 PRs, not 11; versions for Waves 1 to 6 start at 0.3.8.0, not 0.3.5.0; `WidgetProps` is `{ spec, set }` and the frame owns the spec, not the widget; `ModelCore` gains `ranges` and `derive`; `scripts/gen-models.mjs` is `scripts/gen.ts`; `concepts.gen.ts` is `pure.gen.ts`; three lazy imports per model become one loader; the per-subject catalog files become one `catalog.gen.json`, so the subject-prefix lookup and per-subject failure states go; `#/sweep` no longer mounts every model on one page and CI sweeps the built site; `fmt` no longer shows "integers below 100,000 in full" by default; `planned.ts` uses `subjects`; the builder no longer removes `stub`; the raw-input rule is an ESLint error, not a warning; setup no longer copies the whole `.env.local`; `#/try?c=` with no type no longer shows `projectile` first; "the 21 messages" are 27; Wave 0's effort is about 20 to 26 hours and everything about 115 to 165.
- Deferred, written to TODOS.md: a rate limit on `api/spec.ts`; converting the six legacy widgets to the full contract; sweeping only changed models in CI; a scheduled clean-up of `model_events`.
<!-- /autoplan-accepted:eng -->

### CEO phase re-run (the founder's decisions at the approval gate)

<!-- autoplan-baseline-edits:ceo {"sourceSha256":"0922b338269b4d9f9695407539ac2a695fa7ce114bfc76927e527dc80c708448","replacements":[{"oldText":"## Goal\n\nEvery course that contains a concept a model can faithfully show has in-depth interactive\nmodels for those concepts, each built to the same bar as the existing physics models, and\nevery such concept is reachable from the topics page and walkable in unit order.\n\nOne gate and one report:\n\n- **Gate** (the curriculum build, `bun run gen:curriculum`, fails otherwise): every course\n  has at least one mapped concept or an entry in an explicit exclusion list. Wave 0 seeds\n  that list with one \"pending Wave N\" entry per course not yet covered (table below); each\n  wave deletes the entries it satisfies; after Wave 6 the list may contain only permanent\n  exclusions, each with a reason. The gate also fails on an entry naming a course that\n  does not exist and, in check mode, on an entry for a course that is already mapped (in\n  a model branch that is a warning, because the branch does not edit the shared list).\n- **Report** (printed by the build, never a gate): per-course coverage, and every high\n  3D-fit concept that is still unmapped.\n\nExpected coverage after all waves is roughly 250 to 300 of 734 concepts: the families\nnamed in the catalog below. CI; one lockfile and a pinned Bun; tests that pin today's behaviour of the five untested models (closed forms, and each default preset's readout values as `fmt` formats them, with no rendering) written against the current code; the topics column fix already on this branch; the spec endpoint switched off (below) | none, except the endpoint |\n| 0b | 0.3.5.0 | the model folders, the contract, the generator and Vite plugin, the registry, the catalog and the single mapping source, `validateMappings` and the gate, `planned.ts`; the sweep; the browser specs, including the walk through the Algebra 1 mappings and the rendered readout text; the scaffold with its plot and 3D templates; the worktree setup; the three docs | logging |\n\nThe two reference models, PR by PR:\n\n| PR | `projectile` | `function-grapher` |\n|---|---|---|\n| 0b | its maths, equations, parser and widget move into its folder; the widget still holds its own state; nothing a student sees changes | its drawing code moves into its folder; its expression check is rebuilt on `safeExpression`; the Algebra 1 presets and mappings are added as data |\n| 0c | converted to the frame-owned spec and the slots | its drawing code becomes `PlotStage`, which it then uses; converted to the frame-owned spec and the slots; `built: true` |\n| 0d | covered by the sweep and the browser specs | the browser walk through its Algebra 1 concepts |\n\nThe 0a tests keep the same assertions through 0b; only their import paths and file\nlocations change. The one deliberate difference in 0b is `function-grapher`'s expression\ncheck: every pinned preset must still validate and give the same values, and the PR lists\nany expression the old check accepted that the new one refuses. Nothing a\nstudent can reach calls it. 0a makes it answer \"not found\" unless a server setting\n(`SPEC_API_ENABLED`) is on, and makes its error replies generic. A rate limit is a\ncondition of ever switching it on (see \"Not in scope\").\n\n"},{"oldText":"### Engine shape\n\n- **One folder per model**: `src/engine/models/<type>/`. Four files are required and make\n  the model exist; the others are added as the recipe requires. Every file has one default\n  export, checked with `satisfies` against the contract below.\n\n  | File | Default export | Holds |\n  |---|---|---|\n  | `meta.ts` (required) | `ModelMeta` | pure data: `type`, `label`, `subjects` (one or more; descriptive only, it does not restrict which concepts the model may serve), `category`, `stage`, the optional `board` layout and `aux` stage, `description`, `defaultPreset` |\n  | `core.ts` (required) | `ModelCore<Spec, Derived>` | `ranges`, `validate` and `derive`; pure TypeScript, no React or three |\n  | `presets.ts` (required) | `Preset<Spec>[]` | each preset's `id`, `label` and `spec`; pure TypeScript |\n  | `Widget.tsx` (required) | a component taking `WidgetProps<Spec>` | the stage and its controls, inside the frame's slots; `widget.css` beside it, class names prefixed with the model type |\n  | `concepts.ts` | `ConceptMap` | concept id to preset id, with an optional \"try this\" note of its own when the preset's note does not name that concept; and the concepts the builder declined, each with a reason |\n  | `equations.ts` | `ModelEquations<Spec>` | present exactly when the catalog says Eq: the `EquationSet`, and which spec field each of its base symbols is |\n  | `parse.ts` | a function returning `ParseResult<Spec>` | present exactly when the catalog says WP |\n  | `data.ts` | named tables | reference data, each table with its source |\n  | `<type>.test.ts` | none | the model's assertions |\n\n  Shared classes stay in `src/engine/visualize.css`, which new models do not edit.\n- **The contract** is `src/engine/models/types.ts`, written in 0b. It is the one file a\n  builder reads to know every shape:\n\n  ```ts\n  export interface ModelMeta {\n    type: string                 // equals the folder name\n    label: string\n    subjects: Subject[]\n    category: string\n    stage: 'plot' | '3D' | 'board'\n    board?: 'canvas' | 'table'   // board stages only\n    aux?: 'plot' | 'board'       // optional second stage\n    description: string\n    defaultPreset: string\n    stub?: true                  // set by the scaffold; the reviewer removes it on a pass\n    legacy?: true                // the six adapter models only\n  }\n  export interface Preset<S> { id: string; label: string; spec: S }\n  export interface ValidationResult<S> { valid: boolean; reason?: string; field?: keyof S }\n  export interface Range { min: number; max: number; step: number }\n  export interface ModelCore<S, D> {\n    ranges: { [K in keyof S]?: Range }   // each numeric control's range\n    validate(spec: S): ValidationResult<S>\n    derive(spec: S): D                   // every number the stage or a readout shows\n  }\n  export interface WidgetProps<S> {\n    spec: S                              // the live spec, owned by the frame\n    set(patch: Partial<S>): void         // the only way a widget changes it\n  }\n  export interface ModelEquations<S> { set: EquationSet; fields: Record<string, keyof S> }\n  export interface ConceptMap {\n    mapped: Record<string, { preset: string; note?: string }>\n    declined?: Record<string, string>   // concept id -> why this model does not show it\n  }\n  export interface ParseResult<S> {\n    outcome: 'filled' | 'partial' | 'none'\n    patch: Partial<S>\n    message: string\n  }\n  ```\n\n- **Who owns what** between a widget and `ModelFrame`:\n  - The frame owns the live spec. It starts from the preset and changes only through\n    `set`. On every change the frame runs `core.validate`; a refusal's `field` is shown\n    by the control bound to that field. A control takes its range from `core.ranges`.\n  - A widget keeps only state that is not part of the model: the camera, hover, which\n    item is selected.\n  - A widget returns its content inside the frame's slots, `Frame.Stage`, `Frame.Aux`\n    (when `meta.aux` is set), `Frame.Controls` and `Frame.Readouts`. The slots are plain\n    wrappers that the frame's grid places at both widths; a widget never positions them.\n  - The frame renders the equation panel and the word-problem bar itself. It feeds\n    `useEquationPanel` from the live spec through `equations.ts`'s `fields`, puts the\n    solved value into the spec the widget receives, and locks the control bound to that\n    field. A parser's `patch` is applied with `set`. So anything an equation can solve\n    for is a spec field, including the time of a model with `Transport`.\n  - The widget is keyed on model type and preset, so a hop to another preset of the same\n    model starts clean, and reset restores the preset the same way.\n  - The first input event inside the frame is the \"interacted\" usage event.\n  - The six legacy models (`legacy: true`) are the exception. In 0c their existing markup\n    is wrapped in the same slots, a mechanical edit, with their own equation panel and\n    word-problem bar placed in the frame's regions, so they sit in the same layout. They\n    keep their own state, take `{ spec }` only, and are not required to have `ranges` or\n    `derive`. `projectile` and `function-grapher` are converted to the full contract in\n    0b and 0c, which proves it on the hardest existing widget (equations, a parser,\n    transport, 3D, a locked control) before any new model is built.\n- **Scaffold**: `bun run new:model <type> [--stage plot|3D|board] [--eq] [--wp]\n  [--concepts <id,id,...>]` (`scripts/new-model.ts`) writes the folder with every\n  required file in place: a one-slider spec with its range, a default preset, a\n  `validate` and a `derive`, a widget on the chosen stage built from the kits and the\n  slots, a `concepts.ts` with the given concept ids mapped to the default preset, and a\n  test that passes. It takes the stage, Eq and WP from the `PLANNED` list when the type\n  is there, so those flags are only needed for a model that is not in the catalog. It\n  refuses a type that is not kebab-case or whose folder exists. It marks the model\n  `stub: true`, and ends by printing the address to open (`#/try/<type>`) and the\n  commands a builder runs next. A stub works in development and is left out of every\n  production build's registry and catalog; the check-mode build refuses to pass while\n  any model is still a stub, so a scaffold cannot ship as a model.\n- **Reference models**: two folders are complete, readable examples of the contract, and\n  the scaffold's stubs are cut from them. `function-grapher` is the plot example; its\n  expression check is rebuilt on `safeExpression` and the older, looser check in\n  `validate.ts` is deleted. `projectile` is the 3D example with an equation set and a\n  word-problem parser: its maths, equations, parser and widget move out of the shared\n  files into its folder (its schema branch and prompt text stay in `src/engine/spec.ts`).\n- **Generated files**: `bun run gen:curriculum` runs `scripts/gen.ts` (TypeScript, run\n  with Bun): first the model registry, then the curriculum build. Nothing derived from\n  the model folders is committed, so model branches never conflict on it and it cannot\n  be stale:\n  - `src/engine/models/meta.gen.ts`: imports every `meta.ts` and records which optional\n    files each folder has. Small, loaded eagerly by the Try page, the coverage page and\n    `specTypes.ts`.\n  - `src/engine/models/load.gen.ts`: one lazy loader per model, which brings in that\n    model's `core.ts`, `presets.ts`, `Widget.tsx` and, when present, `equations.ts` and\n    `parse.ts` together, so a model has one loading state.\n  - `src/engine/models/pure.gen.ts`: imports every model's `meta.ts`, `core.ts`,\n    `presets.ts` and `concepts.ts`. Used by the curriculum build and the tests only; the\n    browser never loads it.\n  - `src/data/curriculum/catalog.gen.json`: see \"Catalog and mapping\".\n\n  A folder is registered only when all four required files exist, matched by exact name\n  from the directory listing (so `widget.tsx` fails the same way on macOS as on Linux). A\n  folder holding only a `concepts.ts` is skipped, so a half-built sibling never breaks\n  another model's branch. Every run prints one line per folder: registered, or skipped\n  with the file that is missing. Each output is written to a temporary file and renamed\n  into place, and only when its content has changed, so two runs at once are harmless\n  and a save does not make the dev server reload for nothing.\n- **When the generator runs**: the Vite plugin (`scripts/vite-plugin-models.ts`) runs it\n  when the dev server or a build starts, so starting Vite any way at all is enough, and\n  again when a file is added to or removed from a model folder or a `meta.ts`,\n  `presets.ts` or `concepts.ts` changes. The plugin does not watch the generator's own\n  outputs. A failed run in the dev server shows its message in the terminal and the\n  browser overlay and keeps the last good output. Outside Vite it runs on `postinstall`\n  and before `test`, `lint` and `typecheck` (a new script: the generator, then\n  `tsc -b`). A production deploy runs it in check mode.\n- **`specTypes.ts` is derived**: built entries come from `meta.gen.ts`. Catalog ids that\n  are not built yet come from one static `PLANNED` list\n  (`src/data/curriculum/planned.ts`), written once in 0b from this plan's tables with\n  each model's `type`, `label`, `subjects`, `stage`, `eq`, `wp` and `wave`. What the app\n  shows is built, plus planned and not yet built, so finishing a model needs no edit to\n  the list.\n- **Tests**: `bun run test` runs `scripts/run-tests.ts`, which executes every\n  `src/**/*.test.ts` and `scripts/**/*.test.ts` as a standalone script, prints one line\n  per file and a summary of every failure, and exits non-zero if any failed. A file\n  that makes no assertion, runs past 30 seconds or leaves a rejected promise fails.\n  `bun run test <text>` runs only the files whose path contains the text. Assertions use\n  `src/engine/testkit.ts` (`check(name, condition)` and\n  `near(name, actual, expected, tolerance)`). `bun test`, Bun's own runner, is the wrong\n  command for these files: a `bunfig.toml` preload makes it print \"Use `bun run test`\"\n  and exit. `bun run test:catalog` is the curriculum build in check mode (see \"Catalog\n  and mapping\"); CI and the integrator run it, a model branch does not need to.\n  `scripts/`, the curriculum build and `api/` get their own TypeScript project\n  (`tsconfig.scripts.json`), so `bun run typecheck` covers them; today nothing does.\n- **Existing 8 models get a folder**. `function-grapher` and `projectile` are moved in\n  whole (above). The other six get `meta.ts`, a `presets.ts` (their example specs move\n  here from `VisualizePanel.tsx`), a `core.ts` that re-exports `validate` from\n  `validate.ts`, `concepts.ts`, a `Widget.tsx` that re-exports the current component, and\n  a test file. Their maths, equations, parsers, widgets, schema branches and prompt text\n  stay in the files they are in now, and `physicsModels.test.ts` and\n  `circuitNetwork.test.ts` stay where they are. `projectile`, `wave-oscillator`,\n  `free-body-diagram`, `circular-motion` and `function-grapher` get the closed-form\n  assertions they never had, written in 0a against the code as it is today and moved\n  into their folder's test file in 0b; that file is also where each model's Wave 1\n  extension assertions go. A test keeps each legacy schema branch in `spec.ts` in step\n  with its model's default preset (same keys), and asserts that what `api/spec.ts`\n  imports stays free of React and CSS.\n- **`VisualizePanel`** drops its import list, `MODEL_TYPES`, `validate` switch and\n  `renderModel` switch, and looks models up in the generated registry. Its list of\n  supported models in the \"No interactive visual yet\" message comes from the registry.\n  Its layout and states are the \"Try page frame\" section below.\n- **Kits** live in `src/engine/kits/` and are built by the integrator before the wave that\n  first needs them. Each kit is imported from its own file; there is no single file that\n  re-exports them all, so a model's chunk carries only the kits it uses. 0c builds\n  `ModelFrame` and the `controls` kit (see \"Try page frame\"), `Transport` (play, pause,\n  scrub and speed, extracted from the existing `ps__transport` controls, shared by every\n  time-based model), `DragHandle` (the one way to drag something on a stage: pointer\n  capture, `touch-action` on the handle only, a 44px target, arrow-key nudging and a\n  mirrored number field) and `ExpressionInput` (the only typed-maths field: debounced,\n  keeps the last valid picture on screen, and names the offending token under the\n  field; a preset function with sliders is always the first path and typing the\n  second). `BoardStage` is built in Wave 1 with cell, token,\n  arrow and label primitives, a 12px minimum text size, the same overlay slot as\n  `PlotStage`, and one overflow rule: a table wider than the board scrolls inside it with\n  its first column pinned. `PlotStage` is\n  built in 0c (extracted from `widgets/FunctionGrapher.tsx`; it exposes its x and y\n  scales, a pointer-to-data mapping and an overlay slot for children, so a model can draw\n  arrows, segments, rays and draggable points on it without editing the kit), `Stepper`\n  in Wave 1 (a pure step reducer on top of `Transport`, with a caption line for the\n  current step, a \"step 7 of 23\" counter and replay: play stops at the last step,\n  scrubbing pauses, and under reduced motion there is no autoplay, only step buttons),\n  `ParticleBox` in Wave 3 (a pure seeded simulation core, a flat renderer that draws to\n  a 2D canvas, and a three renderer, each its own file, so a board model never loads\n  three; at most 400 particles; under reduced motion it shows a still snapshot with a\n  step button).\n  Each kit's pure part (plot scales, step reducer, particle core) has its own unit test.\n  Before a wave's fan-out, each kit that wave first uses is built against its hardest\n  consumers, as working fixtures on `#/kits`: `PlotStage` against `kinematics-1d` (linked\n  panels), `vector-field` (an arrow field at equal aspect), `integral-explorer` (regions\n  between curves) and `regression` (draggable points); `BoardStage` against\n  `triangle-solver` (a geometric figure), `stoichiometry` (a table), `graph-explorer` (a\n  node-and-edge drawing) and `reaction-mechanism` (curved arrows); `ParticleBox` against\n  `ideal-gas`, `states-of-matter` and `membrane-transport` (two compartments and a\n  membrane that passes some particles).\n  `safeExpression` also lives here from 0b: the one path by which any model turns\n  typed text into a function. It uses its own mathjs instance, parses without\n  evaluating raw strings, caps input at 200 characters, allows only number, operator,\n  parenthesis and listed-function nodes, refuses any symbol that is not a declared\n  variable or `pi` or `e`, and treats a result that is not a plain finite number as\n  \"undefined here\". It has three profiles: one real variable, two real variables, and\n  boolean (for `logic-gates`). It returns either a function of the named variables or a\n  failure carrying a message, the offending token and its position, which\n  `ExpressionInput` shows under the field. Every model that accepts a typed expression\n  uses it.\n  A shared quantity reader (`quantities.ts`) finds each number with its unit and\n  dimension in a word problem, using `units.ts`; a model's `parse.ts` only says which\n  quantity is which. Input is cut at 500 characters, and a test feeds each parser\n  hostile input under a time limit.\n  All KaTeX goes through one kit component with `trust` off.\n  Unit families a wave needs and `src/engine/units.ts` lacks (pressure, amount,\n  concentration, charge, field, electron-volts and so on) are added by the integrator at\n  the same point. Temperature is stored in kelvin and the unit table stays single-factor;\n  `units.ts` gains one temperature helper (`toKelvin` and `fromKelvin` for degrees Celsius\n  and Fahrenheit) that every parser and the slider row's unit switch use, so no model\n  converts temperature by hand. Readouts show kelvin where the equation needs absolute\n  temperature (gas laws, Arrhenius) and degrees Celsius elsewhere, with the other on the\n  unit switch. Concentration is stored in moles per litre, the second stated exception\n  to SI, because every equilibrium constant, pH and cell potential is defined on it; the\n  gas constant is a named constant in each of its two unit forms.\n- **Using and extending the kits**: every kit's props carry doc comments, and a\n  development-only page, `#/kits`, shows each kit rendered beside the code that produced\n  it. When a kit cannot do what a model needs, the builder draws it in the stage's\n  overlay slot and leaves a `KIT-REQUEST.md` in the model's folder saying what was missing\n  and what they did instead. The integrator answers every request between sub-PRs, by\n  extending the kit or by accepting the local drawing, and deletes the file; the\n  check-mode build fails while one remains.\n- **WebGL**: `hasWebGL` is asked once per page and its test context is released; the\n  landing page's own copy of the check is replaced by it. A\n  context lost because a model was unmounted is ignored. A real loss waits for one\n  restore, and falls back to the 2D view only if that does not come.\n- **Sweep**: `#/sweep/<type>`, present in development builds and in builds made with\n  `VITE_SWEEP=1`, mounts one model's presets in turn inside an error boundary and\n  records, per preset, whether it mounted without error, which renderer drew it (three\n  or the 2D fallback), whether that renderer drew anything (at least one render call,\n  or at least one shape with a non-zero box), and whether the page text contains `NaN`,\n  `undefined`, `Infinity` or `[object Object]`. The result is on the page as a table\n  and on `window.__sweep` for the script.\n  `bun run sweep [type] [--width 375|1280] [--nowebgl]` (`scripts/sweep.ts`, using\n  Playwright, a new development dependency) loads one model per page, so the browser's\n  limit on live 3D contexts is never reached, prints the table, saves a screenshot per\n  preset to `sweep-shots/` (not committed) and exits non-zero on any failure, including\n  a 3D model that drew its fallback when 3D was expected. `--nowebgl` turns WebGL off in\n  the browser itself, so it exercises the app's real detection. With no flags it runs\n  all four combinations of width and WebGL. Locally it uses the dev server, which it\n  starts if needed and checks is this worktree's own; in CI it runs against the built\n  site (`vite build`, then `vite preview`), so a failure that only a production build\n  has is caught. Route options always sit inside the hash, after `?`\n  (`#/sweep/projectile?w=375`); `nowebgl=1` on `#/try/...` lets a builder look at one\n  model's fallback by hand.\n\nTests for this section: `src/engine/models/registry.test.ts` asserts every `meta.ts` type\nis unique and matches its folder name; the default preset exists; every preset passes its\nown `validate`; every preset is the default or mapped to a concept; for every model on\nthe `PLANNED` list, that its stage matches and that `equations.ts` and `parse.ts` are\npresent exactly when the list says Eq and WP; and, for every model that is not legacy,\nrange sampling: with each preset as the base, every control at each end of its range and\nat seeded random points in between must pass `validate`, and every number `derive`\nreturns must be finite or an explicit \"undefined here\". Model rules are checked two ways.\nESLint, as errors and visible in the editor: a model's pure files (`core`, `presets`,\n`meta`, `concepts`, `equations`, `parse`, `data`) import no React, three or CSS; no model\nimports another model's folder, `supabase`, `usage` or `Label3D`; no model uses `fetch`,\n`localStorage`, `location` or `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`; no model contains a raw `input`\nelement. `scripts/check-models.test.ts` fails on a literal colour in a model's CSS or\nTSX (`none`, `transparent`, `currentColor` and `inherit` are allowed; a line is exempted\nby an `allow-raw: <reason>` comment above it, and the check prints how many exemptions\nexist). `bun run check:branch <type>` fails if a model branch changed any file outside\nits own folder. `scripts/gen.test.ts` covers the four-file rule, the exact-name match\nand write-only-on-change. `scripts/new-model.test.ts` scaffolds one model per stage into\na temporary folder and asserts that the generator registers it and that its test\npasses. `bun run check:bundle`, run in CI after the build, reads the build's manifest\nand fails if a plot or board model's chunks include three, if a model without a typed\nexpression includes mathjs, or if the Try page's first load is over the size recorded\nin 0c plus a tenth.\n\n","newText":"### Engine shape\n\n- **One folder per model**: `src/engine/models/<type>/`. Four files are required and make\n  the model exist; the others are added as the recipe requires. Every file has one default\n  export, checked with `satisfies` against the contract below.\n\n  | File | Default export | Holds |\n  |---|---|---|\n  | `meta.ts` (required) | `ModelMeta` | pure data: `type`, `label`, `subjects` (one or more; descriptive only, it does not restrict which concepts the model may serve), `category`, `stage`, the optional `board` layout and `aux` stage, `description`, `defaultPreset` |\n  | `core.ts` (required) | `ModelCore<Spec, Derived>` | `ranges`, `validate` and `derive`; pure TypeScript, no React or three |\n  | `presets.ts` (required) | `Preset<Spec>[]` | each preset's `id`, `label` and `spec`; pure TypeScript |\n  | `Widget.tsx` (required) | a component taking `WidgetProps<Spec>` | the stage and its controls, inside the frame's slots; `widget.css` beside it, class names prefixed with the model type |\n  | `concepts.ts` | `ConceptMap` | concept id to preset id, with an optional \"try this\" note of its own when the preset's note does not name that concept; and the concepts the builder declined, each with a reason |\n  | `equations.ts` | `ModelEquations<Spec>` | present exactly when the catalog says Eq: the `EquationSet`, and which spec field each of its base symbols is |\n  | `parse.ts` | a function returning `ParseResult<Spec>` | present exactly when the catalog says WP |\n  | `data.ts` | named tables | reference data, each table with its source |\n  | `<type>.test.ts` | none | the model's assertions |\n\n  Shared classes stay in `src/engine/visualize.css`, which new models do not edit.\n- **The contract** is `src/engine/models/types.ts`, written in 0b. It is the one file a\n  builder reads to know every shape:\n\n  ```ts\n  export interface ModelMeta {\n    type: string                 // equals the folder name\n    label: string\n    subjects: Subject[]\n    category: string\n    stage: 'plot' | '3D' | 'board'\n    board?: 'canvas' | 'table'   // board stages only\n    aux?: 'plot' | 'board'       // optional second stage\n    description: string\n    defaultPreset: string\n    stub?: true                  // set by the scaffold; the reviewer removes it on a pass\n    legacy?: true                // the six adapter models only\n  }\n  export interface Preset<S> { id: string; label: string; spec: S }\n  export interface ValidationResult<S> { valid: boolean; reason?: string; field?: keyof S }\n  export interface Range { min: number; max: number; step: number }\n  export interface ModelCore<S, D> {\n    ranges: { [K in keyof S]?: Range }   // each numeric control's range\n    validate(spec: S): ValidationResult<S>\n    derive(spec: S): D                   // every number the stage or a readout shows\n  }\n  export interface WidgetProps<S> {\n    spec: S                              // the live spec, owned by the frame\n    set(patch: Partial<S>): void         // the only way a widget changes it\n  }\n  export interface ModelEquations<S> { set: EquationSet; fields: Record<string, keyof S> }\n  export interface ConceptMap {\n    mapped: Record<string, { preset: string; note?: string }>\n    declined?: Record<string, string>   // concept id -> why this model does not show it\n  }\n  export interface ParseResult<S> {\n    outcome: 'filled' | 'partial' | 'none'\n    patch: Partial<S>\n    message: string\n  }\n  ```\n\n- **Who owns what** between a widget and `ModelFrame`:\n  - The frame owns the live spec. It starts from the preset and changes only through\n    `set`. On every change the frame runs `core.validate`; a refusal's `field` is shown\n    by the control bound to that field. A control takes its range from `core.ranges`.\n  - A widget keeps only state that is not part of the model: the camera, hover, which\n    item is selected.\n  - A widget returns its content inside the frame's slots, `Frame.Stage`, `Frame.Aux`\n    (when `meta.aux` is set), `Frame.Controls` and `Frame.Readouts`. The slots are plain\n    wrappers that the frame's grid places at both widths; a widget never positions them.\n  - The frame renders the equation panel and the word-problem bar itself. It feeds\n    `useEquationPanel` from the live spec through `equations.ts`'s `fields`, puts the\n    solved value into the spec the widget receives, and locks the control bound to that\n    field. A parser's `patch` is applied with `set`. So anything an equation can solve\n    for is a spec field, including the time of a model with `Transport`.\n  - The widget is keyed on model type and preset, so a hop to another preset of the same\n    model starts clean, and reset restores the preset the same way.\n  - The first input event inside the frame is the \"interacted\" usage event.\n  - The six legacy models (`legacy: true`) are the exception. In 0c their existing markup\n    is wrapped in the same slots, a mechanical edit, with their own equation panel and\n    word-problem bar placed in the frame's regions, so they sit in the same layout. They\n    keep their own state, take `{ spec }` only, and are not required to have `ranges` or\n    `derive`. `projectile` and `function-grapher` are converted to the full contract in\n    0b and 0c, which proves it on the hardest existing widget (equations, a parser,\n    transport, 3D, a locked control) before any new model is built.\n- **Scaffold**: `bun run new:model <type> [--stage plot|3D|board] [--eq] [--wp]\n  [--concepts <id,id,...>]` (`scripts/new-model.ts`) writes the folder with every\n  required file in place: a one-slider spec with its range, a default preset, a\n  `validate` and a `derive`, a widget on the chosen stage built from the kits and the\n  slots, a `concepts.ts` with the given concept ids mapped to the default preset, and a\n  test that passes. It takes the stage, Eq and WP from the `PLANNED` list when the type\n  is there, so those flags are only needed for a model that is not in the catalog. It\n  refuses a type that is not kebab-case or whose folder exists. It marks the model\n  `stub: true`, and ends by printing the address to open (`#/try/<type>`) and the\n  commands a builder runs next. A stub works in development and is left out of every\n  production build's registry and catalog; the check-mode build refuses to pass while\n  any model is still a stub, so a scaffold cannot ship as a model. The scaffold ships in\n  0d with plot and 3D templates. The board template arrives with `BoardStage` in Wave 1;\n  until then `--stage board` is refused with a line saying so.\n- **Reference models**: two folders are complete, readable examples of the contract, and\n  the scaffold's stubs are cut from them. `function-grapher` is the plot example; its\n  expression check is rebuilt on `safeExpression` and the older, looser check in\n  `validate.ts` is deleted. `projectile` is the 3D example with an equation set and a\n  word-problem parser: its maths, equations, parser and widget move out of the shared\n  files into its folder (its schema branch and prompt text stay in `src/engine/spec.ts`).\n- **Generated files**: `bun run gen:curriculum` runs `scripts/gen.ts` (TypeScript, run\n  with Bun): first the model registry, then the curriculum build. Nothing derived from\n  the model folders is committed, so model branches never conflict on it and it cannot\n  be stale:\n  - `src/engine/models/meta.gen.ts`: imports every `meta.ts` and records which optional\n    files each folder has. Small, loaded eagerly by the Try page, the coverage page and\n    `specTypes.ts`.\n  - `src/engine/models/load.gen.ts`: one lazy loader per model, which brings in that\n    model's `core.ts`, `presets.ts`, `Widget.tsx` and, when present, `equations.ts` and\n    `parse.ts` together, so a model has one loading state.\n  - `src/engine/models/pure.gen.ts`: imports every model's `meta.ts`, `core.ts`,\n    `presets.ts` and `concepts.ts`. Used by the curriculum build and the tests only; the\n    browser never loads it.\n  - `src/data/curriculum/catalog.gen.json`: see \"Catalog and mapping\".\n\n  A folder is registered only when all four required files exist, matched by exact name\n  from the directory listing (so `widget.tsx` fails the same way on macOS as on Linux). A\n  folder holding only a `concepts.ts` is skipped, so a half-built sibling never breaks\n  another model's branch. Every run prints one line per folder: registered, or skipped\n  with the file that is missing. Each output is written to a temporary file and renamed\n  into place, and only when its content has changed, so two runs at once are harmless\n  and a save does not make the dev server reload for nothing.\n- **When the generator runs**: the Vite plugin (`scripts/vite-plugin-models.ts`) runs it\n  when the dev server or a build starts, so starting Vite any way at all is enough, and\n  again when a file is added to or removed from a model folder or a `meta.ts`,\n  `presets.ts` or `concepts.ts` changes. The plugin does not watch the generator's own\n  outputs. A failed run in the dev server shows its message in the terminal and the\n  browser overlay and keeps the last good output. Outside Vite it runs on `postinstall`\n  and before `test`, `lint` and `typecheck` (a new script: the generator, then\n  `tsc -b`). A production deploy runs it in check mode.\n- **`specTypes.ts` is derived**: built entries come from `meta.gen.ts`. Catalog ids that\n  are not built yet come from one static `PLANNED` list\n  (`src/data/curriculum/planned.ts`), written once in 0b from this plan's tables with\n  each model's `type`, `label`, `subjects`, `stage`, `eq`, `wp` and `wave`. What the app\n  shows is built, plus planned and not yet built, so finishing a model needs no edit to\n  the list. Where a wave's table has no Subject column the subject is the wave's own\n  (chemistry, biology or physics); `ideal-gas` and `nuclear-decay` are chemistry and\n  physics. A model's label is its type in words (`ideal-gas` is \"Ideal gas\"). Board\n  layouts are listed in \"Stage details\".\n- **Tests**: `bun run test` runs `scripts/run-tests.ts`, which executes every\n  `src/**/*.test.ts` and `scripts/**/*.test.ts` as a standalone script, prints one line\n  per file and a summary of every failure, and exits non-zero if any failed. A file\n  that makes no assertion, runs past 30 seconds or leaves a rejected promise fails.\n  `bun run test <text>` runs only the files whose path contains the text. Assertions use\n  `src/engine/testkit.ts` (`check(name, condition)` and\n  `near(name, actual, expected, tolerance)`). `bun test`, Bun's own runner, is the wrong\n  command for these files: a `bunfig.toml` preload makes it print \"Use `bun run test`\"\n  and exit. `bun run test:catalog` is the curriculum build in check mode (see \"Catalog\n  and mapping\"); CI and the integrator run it, a model branch does not need to.\n  `scripts/`, the curriculum build and `api/` get their own TypeScript project\n  (`tsconfig.scripts.json`), so `bun run typecheck` covers them; today nothing does.\n- **Existing 8 models get a folder**. `function-grapher` and `projectile` are moved in\n  whole (above). The other six get `meta.ts`, a `presets.ts` (their example specs move\n  here from `VisualizePanel.tsx`), a `core.ts` that re-exports `validate` from\n  `validate.ts`, `concepts.ts`, a `Widget.tsx` that re-exports the current component, and\n  a test file. Their maths, equations, parsers, widgets, schema branches and prompt text\n  stay in the files they are in now, and `physicsModels.test.ts` and\n  `circuitNetwork.test.ts` stay where they are. `projectile`, `wave-oscillator`,\n  `free-body-diagram`, `circular-motion` and `function-grapher` get the closed-form\n  assertions they never had, written in 0a against the code as it is today and moved\n  into their folder's test file in 0b; that file is also where each model's Wave 1\n  extension assertions go. A test keeps each legacy schema branch in `spec.ts` in step\n  with its model's default preset (same keys), and asserts that what `api/spec.ts`\n  imports stays free of React and CSS.\n- **`VisualizePanel`** drops its import list, `MODEL_TYPES`, `validate` switch and\n  `renderModel` switch, and looks models up in the generated registry. Its list of\n  supported models in the \"No interactive visual yet\" message comes from the registry.\n  Its layout and states are the \"Try page frame\" section below.\n- **Kits** live in `src/engine/kits/` and are built by the integrator before the wave that\n  first needs them. Each kit is imported from its own file; there is no single file that\n  re-exports them all, so a model's chunk carries only the kits it uses. 0c builds\n  `ModelFrame` and the `controls` kit (see \"Try page frame\"), `Transport` (play, pause,\n  scrub and speed, extracted from the existing `ps__transport` controls, shared by every\n  time-based model), `DragHandle` (the one way to drag something on a stage: pointer\n  capture, `touch-action` on the handle only, a 44px target, arrow-key nudging and a\n  mirrored number field) and `ExpressionInput` (the only typed-maths field: debounced,\n  keeps the last valid picture on screen, and names the offending token under the\n  field; a preset function with sliders is always the first path and typing the\n  second). `BoardStage` is built in Wave 1 with cell, token,\n  arrow and label primitives, a 12px minimum text size, the same overlay slot as\n  `PlotStage`, and one overflow rule: a table wider than the board scrolls inside it with\n  its first column pinned. `PlotStage` is\n  built in 0c (extracted from `function-grapher`'s widget; it exposes its x and y\n  scales, a pointer-to-data mapping and an overlay slot for children, so a model can draw\n  arrows, segments, rays and draggable points on it without editing the kit), `Stepper`\n  in Wave 1 (a pure step reducer on top of `Transport`, with a caption line for the\n  current step, a \"step 7 of 23\" counter and replay: play stops at the last step,\n  scrubbing pauses, and under reduced motion there is no autoplay, only step buttons),\n  `ParticleBox` in Wave 2 (a pure seeded simulation core, a flat renderer that draws to\n  a 2D canvas, and a three renderer, each its own file, so a board model never loads\n  three; at most 400 particles; under reduced motion it shows a still snapshot with a\n  step button).\n  Each kit's pure part (plot scales, step reducer, particle core) has its own unit test.\n  Before a wave's fan-out, each kit that wave first uses is built against its hardest\n  consumers, as working fixtures on `#/kits`: `PlotStage` against `kinematics-1d` (linked\n  panels), `vector-field` (an arrow field at equal aspect), `integral-explorer` (regions\n  between curves) and `regression` (draggable points); `BoardStage` against\n  `triangle-solver` (a geometric figure), `stoichiometry` (a table), `graph-explorer` (a\n  node-and-edge drawing) and `reaction-mechanism` (curved arrows); `ParticleBox` against\n  `ideal-gas`, `states-of-matter` and `membrane-transport` (two compartments and a\n  membrane that passes some particles).\n  `safeExpression` also lives here from 0b: the one path by which any model turns\n  typed text into a function. It uses its own mathjs instance, parses without\n  evaluating raw strings, caps input at 200 characters, allows only number, operator,\n  parenthesis and listed-function nodes, refuses any symbol that is not a declared\n  variable or `pi` or `e`, and treats a result that is not a plain finite number as\n  \"undefined here\". It has three profiles: one real variable, two real variables, and\n  boolean (for `logic-gates`). It returns either a function of the named variables or a\n  failure carrying a message, the offending token and its position, which\n  `ExpressionInput` shows under the field. Every model that accepts a typed expression\n  uses it.\n  A shared quantity reader (`quantities.ts`) finds each number with its unit and\n  dimension in a word problem, using `units.ts`; a model's `parse.ts` only says which\n  quantity is which. Input is cut at 500 characters, and a test feeds each parser\n  hostile input under a time limit.\n  All KaTeX goes through one kit component with `trust` off.\n  Unit families a wave needs and `src/engine/units.ts` lacks (pressure, amount,\n  concentration, charge, field, electron-volts and so on) are added by the integrator at\n  the same point. Temperature is stored in kelvin and the unit table stays single-factor;\n  `units.ts` gains one temperature helper (`toKelvin` and `fromKelvin` for degrees Celsius\n  and Fahrenheit) that every parser and the slider row's unit switch use, so no model\n  converts temperature by hand. Readouts show kelvin where the equation needs absolute\n  temperature (gas laws, Arrhenius) and degrees Celsius elsewhere, with the other on the\n  unit switch. Concentration is stored in moles per litre, the second stated exception\n  to SI, because every equilibrium constant, pH and cell potential is defined on it; the\n  gas constant is a named constant in each of its two unit forms.\n- **Using and extending the kits**: every kit's props carry doc comments, and a\n  development-only page, `#/kits`, shows each kit rendered beside the code that produced\n  it. When a kit cannot do what a model needs, the builder draws it in the stage's\n  overlay slot and leaves a `KIT-REQUEST.md` in the model's folder saying what was missing\n  and what they did instead. The integrator answers every request between sub-PRs, by\n  extending the kit or by accepting the local drawing, and deletes the file; the\n  check-mode build fails while one remains.\n- **WebGL**: `hasWebGL` is asked once per page and its test context is released; the\n  landing page's own copy of the check is replaced by it. A\n  context lost because a model was unmounted is ignored. A real loss waits for one\n  restore, and falls back to the 2D view only if that does not come.\n- **Sweep**: `#/sweep/<type>`, present in development builds and in builds made with\n  `VITE_SWEEP=1`, mounts one model's presets in turn inside an error boundary and\n  records, per preset, whether it mounted without error, which renderer drew it (three\n  or the 2D fallback), whether that renderer drew anything (at least one render call,\n  or at least one shape with a non-zero box), and whether the page text contains `NaN`,\n  `undefined`, `Infinity` or `[object Object]`. The result is on the page as a table\n  and on `window.__sweep` for the script.\n  `bun run sweep [type] [--width 375|1280] [--nowebgl]` (`scripts/sweep.ts`, using\n  Playwright, a new development dependency) loads one model per page, so the browser's\n  limit on live 3D contexts is never reached, prints the table, saves a screenshot per\n  preset to `sweep-shots/` (not committed) and exits non-zero on any failure, including\n  a 3D model that drew its fallback when 3D was expected. `--nowebgl` turns WebGL off in\n  the browser itself, so it exercises the app's real detection. With no flags it runs\n  all four combinations of width and WebGL. Locally it uses the dev server, which it\n  starts if needed and checks is this worktree's own; in CI it runs against the built\n  site (`vite build`, then `vite preview`), so a failure that only a production build\n  has is caught. Route options always sit inside the hash, after `?`\n  (`#/sweep/projectile?w=375`); `nowebgl=1` on `#/try/...` lets a builder look at one\n  model's fallback by hand.\n\nTests for this section: `src/engine/models/registry.test.ts` asserts every `meta.ts` type\nis unique and matches its folder name; the default preset exists; every preset passes its\nown `validate`; every preset is the default or mapped to a concept; for every model on\nthe `PLANNED` list, that its stage matches and that `equations.ts` and `parse.ts` are\npresent exactly when the list says Eq and WP; and, for every model that is not legacy,\nrange sampling: with each preset as the base, every control at each end of its range and\nat seeded random points in between must pass `validate`, and every number `derive`\nreturns must be finite or an explicit \"undefined here\". Model rules are checked two ways.\nESLint, as errors and visible in the editor: a model's pure files (`core`, `presets`,\n`meta`, `concepts`, `equations`, `parse`, `data`) import no React, three or CSS; no model\nimports another model's folder, `supabase`, `usage` or `Label3D`; no model uses `fetch`,\n`localStorage`, `location` or `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`; no model contains a raw `input`\nelement. `scripts/check-models.test.ts` fails on a literal colour in a model's CSS or\nTSX (`none`, `transparent`, `currentColor` and `inherit` are allowed; a line is exempted\nby an `allow-raw: <reason>` comment above it, and the check prints how many exemptions\nexist). `bun run check:branch <type>` fails if a model branch changed any file outside\nits own folder. `scripts/gen.test.ts` covers the four-file rule, the exact-name match\nand write-only-on-change. `scripts/new-model.test.ts` scaffolds one model for each stage the\nscaffold has a template for into a temporary folder and asserts that the generator\nregisters it and that its test passes. `bun run check:bundle`, run in CI after the build, reads the build's manifest\nand fails if a plot or board model's chunks include three, if a model without a typed\nexpression includes mathjs, or if the Try page's first load is over the size recorded\nin 0c plus a tenth.\n\n"},{"oldText":"### Try page frame\n\nOne frame for every model, built once as `ModelFrame` and extracted from the layout the\nphysics models already share, so old and new models read as one product.\n\n```\n1280px (page does not scroll)                    375px (page scrolls, stage stays put)\n┌──────────────────────────────────────────┐    ┌────────────────────────┐\n│ Saras   AP Physics 1 · Fluids    all topics│    │ Saras       all topics │\n│ Buoyancy and Archimedes' principle        │    │ AP Physics 1 · Fluids  │\n│ Change the density and watch the block... │    │ Buoyancy and Archim... │\n│                     ← prev   2 of 5  next →│    │ ← prev  2 of 5  next → │\n├───────────────────────────┬──────────────┤    ├────────────────────────┤\n│ word problem (when WP yes)                │    │ stage, pinned to the   │\n├───────────────────────────┬──────────────┤    │ top, 45% of the screen │\n│                           │ controls     │    ├────────────────────────┤\n│   stage                   │ readouts     │    │ try-this note          │\n│   (3D, plot or board)     │ equations    │    │ controls               │\n│                           │ (when Eq yes)│    │ readouts               │\n│                           │   reset      │    │ equations, word problem│\n└───────────────────────────┴──────────────┘    └────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n- Header, in reading order: the course and unit as a small label; the concept title as\n  the page's one heading (the model label when no concept is given); the \"try this\" note,\n  at most two lines; previous and next concept links with \"2 of 5\". The Saras mark links\n  home and is no longer the heading. The tab title is \"<concept title> · Saras\".\n- The header has a fixed height at 1280px, so adding the note never makes the page\n  scroll. At 375px the stage is pinned to the top of the screen at 45% of its height and\n  the controls scroll beneath it, so a student always sees the picture they are changing.\n  The 45% is of the small viewport height (the height with the browser's bars showing),\n  with a floor; when the visible height is under about 480px (a phone on its side, or\n  the keyboard up) the stage is not pinned and scrolls with the page.\n- A model with an `aux` stage shows both. At 1280px the stage column is split, the main\n  stage above and the aux stage below it at a third of the column's height. At 375px the\n  pinned area holds one of them, chosen by a two-button switch on the pinned stage\n  (labelled by the model, \"Model\" and \"Graph\" by default); the other sits first in the\n  scrolling column.\n- Previous and next follow catalog order within the unit and skip unmapped concepts,\n  labelled with the concept they lead to. At the last mapped concept of a unit, next\n  becomes \"Next unit: <its first mapped concept>\", or \"Back to <course>\" when there is\n  none.\n- \"All topics\" returns to the topics page with the same subject, search and toggle, and\n  the course the student came from open and in view (carried in the link, see Topics).\n- The `controls` kit is the only source of controls: a slider row (label, value, unit),\n  a choice (segmented buttons, or a list when there are more than five options), a number\n  field with a unit, a readout row, a legend, and a reset button that restores the\n  preset. A model that needs to switch between a few built-in cases (a molecule, a\n  lattice, a mechanism) uses the choice control, never its own picker. There is no\n  model picker and no preset picker: other models and presets are reached through\n  topics and the previous and next links.\n- One number format everywhere, `fmt(value, options)` (the existing `fmt`, extended): by\n  default three significant figures, and never fewer digits than the whole-number part\n  has (3 is \"3.00\", 12345.6 is \"12346\"), so a readout does not change shape as a slider\n  crosses a whole number; scientific notation below 0.001 and from 100,000 up; the unit\n  after a thin space; an en dash for a value that does not exist. Readouts use\n  fixed-width numerals. The three private copies of `fmt` (in `EquationPanel.tsx`,\n  `ProjectileSim.tsx` and `FunctionGrapher.tsx`) are deleted in favour of this one. Options cover what three figures cannot:\n  `sig` for a value whose point is its precision (109.47 degrees, 68.27 percent),\n  `kind: 'int'` for counts and `kind: 'percent'`. A readout row also accepts an exact\n  string the model formats itself (a surd, a ratio such as 9:3:3:1, a binary or\n  hexadecimal number). The eight existing models move from two decimals to this format;\n  the Wave 0 browser pass reads each of their readouts against a hand calculation.\n- On a touch screen a 3D stage does not capture one-finger drags (the page scrolls);\n  a \"Rotate\" button on the stage turns orbiting on and off.\n- A 3D model that falls back to 2D says so in one line under the stage (\"Showing the 2D\n  view\"). If WebGL is lost mid-session the model switches to its fallback.\n- States of the stage:\n\n| State | What the student sees |\n|---|---|\n| loading | a skeleton with the stage's own outline and fixed size (no layout shift; no shimmer under reduced motion) |\n| ready | the model |\n| chunk failed | \"This model didn't load.\" and a \"Reload\" button that reloads the page |\n| refused by the gate | \"Couldn't verify a correct visual for this\" and the reason |\n| crashed | \"This model hit an error.\" with \"Reset to the starting values\" and a link to topics |\n| no such model | \"No model called X\" (X shortened to 40 characters) and a link to topics |\n\n- A model's readouts are announced after the student stops changing a control, not on\n  every step of a drag, and not at all while a model is playing.\n- A throw anywhere in a page (the address resolver, the header, the topics page) shows\n  a one-line notice with a link home, from an error boundary at the route, instead of a\n  blank page. After an in-place move to another concept, focus goes to the heading and\n  the new title is announced.\n- In a development build every silent fallback also writes one console warning naming\n  what was asked for and what was shown (an unknown preset, an unknown or unmapped `c`, a\n  crashed model with its type, preset and spec), so a builder's broken link does not look\n  like a working one.\n- Navigation between pages no longer reloads the whole site: `Root` re-renders on a hash\n  change, and the Try page re-resolves when its hash changes, so stepping to the next\n  concept keeps the header in place and swaps only the stage.\n- A footer line on the Try and topics pages: \"We count which models get opened. No names,\n  no accounts.\"\n- The Try page's body text uses the site's body font variable; the leftover warm tint on\n  the old topic pill and the system-font fallback in `.viz` are removed with the rebuild.\n\nTests: `fmt` cases (three figures, whole-number digits kept, the `sig`, `int` and\n`percent` options, both scientific thresholds, non-finite); the previous, next, position\nand unit-end lookups. Browser specs (`bun run test:e2e`, Playwright, run in CI against\nthe built site): the frame at 375px (stage stays visible while the last control is in\nview) and 1280px (no page scroll); each of the six stage states, using a fixture model\nthat exists only in development and sweep builds and can refuse, crash and fail to\nload; every row of the deep-link table; the topics filters and empty states; the walk\nlanding, topics, model, next, \"all topics\", with no full page load and no canvas left\nbehind; a hash change away from the sign-in and onboarding pages, which used to rely on\nthe reload; a tab-order walk of one model per stage kind; reduced motion (two screenshots\nhalf a second apart are identical); and an accessibility scan of each model's default\npreset (axe, a second new development dependency).\n\n","newText":"### Try page frame\n\nOne frame for every model, built once as `ModelFrame` and extracted from the layout the\nphysics models already share, so old and new models read as one product.\n\n```\n1280px (page does not scroll)                    375px (page scrolls, stage stays put)\n┌──────────────────────────────────────────┐    ┌────────────────────────┐\n│ Saras   AP Physics 1 · Fluids    all topics│    │ Saras       all topics │\n│ Buoyancy and Archimedes' principle        │    │ AP Physics 1 · Fluids  │\n│ Change the density and watch the block... │    │ Buoyancy and Archim... │\n│                     ← prev   2 of 5  next →│    │ ← prev  2 of 5  next → │\n├───────────────────────────┬──────────────┤    ├────────────────────────┤\n│ word problem (when WP yes)                │    │ stage, pinned to the   │\n├───────────────────────────┬──────────────┤    │ top, 45% of the screen │\n│                           │ controls     │    ├────────────────────────┤\n│   stage                   │ readouts     │    │ try-this note          │\n│   (3D, plot or board)     │ equations    │    │ controls               │\n│                           │ (when Eq yes)│    │ readouts               │\n│                           │   reset      │    │ equations, word problem│\n└───────────────────────────┴──────────────┘    └────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n- Header, in reading order: the course and unit as a small label; At 375px the stage is pinned to the top of the screen at 45% of its height and\n  the controls scroll beneath it, so a student always sees the picture they are changing.\n  The 45% is of the small viewport height (the height with the browser's bars showing),\n  with a floor; when the visible height is under about 480px (a phone on its side, or\n  the keyboard up) the stage is not pinned and scrolls with the page.\n- A model with an `aux` stage shows both. At 375px the\n  pinned area holds one of them, chosen by a two-button switch on the pinned stage\n  (labelled by the model, \"Model\" and \"Graph\" by default); the other sits first in the\n  scrolling column.\n- Previous and next follow catalog order within the unit and skip unmapped concepts,\n  labelled with the concept they lead to. At the last mapped concept of a unit, next\n  becomes \"Next unit: <its first mapped concept>\", or \"Back to <course>\" when there is\n  none.\n- \"All topics\" returns to the topics page with the same subject, search and toggle, and\n  the course the student came from open and in view (carried in the link, see Topics).\n- The `controls` kit is the only source of controls: a slider row (label, value, unit),\n  a choice (segmented buttons, or a list when there are more than five options), a number\n  field with a unit, a readout row, a legend, and a reset button that restores the\n  preset. A model that needs to switch between a few built-in cases (a molecule, a\n  lattice, a mechanism) uses the choice control, never its own picker. There is no\n  model picker and no preset picker: other models and presets are reached through\n  topics and the previous and next links.\n- One number format everywhere, `fmt(value, options)` (the existing `fmt`, extended): by\n  default three significant figures, and never fewer digits than the whole-number part\n  has (3 is \"3.00\", 12345.6 is \"12346\"), so a readout does not change shape as a slider\n  crosses a whole number; scientific notation below 0.001 and from 100,000 up; the unit\n  after a thin space; an en dash for a value that does not exist. Readouts use\n  fixed-width numerals. The three private copies of `fmt` (in `EquationPanel.tsx`,\n  `ProjectileSim.tsx` and `FunctionGrapher.tsx`) are deleted in favour of this one. Options cover what three figures cannot:\n  `sig` for a value whose point is its precision (109.47 degrees, 68.27 percent),\n  `kind: 'int'` for counts and `kind: 'percent'`.
